# Environment flags

This catalog lists every environment variable and build switch memra reads, for operators
setting up a deployment and for contributors who add, change or remove a flag.

A naked run needs none of them. Winners are defaults, and a flag exists only as a runtime
parameter, machine-specific config, rollback seam, diagnostic or blocked experimental door.
AGENTS.md, ["Flags doctrine and door hygiene"](../AGENTS.md#flags-doctrine-and-door-hygiene), says
when a flag may exist, when a door is decided and how a default changes.

Each row has four cells:

- **Flag**: a name, several related names, or a family prefix.
- **Default**: what a run gets with the flag unset, with a device or arch key where it differs by
  card and a `decide-by: YYYY-MM-DD` date on a default-off door.
- **What it does**: each value, when it engages, what it refuses, and a seam's rollback value.
- **Record**: the decision, research or guide behind the row, and its section in
  [FLAGS-HISTORY.md](FLAGS-HISTORY.md), which keeps the row's text from before the rewrite.

A new read lands with its row in the same commit; a backticked `NAME*` prefix row covers a family.
The engine's boot audit checks every `MEMRA_*` name in the environment against
`crates/memra-engine/env-registry.txt`, generated from this file, so an edit that adds, retires or
removes a name also runs `python3 tools/gen-env-registry.py` and commits its output;
`tools/check-flags.sh` refuses a stale registry.

The notes that stood between the old tables are in
[FLAGS-HISTORY.md](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#catalog-notes-before-the-2026-11-12-rewrite), and decisions are
in [decisions/](decisions/). [Removed doors](#removed-doors), at the end, lists every door that is
gone; the boot audit refuses a removed name and prints the heading it sits under.

Sections: [server](#server-and-api), [scheduling](#scheduling-and-admission),
[KV cache](#kv-cache-prefix-cache-and-memory-tiers), [speculative decoding](#speculative-decoding),
[sampling](#sampling-and-constrained-output), [loading](#loading-memory-budgets-and-spill),
[multi-GPU](#multi-gpu), [build](#build-time-switches), [tools](#command-line-tools),
[kernels](#kernel-arms-and-rollback-seams), [model families](#model-family-doors),
[hardware](#hardware-keyed-knobs), [diagnostics](#diagnostics-and-tracing),
[gates](#gate-bench-and-ci-inputs), [removed doors](#removed-doors).

## Server and API

Settings for `memra-server`: listen address and keys, the model list, context, deadlines and drain, health and GPU watch, request logging and metrics, vision, audio and background jobs. A deployment usually sets `MEMRA_ADDR`, `MEMRA_API_KEY` or `MEMRA_API_KEYS`, and `MEMRA_MODELS`; a non-loopback address without a key refuses to start unless `MEMRA_ALLOW_OPEN_BIND=1`. Rows whose default reads "the stock server refuses to start" belong to a binary that embeds `memra-server` and claims them through `ServerWiring`; the operator guide is [SERVING.md](SERVING.md).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_ADDR` | `127.0.0.1:8080` | Listen address. A non-loopback address with neither `MEMRA_API_KEY` nor `MEMRA_API_KEYS` is fatal at startup, before model or GPU init, unless `MEMRA_ALLOW_OPEN_BIND=1`. |  |
| `MEMRA_ADMIN_ADDR` | unset; set, the stock server refuses to start | Not read by the stock binary: the provisioning listener is a deployment-binary surface. Wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the variable (`ServerWiring::claiming`); otherwise the stock server refuses to start when it is set, so set-but-unread configuration never fails open. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admin_addr) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIN_TOKEN_FILE` | unset; set, the stock server refuses to start | Not read by the stock binary: the provisioning bearer is a deployment-binary surface. Wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the variable (`ServerWiring::claiming`); otherwise the stock server refuses to start when it is set, so set-but-unread configuration never fails open. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admin_token_file) |
| `MEMRA_ALLOW_OPEN_BIND` | unset (`1` arms) | `1` permits a no-key non-loopback development bind. Completion routes are then open, while `/metrics` and `/yield/metrics` still require `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN`; the boot prints a warning. Not a substitute for API keys. |  |
| `MEMRA_API_KEY` | unset (no key: open on loopback) | Single static bearer key; authenticates as tenant `default` and makes `MEMRA_COMPAT` default to `openai`. Without `MEMRA_API_KEYS` or `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN` it also authorizes both metrics views. Composes with `MEMRA_API_KEYS`: with both live, the keyring's tenant-only metrics policy applies to every completion credential. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_api_key) |
| `MEMRA_API_KEYS` | unset | Multi-key tenant keyring: a `keys.toml` path (SHA-256 hex, tenant, lane class, optional `rate_limit`; hot-reloads on mtime, 2 s poll, atomic revoke) or an inline `tenant:sha256hex[:lane]` list. A keyring file must be one private regular non-symlink file (0600/0640), owned by the service uid, one hard link, at most 8 MiB, at boot and on every reload. Tenants get their own cache namespace (`t:<tenant>`), batch keys default to harvest and get 403 on `x-lane: interactive`, and without `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN` a key sees only its tenant's `/metrics` rows. Manage with `memra-server --gen-key` / `--revoke-key`. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#api-keys--multi-key-tenant-auth-laneapi-keys-2026-08-05), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_api_keys) |
| `MEMRA_AUDIO_DRIVE` | `fused` | `fused`: one batched step per tick consumes one frame from every ready lane. `per_lane_worker`: one worker at batch 1 walking lanes round-robin, the drive shape of the c4 collapse; it exists so the G8 red arm can re-run that failure. An unparseable value resolves to `fused`, never to the red arm. A gate-set red arm kept under the door-hygiene rule, no decide-by. Rollback: unset. Gate: `cargo test -p memra-lanes --lib audio_stream` (`red_arm_per_lane_worker_sheds_at_c4`, `red_arm_is_invisible_to_a_short_probe`, `fused_drive_holds_64_streams`, `drive_shape_alone_decides_c4`). | [SPEECH.md](SPEECH.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_audio_drive) |
| `MEMRA_AUDIO_FINAL_SLO_MS` | `800` | The SLO inside `streams@SLO`: p95 end-of-speech to final, in ms. `GET /v1/audio/sessions` reports it and the G8 battery enforces it; the engine does not. 800 is the speech program's finalization bound (`docs/SPEECH.md`), not a published claim. Positive integer; `0` or garbage keeps 800. Rollback: unset. | [SPEECH.md](SPEECH.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_audio_final_slo_ms) |
| `MEMRA_AUDIO_MAX_STREAMS` | `64` | Resident concurrent streaming sessions. 64 is the count the fused arm was measured holding; it moves only when a G8 battery on a named card moves it. Past the cap a new session gets a typed `sessions_exceeded` refusal (HTTP 503 with `Retry-After`), never a dropped stream. Positive integer; `0` or garbage keeps 64. Rollback: unset. | [SPEECH.md](SPEECH.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_audio_max_streams) |
| `MEMRA_AUDIO_QUEUE_FRAMES` | `64` | Per-session bounded frame queue, in 80 ms frames, kept at the pinned c4 harness cap so the red arm reproduces the incident's geometry. Overflow is a typed `queue_overflow` (HTTP 429 with `Retry-After`) carrying the cap and the observed depth, never a silently dropped stream. Positive integer; `0` or garbage keeps 64. Rollback: unset. | [SPEECH.md](SPEECH.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_audio_queue_frames) |
| `MEMRA_BACKGROUND_JOB_MAX_BYTES` | 67108864 (64 MiB total) | Resident-byte cap across every job in `job_store::InMemoryJobStore`, terminal or not; a `put` that would exceed it is refused with `JobStoreError::CapacityExceeded`, never admitted and grown. `0`, unset, empty or unparsable falls back to 67108864. A conservative placeholder, not a measured number. Gate: `cargo test -p memra-server job_store::tests::byte_cap_refuses_a_record_that_would_exceed_it`. | [COMPLETE-RESULT-PATH-V1.md](decisions/COMPLETE-RESULT-PATH-V1.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_background_job_max_bytes) |
| `MEMRA_BACKGROUND_JOB_TTL_SECS` | 900 (seconds after a job goes terminal) | How long `job_store::InMemoryJobStore` keeps a finished job's buffered output before the lazy sweep evicts it. Read once per store construction; `0`, unset, empty or unparsable falls back to 900. Queued and in-progress jobs are never TTL-evicted. A starting point, not a measured number. Gate: `cargo test -p memra-server job_store::tests::ttl_eviction_removes_finished_jobs_only`. | [COMPLETE-RESULT-PATH-V1.md](decisions/COMPLETE-RESULT-PATH-V1.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_background_job_ttl_secs) |
| `MEMRA_CAPTURE_DIR` | unset; set, the stock server refuses to start | Not read by the stock binary: per-tenant capture is a deployment-binary surface. Wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the variable (`ServerWiring::claiming`); otherwise the stock server refuses to start when it is set, so set-but-unread configuration never fails open. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_capture_dir) |
| `MEMRA_COMPAT` | native; `openai` when `MEMRA_API_KEY` is set | Response shape: `openai` gives the OpenAI completions body and SSE chunks; any other set value keeps the native memra shape that validation harnesses read. Read once. |  |
| `MEMRA_CTX` | unset: the checkpoint's declared context (`max_position_embeddings`) | Served context default: the session window for a request that omits both `max_ctx` and a finite `max_tokens`; also the prefix-cache budget's context term and the DSv4 and DeepSeek-V4.1 routes' windows (`dsv41_serve::load`). An explicit value wins; empty, non-integer, negative, `0` or non-Unicode refuses. Unset, a checkpoint that declares nothing refuses as undeclared, and an unusable declaration or one above `ENGINE_MAX_CTX` (`u32::MAX`, a representation bound) refuses instead of narrowing. Resolver: `worker::resolve_ctx`. Pin it to the unbounded-request workload where sessions would not fit VRAM. | [RED-ARM.md](../research/ctx-resolve-20260910/RED-ARM.md), [DAY27.md](../research/spill-b-20260919/DAY27.md), [batched-tick-inc3-20260801](../research/batched-tick-inc3-20260801/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_ctx) |
| `MEMRA_DECODE_FLOOR_TOK_S` | 60 | Pessimistic decode rate (tok/s) for the same deadline estimate. With the compile-time `DEADLINE_INFEASIBLE_MARGIN_PCT` (150) it places the refusal boundary between the measured 6144-token pass and 8192-token fail at a 30k-token prompt. `0` or a non-number keeps 60. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_decode_floor_tok_s) |
| `MEMRA_DRAIN_S` | 30 | Graceful-drain deadline in seconds. On SIGTERM new completion requests get 503 (`code: draining`, `Retry-After` = this value clamped to 60 s, plus `retry-after-ms`), in-flight requests and streams finish within it, then the process exits 0. During drain `/health` stays 200 (`status:"draining"`) and `/readyz` answers 503. Keep it below the unit's `TimeoutStopSec` (twice it without `Type=notify`); under `Type=notify` the server extends the stop timeout to two deadlines plus 5 s, so a DSv4 lane mid-call gets one more deadline. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_drain_s) |
| `MEMRA_ENV_AUDIT` | refuse | Boot audit of every `MEMRA_*` name in the environment (`memra_engine::env_audit`, registry generated from this file): a name in a Removed ledger with no live row refuses the boot naming that ledger; an unknown name inside an owned family (a `MEMRA_<FAMILY>_` prefix the code reads under at least 3 names) refuses; any other unknown name prints one warning. `warn` downgrades refusals to warnings for one boot; `0` or `off` turns the audit off, announced. | [env-audit-20260921](../research/env-audit-20260921/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_env_audit) |
| `MEMRA_FETCH_URLS` | off (operator egress switch) | `MEMRA_FETCH_URLS=1` lets chat completions and Responses input images fetch bounded remote `http(s)` URLs; unset or `0` keeps the data-URI-only path. An operator egress permission, not a tuning door. Every resolved address is filtered, literal IPs need an exact allowlist entry (redirects included), proxies stay off, at most 5 redirect hops and no scheme change. Inline and remote parts share the eight-image cap; each image keeps its 12 MiB raw cap inside the 192 MiB body budget. Limits: 5 s connect, 10 s per image, 20 s per pass, never past the caller's deadline. Failures are named 400s. | [VISION-LANE.md](decisions/VISION-LANE.md), [RESULTS.md](../research/image-url-fetch-20261002/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fetch_urls) |
| `MEMRA_FETCH_URLS_ALLOWED_HOSTS` | unset; read only with `MEMRA_FETCH_URLS=1` | Comma-separated exact hostnames or literal IPs (case-insensitive) exempt from the address-range refusal. Bypasses the address check only: scheme and redirect-hop limits still apply, and an entry covers only that host or IP. | [VISION-LANE.md](decisions/VISION-LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fetch_urls_allowed_hosts) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_VISION` + `MEMRA_GEMMA_MMPROJ` | off | Gemma-4 image serving needs both: `MEMRA_GEMMA_VISION=1` arms the seam and `MEMRA_GEMMA_MMPROJ=<gemma4v mmproj path>` names the tower; unloadable is a boot panic. A deployment serves one vision family, so this process-wide switch also picks the placeholder and image prep. Image parts also pass the boot-time placement decision (`MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`): an inadmissible placement refuses each image part with a named 400, never mid-prefill, and text rendering never moves with placement. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gemma_mmproj) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION` (+ optional `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION_DIR`) | on (auto-detect) | GLM-5.3-Flash (glm5_next) image serving. A directory checkpoint whose own safetensors index carries `model.visual.*` (probe `model.visual.patch_embed.proj.weight`) loads its tower from itself at boot; without those tensors it is text-only. `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION=0` is the rollback seam (no tower, `image_url` refuses by name); `MEMRA_GLM5_VISION_DIR=<dir or shard>` overrides the tower source. Detected or configured but unloadable is a boot panic. HTTP intake keys on the worker's tower decision (`GLM5_VISION_SERVING`); `video_url` refuses. The boot line prints the measured device delta. | [glm5-vision-20260830](../research/glm5-vision-20260830/), [glm53-vision-ppn-20260901](../research/glm53-vision-ppn-20260901/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_vision) |
| `MEMRA_GPU_PROBE_MISSES` | 3 (minimum 1) | Consecutive steady-state `nvidia-smi` probe hangs before the fatal GPU fault latches; below it the process is degraded and live, and an answer clears it. `1` restores the single-hang latch. At the 60 s interval and 10 s deadline, 3 is about three and a half minutes of a silent driver. Fatal Xid lines, uncorrected ECC and row-remap failures latch on first sight regardless. Machine-specific config, not a door. | [gpu-probe-recovery-20260922](../research/gpu-probe-recovery-20260922/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gpu_probe_misses) |
| `MEMRA_GPU_PROBE_TIMEOUT_S` | 10 | Hard deadline for one `nvidia-smi` probe, the alarm for the Blackwell GSP hang class (Xid 119/120), which emits nothing and hangs the query tools. One steady-state probe past it is a miss: `/health` stays 200 and publishes `worker.gpu_probe.{degraded,miss_streak,last_ok_age_ms,degraded_reason}`; the fault latches at `MEMRA_GPU_PROBE_MISSES`, and an answer clears degradation, never a latched fault. Startup allows six consecutive hangs before latching. The probe is a killed-on-deadline child on its own thread, so a hang never blocks a health answer. Minimum 1. | [gpu-probe-recovery-20260922](../research/gpu-probe-recovery-20260922/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gpu_probe_timeout_s) |
| `MEMRA_GPU_WATCH` | on | `0` disables the GPU fault watcher: the Xid tail (`/dev/kmsg`, falling back to `journalctl -k -f`; fatal classes 48/64/79/94/95/119/120 latch unhealthy, others count as `/health` warnings) and the periodic `nvidia-smi` ECC and row-remap probe. Diagnostics door; leave it on. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gpu_watch) |
| `MEMRA_GPU_WATCH_S` | 60 | GPU probe interval in seconds (minimum 1). |  |
| `MEMRA_HEALTH_PROGRESS` | 1 (on) | Feed the engine's forward-progress odometer into the busy-stall verdict. `0` is the rollback seam: pure beat-age semantics. On can only make the verdict less eager to restart (it takes the fresher of two liveness signals), and a hung worker advances neither, so hang detection stays at `MEMRA_HEALTH_STALL_S`. Off is the arm with the measured defect: it declares a busy, progressing worker unhealthy. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_health_progress) |
| `MEMRA_HEALTH_STALL_S` | 120 | How long a busy worker may go without forward progress before `/health` (`/livez`) reports 503. Progress is the fresher of the scheduler heartbeat and the engine's prime odometer (per completed prime chunk); an idle worker is healthy at any beat age. 120 s is about 2.2x the largest concurrent prefill pass at the defaults (`MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` 1024 x `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS` 64). Keep it below the supervisor's `WatchdogSec` and raise both together. Not detected: a hang inside one chunk (full threshold), a livelock that completes chunks, per-session starvation. Under `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK=0` size it by hand. Minimum 1. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_health_stall_s) |
| `MEMRA_METRICS_TOKEN` | unset | Exclusive bearer for `/metrics` and `/yield/metrics`; when set, completion keys get 403 there and the token sees all tenant rows. It does not authorize completions. A `MEMRA_API_KEYS` deployment without it serves tenant-only `/metrics` and withholds `/yield/metrics`. Metrics need a bearer whenever a key source or this token is set or `MEMRA_ADDR` is non-loopback. Empty or non-UTF-8 is fatal at boot. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_metrics_token) |
| `MEMRA_MODEL_METADATA` | unset | TOML metadata for the catalog feeds (`/v1/models`, `?schema=openrouter`, `?schema=openmodels`): `[models."<alias>"]` declares description, quantization, limits and nested `.pricing`/`.capacity` tables; `[planned_models]` is validated, never served. `max_output_length`/`max_prompt_length` bound requests. Optional `default_*` sampling keys and `default_reasoning_effort` fill only fields the client omitted (`default_temperature = 0` refuses); `.non_thinking_sampling` is the thinking-off arm; `surface` (`chat`, `embedding` or `rerank`) sets the advertised type. Unknown keys refuse, so ship the binary before such a config. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#gateway-listing-surface), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_model_metadata) |
| `MEMRA_MODELS` | unset: the BASE-4 test pair (`main` 27B, `judge` 9B) | Model list `alias=path[+draft.gguf],...`; `+draft` attaches that model's regime draft. A path may be a GGUF, an `hf:` spec, an HF safetensors checkpoint dir (`config.json` plus `model.safetensors[.index.json]`) or a memra repack dir (`manifest.json`), validated at startup; a template-less dir checkpoint answers 400 on chat. A malformed entry (no `=`) or an empty list refuses to start. Each model installs its own `NumericProfile`, so one roster may mix families and shapes in any load order. | [DRAFT-REGIME.md](DRAFT-REGIME.md), [serve-st-20260803](../research/serve-st-20260803/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_models) |
| `MEMRA_NONSTREAM_DEADLINE_GATE` | on (`0`, `off` or `false` turns it off) | Refuses at admission a non-streaming request whose pessimistic estimate cannot finish inside its `timeout_ms` deadline: 400 `nonstream_deadline_infeasible`, naming the `max_tokens` that would fit and the streaming alternative. Costs no slot or GPU. Streaming and ctx-bounded requests (no `max_tokens`) are never gated. With the gate off, `/v1/completions` and `/v1/chat/completions` fall back to partial delivery, but `/v1/messages` and `/v1/responses` answer 408 and discard the work. Boundary pinned by `the_feasibility_gate_boundary_matches_the_measured_ladder`. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_nonstream_deadline_gate) |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_FLOOR_TOK_S` | 2000 | Pessimistic prefill rate (tok/s) for the non-streaming deadline estimate and the first-token deadline gate's prime-backlog estimate; never billing or admission accounting. Deliberately below measured prefill, because a false refusal is worse than a slow success; it matches the api-router's header-budget floor. `0` or a non-number keeps 2000. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prefill_floor_tok_s) |
| `MEMRA_REQUEST_LEDGER` | unset; set, the stock server refuses to start | Not read by the stock binary: the request and cost ledger is a deployment-binary surface. Wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the variable (`ServerWiring::claiming`); otherwise the stock server refuses to start when it is set, so set-but-unread configuration never fails open. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_request_ledger) |
| `MEMRA_REQUEST_LOG` | unset (no log; the middleware is not installed) | Config, not a door (it names a file; no decide-by). One JSON line per request on the four completion routes, streaming or not, refusals included: arrival, first-token and end times, end reason, status, token counts, tool-call names with an argument sha256, a session-key sha256 and a prompt-token sha256. No prompt or reply text; digests are unsalted, so the log is pseudonymous. Opened for append at boot (0640); an unopenable path is fatal. A writer thread drains a bounded queue (4096 lines, 64 MiB); overflow drops and counts, never blocks. Output bytes are identical set or unset. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#per-request-timing-log-memra_request_log-2026-11-09), [request-log-20261109](../research/request-log-20261109/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_request_log) |
| `MEMRA_RL_RESET_S` | 2 | Static `X-RateLimit-Reset` in seconds when every slot is taken and the live meter has no signal yet. With signal, Reset is mean tokens per request times p50 step latency, clamped to 1..600 s. |  |
| `MEMRA_SSE_PREFILL_COMMIT_MS` | unset or `0`: off; unified memory with the derived streaming deadline: 60,000 | For a streaming deadline above 90 s, bounds the pre-header hold from request start: with no first token by this threshold, commit HTTP 200 and send the 5 s SSE comment keepalive, whether queued or prefilling. A later refusal is a dialect-native error event under 200; comments are not generated tokens; the original TTFT deadline keeps running and expiry ends the stream with the dialect's timeout event. Requests at or below 90 s keep the pre-header 408; non-streaming never uses it. Keep threshold plus heartbeat below every upstream header timeout. An explicit `0` on unified memory restores the shipped 90 s window. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#unified-memory-devices-gb10-dgx-spark), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_sse_prefill_commit_ms) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_VISION` | on when `MEMRA_STEP_VISION_DIR` is set | `0` skips the step tower even with the dir configured, as `MEMRA_VISION` does for Qwen; image requests then 400. The same 400 fires when the placement is inadmissible (see `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_vision) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_VISION_DEVICE` | unset (the root engine) | Step-3.7-Flash tower placement: the step TP rank whose device loads, runs and publishes the perception tower (3.96 GB BF16 upcast to 7.9 GB f32). Moving it off rank 0 frees that VRAM for long prompts on the device holding every non-TP resident. Overlay rows reach the intake context through `EmbedOverlay::new_published` (`MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`); boot lines `[step-vision] tower placed on step TP rank N` and `overlay intake: ... cross_context=true`. A rank the TP runtime lacks, no step TP runtime, or a non-integer value is a boot panic. Rollback: unset. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_vision_device) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_VISION_DIR` | unset (text-only) | Step-3.7-Flash image serving: point it at the serving artifact's own directory, where the perception tower and `vit_large_projector` live unquantized (BF16) inside the NVFP4 checkpoint. Set: the worker loads the tower at spawn (about 8 GB f32-resident) and the HTTP layer accepts `image_url` parts with the step placeholder expansion and separator law. Unset: image requests 400 and text is byte-identical. Unloadable is a boot panic. Image parts also pass the boot-time placement decision (`MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`). | [step37-vision-20260830](../research/step37-vision-20260830/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_vision_dir) |
| `MEMRA_STREAM_TTFT_MS_MAX` | unset: `MEMRA_TIMEOUT_MS_MAX` (90,000 ms); unified memory (GB10): derived | Streaming-only maximum and default for `timeout_ms`. A value above 90,000 needs `MEMRA_SSE_PREFILL_COMMIT_MS` positive and below it, or the boot fails: a longer first-token promise without SSE keepalives dies at a fronting proxy. Unified memory, with this and `MEMRA_TIMEOUT_MS_MAX` unset and the commit flag not `0`: one full served context prefilled at 4118 tok/s x SMs / 188 / 4, at least 90,000 ms, paired with a 60,000 ms commit (997,311 ms on GB10 at 262,144 tokens). Discrete cards never take that arm. Rollback: unset this and the commit flag; on GB10, `MEMRA_SSE_PREFILL_COMMIT_MS=0`. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#unified-memory-devices-gb10-dgx-spark), [gb10-defaults-20261101](../research/gb10-defaults-20261101/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_stream_ttft_ms_max) |
| `MEMRA_TENANT_BUDGETS` | unset; set, the stock server refuses to start | Not read by the stock binary: tenant budgets are a deployment-binary surface. Wire a `metering::Metering` implementation through `ServerWiring` and claim the variable (`ServerWiring::claiming`); otherwise the stock server refuses to start when it is set, so set-but-unread configuration never fails open. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_tenant_budgets) |
| `MEMRA_TIMEOUT_MS_MAX` | unset: 90,000 ms; unified memory (GB10): derived non-streaming deadline | Direct override of the `timeout_ms` ceiling and default on every response mode, for measurement cells; also the fallback streaming ceiling. Do not widen a client-facing route with it: it widens non-streaming too, which has no heartbeat before headers. On a unified-memory device with it unset and `MEMRA_SSE_PREFILL_COMMIT_MS` not `0`, the non-streaming default is the derived streaming TTFT (`MEMRA_STREAM_TTFT_MS_MAX`) plus 8192 output tokens at 4 tok/s (`[deadline] unified memory` boot line). Unparseable or below 1,000 ms keeps the shipped 90 s. Rollback: unset. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#unified-memory-devices-gb10-dgx-spark), [gb10-defaults-20261101](../research/gb10-defaults-20261101/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_timeout_ms_max) |
| `MEMRA_VIDEO_MAX_PIXELS` | 2097152 | Serving cap on total video patches (`groups*gh*gw` across all sampled frames). Below the HF default because `sdpa_naive` keys the whole span in shared memory. Frame floor and cap are compile-time (`VID_MIN_PIXELS` 4096, `VID_MAX_FRAMES` 32). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_video_max_pixels) |
| `MEMRA_VISION` | on when `MEMRA_VISION_DIR` is set | `0` skips the tower even with the dir configured, for VRAM-tight cards; image requests then 400 at the HTTP layer. The same 400 fires when the boot-time placement decision finds the overlay cannot reach embedding intake (see `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_vision) |
| `MEMRA_VISION_DIR` | unset (text-only) | Qwen-class vision tower: the checkpoint's `outside.safetensors` with `model.visual.*`, or a safetensors checkpoint directory whose shards carry the BF16 tower (point it at the served checkpoint). Set: the worker loads the tower at spawn (about 1.8 GB f32-resident) and the HTTP layer accepts `image_url` parts. Configured but unloadable is a boot panic. | [VISION-LANE.md](decisions/VISION-LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_vision_dir) |
| `MEMRA_VISION_OVERLAY_PUBLISH` | `auto` (publish only when the intake context differs) | Where a vision overlay's rows live. A device pointer is valid in one CUDA context and `splice_into` refuses a mismatch. Under a per-stage-stream ppN split the splice runs on pp stage 0, a different device from the primary, so `auto` publishes the tower's rows into stage 0's context once per session, prefill only; on one device it is a no-op. `force` always publishes (the gate arm); `0`/`off` is the rollback seam (a cross-context intake refuses by name; only `MEMRA_PP_STREAMS=0` serves images). `1` or empty mean `auto`; any other value kills the boot. Resolved once at boot into one placement decision for every tower family. | [glm53-vision-ppn-20260901](../research/glm53-vision-ppn-20260901/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_vision_overlay_publish) |
| `MEMRA_WORKER_AFFINITY` | off (unset) | Alias of `MEMRA_WORKER_CPUSET`, the original name its host-audit receipts were minted under. Same values, validation and announce lines, resolved by `affinity::resolve_worker_cpuset_env`: set alone it is honored and refusals name it; set beside a different `MEMRA_WORKER_CPUSET` value (compared literally) the boot refuses naming both. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_worker_affinity) |
| `MEMRA_WORKER_CPUSET` | off (unset) | CPU affinity for the one `memra-gpu-worker` thread every family's decode runs on, set in `worker::run` before `Engine::new` so the CUDA context and per-step threads inherit it. Values: a Linux cpu list (`8-15,104-111`); `ccx`/`on` = the worker's current L3 domain; `ccx:N` = the Nth L3 domain in sysfs order; `0`/`off`/unset = no `sched_setaffinity`. Resolved once at boot with the alias `MEMRA_WORKER_AFFINITY`: disagreeing values refuse the boot naming both; a malformed or unsatisfiable value fails the boot. Announces `[worker-affinity] engaged` (with `CLAMPED-BY-OUTER-CPUSET` when narrowed) or `[worker-affinity] off`. Off by design: a mask is machine-specific. Unrelated to `MEMRA_AFFINITY`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/host-audit-20260901/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_worker_cpuset) |
| `MEMRA_WORKER_RESPAWN` | 1 | In-process respawn attempts after the GPU worker thread panics, before the process exits 70 for the supervisor to restart it. One by default: CUDA errors are sticky per process, so a respawn rarely works, and a loop against a poisoned context looks alive and serves nothing. `0` never retries in-process. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_worker_respawn) |

## Scheduling and admission

Sessions, lanes, the queue, shedding and the SLO, batch formation, prime batching, yield and memory-shaped admission. The knobs an operator most often sets are `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS`, the lane caps (`MEMRA_LANE_MAX_INTERACTIVE` and its two siblings) and `MEMRA_MAX_QUEUE_DEPTH`; memory-shaped admission has no switch, and where a card arms it its runtime parameters are `MEMRA_ADMIT_OPEN_OUTPUT_TOKENS` and `MEMRA_ADMIT_DEFER_BUDGET_MS`. Lanes, shedding and admission are explained in [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#admission-and-backpressure).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `[prime-walk]` boot line | always on | Not a door: one boot line per spec-calibrated model, `[prime-walk] model=.. route=.. supported=.. yield_door=..` plus every term of `mtp_prime_walk_terms()`. `MEMRA_PRIME_YIELD` prints nothing on a route the walker does not handle, so read this line before scoring an arm: an unengaged arm and a negative arm otherwise look the same. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_yield) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_CALIBRATE` | on (`=0` skips) | At boot, before ready, one spec-shaped generation runs through the real serving path: the pool high-water on every device becomes the transient admission floor (never below the static `SPEC_SHRINK_RESERVE`, 1536 MiB), first-request pools materialize, and the first per-session draft-state charge is recorded (`[admit-cal] boot calibration done:`). Meanwhile the worker phase is `warming` and `/readyz` answers 503. A probe failure is loud and non-fatal. `=0` keeps the static floor and a zero draft-state charge until live captures observe one. | [RESULTS.md](../research/step37-vram-admission-20260830/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_calibrate) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_DEFER_BUDGET_MS` | `8000`; read where memory-shaped admission is armed, and always on the DSv4 route | How long one arrival may sit memory-deferred before a 429 with `Retry-After` replaces the wait, so the client hears a retryable refusal before its pre-header timeout. The stamp latches at the request's first memory defer and is carried across a step-OOM park replay. `0` disables the refusal arm and restores the unbounded FIFO defer, keeping the open-output charge and demote-before-flush. On the DSv4 route it is clamped to half of `MEMRA_HEALTH_STALL_S`, and `0` means that half bound. | [ADMIT-BY-MEMORY-DEFAULT.md](decisions/ADMIT-BY-MEMORY-DEFAULT.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_defer_budget_ms) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_DRIVER_HEADROOM_MB` | 2048; `0` = off | Driver-headroom rung of admission. Admission counts the async pool's cached blocks as free, which a large prime's driver allocations (cuBLAS workspaces, graph instantiation, blocks larger than any cached fragment) cannot use. After every admission, on each owned device whose driver free is below the floor while the pool holds cached blocks, `cuMemPoolTrimTo` releases only the shortfall (`[admit-trim]` line); the slice re-maps once on next use. `0` is the rollback seam. Unified memory (GB10) keeps 2048 on the raw `cuMemGetInfo` reading, and the derived unified prefix-cache share subtracts this floor. | [PREFIX-CACHE-DISCRETE.md](decisions/PREFIX-CACHE-DISCRETE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_driver_headroom_mb) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_OPEN_OUTPUT_TOKENS` | `32768`; read only where memory-shaped admission is armed (one full-power RTX PRO 6000 Workstation card) | The output length a naked open-output request (no `max_tokens`, no `max_ctx`) is charged and given as its output budget, both capped at the `MEMRA_CTX` envelope the unarmed program would charge. Without it such a request books the whole `MEMRA_CTX` envelope. Positive integer; `0` or garbage keeps 32768. A deployment that advertises a different `max_output` sets this to that number, derived from its model registry, never hand-typed. | [ADMIT-BY-MEMORY-DEFAULT.md](decisions/ADMIT-BY-MEMORY-DEFAULT.md), [DAY40.md](../research/spill-b-20260919/DAY40.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_open_output_tokens) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_BUDGET_MB` | unset (derived at boot) | KV budget arm in MiB the predictive check compares against (companion to `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_SHADOW`). Unset, the worker derives it at boot: `effective_free_bytes` after load and calibration, minus the device prefix-cache budget, minus the admission transient reserve (at least 1,536 MiB; `MEMRA_ADMIT_RESERVE_MB` wins where set), saturating at 0, so a config-changing redeploy re-derives it. A set value is the operator override and wins. The boot line states the value and `budget_src=`. If unset and the boot free-VRAM query fails, the KV arm stays unarmed. Rollback: set it explicitly. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_predict_budget_mb) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_ENFORCE` | `0` (OFF) | `1` arms the shadow and turns a `reject-kv` verdict into the shed 429 (`rate_limit_error`, Retry-After the predicted earliest in-flight completion, 5 s when unknown) before any device work, with a stable client sentence. The static capacity check (`request_hat + booked_hat <= static_budget`) is separate from the physical live-headroom check (`AdmissionCostModel`). Both call sites charge through the ring-aware `kv_hat_ring` with fail-closed geometry validation. On multi-device (PP/TP) deployments `1` fails server boot. `reject-slot` keeps the FIFO wait; exempt tenants (`MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_EXEMPT_TENANTS`) keep shadow behavior. The line carries `enforce=1 live_free_bytes=<n>`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_predict_enforce) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_EXEMPT_TENANTS` | empty | Comma-separated tenant ids exempt from predictive-admission enforcement (companion to `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_SHADOW`), meant for probe and watchdog identities. Matching is on the tenant row identity (`auth::meter_key`, `t:` prefix stripped). Exempt tenants still count as in-flight load; their would-reject lines carry `exempt=1`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_predict_exempt_tenants) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_SHADOW` | `0` (OFF) | `1` (any value but `0`; `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_ENFORCE=1` implies it) logs one `[admit-predict]` line per interactive request at its first decisive admission: slot check (`inflight(model) >= cap`), then the KV book (`kv_hat` plus in-flight `kv_hat`s against the `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_BUDGET_MB` arm). `kv_hat` charges B/token x (prompt + predicted completion + 8) plus the physical cost's fixed terms (`RequestCharge::from_physical_cost`). The predicted completion is the per-(tenant, model) p95 over a 512-sample ring, restart-volatile. Fields are locked by `verdict_line_locks_fields`. Log only: nothing is rejected. Rollback: unset or `0`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_predict_shadow) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREDICT_VG_DEBT` | unset (OFF), decide-by: 2026-10-11 | `1` makes the predictive verdict read the same `dspark_vg_admission_debt` the physical gate reserves later in the admission, through a non-recording peek, and compare `request_kv_hat + booked` against `budget_bytes - vg_debt` (saturating); the `[admit-predict]` line gains ` vg_debt=<bytes>`. The debt is non-zero only where the verify-graph pool can engage. Unset reads nothing. | [DAY48.md](../research/spill-b-20260919/DAY48.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_predict_vg_debt) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_PREFILL_WORKSPACE` | on (`0` = the older estimate) | Workspace-aware admission: each request's charge includes the prefill workspace its prime allocates, keyed on prompt rows, never `ctx_cap`. Cold charge (every card but the RTX 5090 Laptop GPU): non-hyper trunks charge per-call row bytes (`38 x n_embd + 14 x n_ff_max`, plus routed-expert staging on MoE) times the widest call any prime of at most the prompt's rows makes under the deployment's schedule (`MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK`, the pipeline geometry; `PrimeWorkspaceShape::call_rows`) plus a whole-prompt hiddens stack; hyper trunks use `hyper_prime_workspace_shape`. On the RTX 5090 Laptop GPU a single-device slab prime is charged what it allocates beside the resident slab (boot line `[admission] prefill workspace: held` or `cold`), with the exceptions in the decision record. `0` is the rollback seam. | [PREFILL-WORKSPACE-HELD.md](decisions/PREFILL-WORKSPACE-HELD.md), [c8-workspace-20261108](../research/c8-workspace-20261108/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_prefill_workspace) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_RESERVE_MB` | unset (1536) | Teeth and diagnostics door, not a tuning knob: overrides the spec-session admission transient reserve (`SPEC_SHRINK_RESERVE`, 1.5 GiB), which admission charges only when the placement can admit spec (PP-2's LOW=0 keeps the legacy `cost` headroom term). `tools/serve-stress-gate.sh --teeth` sets it to 16 to prove the gate still catches a broken cost model. Warns when set. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_reserve_mb) |
| `MEMRA_ADMIT_YIELD` | on | Fairness parameter, not a rollback seam. On: a request arriving mid-burst ends the in-flight spec burst at the next round boundary (`PENDING_ADMITS`), and not-yet-emitted sessions burst before mid-generation peers, so contended first text no longer scales with `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST`. Content is byte-identical either way. `0` selects lockstep fairness (full-burst holds and index-order stepping) for pure-batch tiers. A session holding a carried token yields at burst entry with nothing emitted at most once in a row, then runs one round (`SPEC_ENTRY_YIELD_MAX`). Since memra#1227 the newcomer-first order also holds against a pooled cohort: an MTP session that has emitted nothing and has a same-model speculative peer primes before the tick's cohorts and joins that tick's lockstep, where before it waited out the cohort's burst (`0` keeps the cohorts first). Since memra#1244 an interactive plain session that gets its step only after the tick's bursts ends each MTP burst of the tick at its first round, so it advances once per round instead of once per burst (`0` keeps full bursts). The classes: one that decodes and cannot ride (constrained, vision, fork, eager-only model), a rider candidate no cohort carries this tick (no pooled cohort of its model, or an interactive decoding non-rider keeps the model's riders in phase (c)), and one whose prime phase (b) runs this tick with the rest of its prompt within one chunk; only in a tick with an MTP burst and no DFlash2, glm5 or gemma burst. Judge and harvest sessions never engage it, never keep interactive riders in phase (c), and on yielded ticks phase (c) steps them and phase (d) primes them once every N yielded ticks (N the rounds of the longest recent full burst; about half their burst cadence on GB10). Host-sampled riding (an interactive greedy request with a penalty, or an interactive sampled penalty the card keeps on the host, rides a cohort like a device-sampled session; a dark host-sampled session stays in phase (c)) is not under this switch, nor is the rule that only an interactive decoder that cannot ride holds a model's riders in phase (c) (with `0` a dark json job beside interactive riders lets them ride, where before this change it kept them in phase (c)): on a card without a cost table for the model (the RTX 5090 Laptop GPU on Qwen3.8-27B) a rider takes a plain step inside every round, so with `0` the streams still pay that step in each round of a full burst. | [SPEC-ENTRY-YIELD-BOUND.md](decisions/SPEC-ENTRY-YIELD-BOUND.md), [SPEC-ROUND-YIELD.md](decisions/SPEC-ROUND-YIELD.md), [admission-20260806](../research/admission-20260806/), [spec-entry-yield-bound-20261110](../research/spec-entry-yield-bound-20261110/), [spec-cohort-newcomer-20261112](../research/spec-cohort-newcomer-20261112/), [spec-plain-round-yield-20261112](../research/spec-plain-round-yield-20261112/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_admit_yield) |
| `MEMRA_BG_CKPT_GRACE_MS` | 5000 | Checkpoint-mode grace: a job still alive this long after SIGUSR1 is SIGKILLed and resumes from its last on-disk write, so the yield bound holds against a wedged job. |  |
| `MEMRA_BG_JOB` | unset (no runner) | Background job command (`sh -c`, own process group, PDEATHSIG=KILL) that runs only in valleys, a class below every serving lane and not a request. Yield is SIGSTOP to the group at the first sign of traffic; resume is SIGCONT in the next valley. Exit 0 = complete; 75 = checkpointed, relaunched next valley; anything else = failed, never relaunched. Unset: the runner and the `/metrics` `bg` block do not exist. | [darktrain-20260807](../research/darktrain-20260807/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_bg_job) |
| `MEMRA_BG_POLL_MS` | 25 | Background runner poll interval, the detection half of the yield bound (bound = poll plus signal delivery). Lower is a tighter yield with more wakeups; minimum 1. |  |
| `MEMRA_BG_VRAM_MB` | 0 (CPU-only job) | Background job VRAM budget. `0` = CPU-only, no GPU probe. `>0`: launch is refused (`refused_vram`, retried next valley) unless the minimum free across visible GPUs covers budget plus `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_HEADROOM_GB`; minimum, not sum, so a PP-2 pair fits the tightest card. An unreadable `nvidia-smi` refuses. The budget is exported to the job as `MEMRA_BG_VRAM_MB`; staying inside it is the job's contract (no enforcement), and VRAM-aware admission still guards serving. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_bg_vram_mb) |
| `MEMRA_BG_YIELD_MODE` | `stop` | `stop` = SIGSTOP/SIGCONT; the job's memory, VRAM included, stays resident while stopped, so budget for the job's life. `yielded` publishes only once every thread of every process in the job's process group is stopped or bound to stop; a member that cannot be read or signaled fails the job. Not covered: a process that left the group or is hidden from /proc. `checkpoint` = SIGUSR1 means checkpoint to disk and exit 75; relaunch resumes next valley (at-least-once). Toy proof: `tools/bg-ckpt-counter.py`. An unknown value warns and uses `stop`. | [darklane-vfork-stop-20261109](../research/darklane-vfork-stop-20261109/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_bg_yield_mode) |
| `MEMRA_DECODE_BATCH_CAP` | auto: exact-16 models 32 or 16 by a boot wave probe (16 under PP), else 8; step35 and gemma4 at 8 | Batched-decode chunk width (engine assert and scheduler chunking, clamp 1..32). Unset is per model: an exact-16 tier model (`decode_batch_exact16_ok`: every matmul has a bit-exact b16-class kernel) takes 32-row waves when a boot probe finds one wave faster than two 16-row waves, else 16, and 16 under PP; every other model takes 8. step35 and gemma4 cap at 8, and the GLM-5.3 hyper plan at 15 (`PRIME_MIN_T - 1`); the env can only narrow those. One class without a b16 kernel drops the checkpoint to 8; diagnose with `MEMRA_EXACT16_WHY=1`. An explicit value skips the probe. Widths above a model's exact tier are a measurement door: m=16 GEMM/MMQ classes shift logits. | [batched-tick-inc3-20260801](../research/batched-tick-inc3-20260801/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_decode_batch_cap) |
| `MEMRA_FIRST_TOKEN_DEADLINE_GATE` | `0` (OFF) | `1` arms a first-token feasibility check at the worker's admission seam (post-tokenize, interactive lane only, capture routes exempt, re-checked every defer tick): when (prompt tokens plus every active session's unprimed prefill tokens) / `MEMRA_PREFILL_FLOOR_TOK_S` exceeds the remaining wire deadline x1.5, the request gets the shed contract's 429 with Retry-After 1..=60 s, unbilled and not admitted. Streaming included: it is the load-dependent counterpart of `MEMRA_NONSTREAM_DEADLINE_GATE`. Not admit-predict enforcement. Off is byte-identical. Rollback: unset or `0`. Log line: `[first-token-gate] id=<request_id> refused: ...`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_first_token_deadline_gate) |
| `MEMRA_LANE_MAX_INTERACTIVE`/`_JUDGE`/`_HARVEST` | 32 / 4 / 8 | Per-lane resident session caps. The interactive cap applies to the sidecar shape; the serve path caps interactive with `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS`. |  |
| `MEMRA_LANE_WINDOW_S` | 30 | Window in seconds of the interactive step-p99 estimator that the `MEMRA_SLO_P99_MS` shed gate reads. |  |
| `MEMRA_MAX_QUEUE_DEPTH` | 4 x the selected lane's session cap | Absolute per-lane queue bound, the backpressure backstop. Past it a new request sheds with 429 plus `Retry-After` (`code: shed_queue`) instead of joining a queue it cannot be served from; an admitted request is never shed. Reservations count per lane, so judge and harvest traffic cannot take interactive queue capacity. The interactive lane admits up to `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS` and queues beyond it. Raise it for clients that would rather wait; lower it to fail fast. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_max_queue_depth) |
| `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS` | 64 | Maximum concurrently active interactive sessions under batched scheduling; admits beyond it queue FIFO. The deepseek-v4.1 route (`dsv41_serve`) takes its session cap from the same derivation: that many requests share each runner forward, its memory book queueing the rest. Not a capacity guarantee: the VRAM-aware admission gate is the binding cap and can 429 a request with no attainable headroom even with zero peers (`[admit-oom] VRAM reject`, pre-header). Judge and harvest lanes cap separately (`MEMRA_LANE_MAX_*`). | [RESULTS.md](../research/step37-vram-admission-20260830/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_max_sessions) |
| `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` / `MEMRA_PREFILL_JUDGE` / `MEMRA_PREFILL_HARVEST` | 1024 / 256 / 256 | Per-tick prefill token budgets. Concurrent interactive sessions take 1024 each; a naked sole fresh interactive request widens once to at most 8192 tokens so PP-2 keeps its eight-microbatch geometry in one prime call, and an armed affinity checkpoint (`ckpt_at`) does not veto that. An explicit `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK` is authoritative and disables the widening. Judge and harvest are per-tick stall bounds, further capped by SLO headroom. A Gemma prime with a vision overlay refuses by name above about 12,288 tokens per take (`sdpa_naive_island_f32`). | [GRID-Y-ROW-BLOCKS.md](decisions/GRID-Y-ROW-BLOCKS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prefill_harvest) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH` | `6` | Most sequences per cross-request prime batch. Complete fresh prompts batch on every architecture with batched prime. Dense models also batch one bounded continuation chunk per eligible cold session per tick, so cold primes do not serially hold decode-ready rows. Routed-MoE carried chunks and cache-hit continuations stay on per-session primes on every lane until a multi-chunk plus serving-decode gate admits them. `1` is the full rollback seam: per-session serialized cold primes. | [coldhol-20260812](../research/coldhol-20260812/), [coldfix-20260812](../research/coldfix-20260812/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_batch) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH_HOLD_MS` | `4` | Batch-formation hold in ms for staggered arrivals. The same window is the post-retirement refill grace: when an interactive completion frees a client slot while cold prefill remains, cold prefill waits briefly for the replacement request. A formation window shorter than the previous tick is waited out inside the tick (`formation_hold`): an arrival in the window defers the prime to the next tick, otherwise it primes this tick, blocking the worker for at most the remaining window. Longer windows, and the refill grace, keep the whole-tick skip. `0` disables both holds. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_batch_hold_ms) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_BATCH_MAX_T` | `2048` | Most tokens one sequence brings to a concat prime batch. |  |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_CALLLOCAL` | off | `1` restores the full pre-fix tick-segmentation arithmetic: the per-call `seq_end` (`cache.pos + t`, ignoring the caller's `queued_after`) plus the raw, unaligned step35 SWA view offset; both halves are needed since the windowed hd128 FA default. Rollback seam of the tick-segmentation fix and the `tickinv35` gate's canary injection; never on in a measured default run. | [tick-seg-20260807](../research/tick-seg-20260807), [v072-fix1-20260808](../research/v072-fix1-20260808), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_calllocal) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK` | `4096`; PP-2 auto microchunks when unset | Nominal chunked-prime size in tokens. `0` = monolithic up to `PRIME_CHUNK_LAUNCH_CAP` (65,520), chunked at the cap beyond. Unset on a live PP-2 prime split: up to eight microchunks of `min(4096, max(128, ceil(T/8)))` for `T >= 256`, schedule from `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK_SCHED`. An explicit value gives fixed ranges, caps at 65,520 on ring-off walks, and keeps the 4096 clamp on a SlidingGatedMoe ring plan. Serial-trunk prefill logits are bit-identical across values, except: `1024` selects the FA2 attention class on the Qwen FA2 profile (170-SM sm_120a); chunked-GDN boundaries snap to the `gdn_chunk_size()` grid; the mHC trunk (glm5_next) above 4096 is a near-tie class gated at 2e-5 relative. | [GB10-PRIME-CHUNK-8192.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-CHUNK-8192.md), [QWEN-PRIME-FA2-DEFAULT.md](decisions/QWEN-PRIME-FA2-DEFAULT.md), [VERDICT.md](../research/chunk-invariance-20260805/VERDICT.md), [step35-chunkfix-20260807](../research/step35-chunkfix-20260807), [1m-context-20260828](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/1m-context-20260828/), [1m-demo-20260829](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/1m-demo-20260829/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_chunk) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK_SCHED` | `dynamic` | Schedule of the PP-2 naked-auto microchunks. `dynamic` keeps the fixed policy's chunk count, emits a short fill chunk, then places the boundaries at equal increments of the work model `W(L)=L^2+8*T*L` (a larger middle, a shrinking tail); any other value, such as `fixed`, is the rollback to equal-token ranges. Scheduler-only: on chunked-GDN archs both schedules emit grid-aligned boundaries (`align_prime_ranges_to_gdn`). An explicit `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK` overrides it and stays fixed. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_chunk_sched) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_F32CHUNK0` | off | `1` restores the legacy first-chunk arithmetic: chunk 0 attends this batch's f32 K/V through `fa_prefill` instead of the quantized cache. Rollback seam of the chunk-invariance fix and the chunkinv gate's canary injection; chunk-variant by construction, so never on in a measured default run. | [VERDICT.md](../research/chunk-invariance-20260805/VERDICT.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_f32chunk0) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_TOK_PER_MS` | 8.0 | Dark-prefill chunk estimator in the x-lane QoS tick: the prime rate (tokens per ms) that converts SLO headroom (`slo_p99_ms` minus decode time) into a prefill chunk cap. |  |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_TOKENWISE` | unset (batched prime from 16 tokens) | Set (any value): every phase primes token by token through the decode step; prompts under 16 tokens take that path anyway, and frozen Hy3 CPU/GPU expert serving selects it after its profiling warmup. It also pins the first generated token to the tokenwise oracle stream: the batched prime is a different numeric config and may flip first tokens at near-tie margins. | [prime-gate-coverage-20260802](../research/prime-gate-coverage-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_tokenwise) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_YIELD` | on (unset or `1`); `0` rollback; decide-by: 2026-10-06 | A `PrimeWalker` advances one frozen prime chunk per tick, returns to drain and admission, then serves peers in bounded rotation; `0` drains the same walker in one call. Both arms run the same frozen range program, so the seam changes interleaving, never bytes. Each peer gets one prime chunk, spec round or decode step per advance; for m advances, N slots and peer quantum Q, added long-prompt TTFT is at most `(m-1)*(N-1)*Q` plus overhead. Walker routes only (GDN MTP prime where `[prime-walk] supported=true`, DFlash, GLM plain and spec); the serial plain trunk is bounded by `MEMRA_PREFILL_TICK`; E4B and dsv4 stay monolithic. `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK` is the quantum. Trace: `MEMRA_TICK_TRACE=1`. | [PRIME-FAIRNESS-DEFAULT.md](decisions/PRIME-FAIRNESS-DEFAULT.md), [prefill-fairness-20260908](../research/prefill-fairness-20260908/), [prime-fairness-default-20260922](../research/prime-fairness-default-20260922/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_yield) |
| `MEMRA_QUEUE_WAIT_CEILING_S` | 0 (off) | `N > 0`: an interactive request whose estimated queue wait (mean tokens per request x p50 step latency, scaled by queued waves) exceeds `N` seconds is refused at submission with 429 (`code: shed_queue_wait`, `Retry-After` = the estimate clamped to 1..60 s, X-RateLimit trio). Evaluated only for a request that would wait, interactive lane only, after the `shed_queue` and `shed_deadline` arms, never against an admitted request. Off keeps the silent FIFO wait up to the caller's deadline (see `MEMRA_MAX_SESSIONS`). Rollback seam: unset or `0`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_queue_wait_ceiling_s) |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_B1FAST` | OFF | `1` opts an eligible fixed-solo tick into `decode_layers_eager`, the m=1 fusion trunk `decode_step_h` uses. Unset, `0` and other values keep B=1 on the generic batched body used at B>=2, the one-class correctness default. The eager arm changes FP composition, so it is a measurement door, not a kernel-tier fix. Q35-MoE stays ineligible even under `=1`; gemma4/e4b, m3 and verify-exact are excluded. `decode-batch-gate` pins default B=1 against B=N bit identity; the release battery's `argmax-margin-batch` cell unsets it. | [servepath-p2-20260805](../research/servepath-p2-20260805/), [b1fix-20260810](../research/b1fix-20260810/), [q35bug-20260812](../research/q35bug-20260812/), [eosclass-20260813](../research/eosclass-20260813/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_serve_b1fast) |
| `MEMRA_SHED_JUDGE` / `MEMRA_SHED_HARVEST` | 1.00 / 0.90 | Per-lane shed thresholds as fractions of `MEMRA_SLO_P99_MS`. |  |
| `MEMRA_SHED_MIN_SAMPLES` | 32 | Minimum estimator-window samples before the p99 shed gate may fire; below it the window counts as cold (admit), like the empty-window rule. With n samples the window p99 is the maximum for every n <= 50, so a sparse probe's own tick could shed real requests on an idle server; 32 separates probe cardinality (5 to 10 per window) from real load. The `starved` sentinel needs no window and still sheds. `0` disables the floor. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_shed_min_samples) |
| `MEMRA_SLO_P99_MS` | 50 | Interactive decode-step p99 SLO in ms for the `x-lane` QoS gate. Requests tag a class with the `x-lane` header (`interactive`, `judge`, `harvest`; absent = interactive). Interactive always admits; judge and harvest shed at admission (429 plus `Retry-After`) when measured interactive step p99 crosses `MEMRA_SHED_JUDGE` / `MEMRA_SHED_HARVEST` of the SLO, or when a starvation sentinel fires. Interactive decode rows sort first; judge and harvest prefill run inside SLO headroom only. Lowering it trades bulk throughput for request latency. | [qos-p95-20260802](../research/qos-p95-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_slo_p99_ms) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_OOM_RETRIES` | 3 | Step-time CUDA OOM park budget per request. Every teardown reclaims before the retry: the parked reuse, spec and dspark pools drop, the device prefix cache evicts to half (all of it on a consecutive OOM tick), then the pool is fenced and trimmed (`[admit-oom] step-OOM reclaim:` line). Parks only a session that has emitted nothing and only on a quoted CUDA OOM; a spec-burst step re-queues at the front; a park replay serves capture-disabled. `0` is the rollback seam (kill on OOM). | [admit-oom-20260806](../research/admit-oom-20260806/), [RESULTS.md](../research/step37-vram-admission-20260830/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_oom_retries) |
| `MEMRA_VALLEY_S` | 2.0 | Seconds the worker must be completely idle (no active sessions, queued admissions or pending HTTP handoffs) before the background job (`MEMRA_BG_JOB`) may launch or resume. Published as `/metrics` `serve_idle_seconds` (0.0 at any work). Asymmetric on purpose: yield fires on the busy edge with no debounce; only resume waits. Read once. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_valley_s) |

## KV cache, prefix cache and memory tiers

KV formats, the prefix-reuse pool and session affinity, the device prefix-cache budget and policy, the pinned host tier and its handoff file, pause demotion, on-demand KV planes and the sliding-window ring. Operators size `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` (derived at boot when unset) and arm the host tier with `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB`; entry sizes are exact `prefix_cache_bytes` deltas per model and prefix length, with raw receipts in [research/cachesize-20260813/](../research/cachesize-20260813/). Caching behavior and the host-tier lifecycle are in [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#prompt-caching).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_AFFINITY` | on (forced off while PP host-bounce is active) | `0` disables session-affinity resume: a conversation whose history the client rewrote (think-strip) no longer resumes its parked session and re-primes in full. Rollback seam and exactness A/B arm, not a tuning knob; prefer it over `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL=0`, which also kills token and text-prefix resumes. Under `0` a recurrent plain cache loses its token-prefix resume checkpoint, and a recurrent spec session keeps only its prompt-end checkpoint, so it resumes only when that prompt end is on the 32-token grid. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#session-affinity--resuming-a-rewritten-conversation-lanesession-affinity-2026-08-05), [session-affinity-20260805](../research/session-affinity-20260805/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_affinity) |
| `MEMRA_DSA_INDEX_RING` | on, `5120` rows | The DSA k-pool indexer state plane (glm5_next, GLM-5.3-Flash) is a tail ring: each row is read once by its own pool's key build, and `mla_kpool_indices` drains the ring inside the call, so any call size fits any ring. Unset = 5120 rows, a working-set choice; the floor is one pool (4 rows). `0` is the flat `max_ctx` plane, byte for byte the pre-ring allocation, but not the safe arm: it was much slower at long prefill. `<n>` pins the rows, rounded down to a whole pool and refused below one; unparseable = unset; a ring not shorter than `max_ctx` is the flat plane. A pool-key build further behind the append than the ring fails loudly. | [ring-sizing-20260828](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/ring-sizing-20260828/), [rebaseline-and-surface-20260828](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/rebaseline-and-surface-20260828/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsa_index_ring) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_GKV` | on (`=0` reverts) | Gemma global (hd512) layers store KV as e4m3 through the kf8vf8 flash module. `=0` returns them to q8_0/q5_1 KV. |  |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_WKV` | spec serving (`MEMRA_DRAFT` set): off (q8_0/q5_1); otherwise on (e4m3) | Gemma windowed (hd256) layers: e4m3 KV or q8_0/q5_1 KV. The unset default is serving-keyed because the MTP drafter's single SWA attention loses acceptance on e4m3 KV noise. Any value other than `0` forces e4m3; `=0` forces q8_0/q5_1. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gemma_wkv) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_KV_HOST` | off, decide-by: 2026-09-21 | `1` pins the whole `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB` budget as one portable cacheable arena before readiness, after cgroup and MemAvailable checks with a 32 GiB margin; startup fails rather than shrinking. Request paths never pin or free: whole-image leases reserve atomically and recycle on eviction, and a capacity refusal is a bounded admission error. Leases cover K/V, conv/SSM, GLM/TP latent and index state, draft planes, logits and the boundary row. GLM latent and TP prefix images may then demote to host and promote on their capture streams. Needs positive host and device budgets and `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT`; TP device capture exists only on sm_100a builds. Off refuses GLM host demotion and promotion. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_tp_kv_host) |
| `MEMRA_KV_FLEX` | `0` (OFF) | `1` lets the chat model's device prefix cache borrow idle VRAM above its `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` floor and shed it when capture traffic (embeddings, rerank) arrives. The insert budget becomes floor plus a grant re-derived each tick as effective-free VRAM minus `MEMRA_KV_FLEX_GUARD_MB`. A capture arrival evicts the borrowed residency back to the floor before any of its admission math; under session pressure the borrow sheds first, before `evict_all`. Borrowed bytes evaporate on shed, never demote. `PrefixCache::total_bytes` stays the only byte accountant. Off is byte-identical. Rollback: unset or `0` and redeploy. Counters: `kv_flex_borrowed_bytes`, `kv_flex_sheds`, `kv_flex_shed_ms`. | [kv-flex-20260831](../research/kv-flex-20260831/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_flex) |
| `MEMRA_KV_FLEX_GUARD_MB` | `4096` | Effective-free VRAM in MiB the flex grant never touches (companion to `MEMRA_KV_FLEX`). It bounds the mid-tick transients that allocate between grant refreshes (prefill and FA workspaces, capture prefill slabs). Malformed values fall back loudly to the default. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_flex_guard_mb) |
| `MEMRA_KV_FLEX_HOLD_MS` | `2000` | How long in ms the flex grant stays at 0 after a capture arrival (companion to `MEMRA_KV_FLEX`). An embeddings array admits one worker request per input, so every arrival re-arms the hold; expiry re-opens the grant at the next tick refresh. Malformed values fall back loudly to the default. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_flex_hold_ms) |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_HANDOFF` | empty (OFF) | Path of the cross-deploy host-tier handoff file; empty or unset keeps the tier process-lifetime, so a blue/green flip starts cold. Set the same path on both slots of a host. `POST /admin/kv-host/export` drain-demotes every evictable device prefix entry, then writes the tier newest-first atomically; a slot still carrying traffic refuses unless forced. Import (`POST /admin/kv-host/import`, or at boot when the file exists) validates the header and re-inserts one entry per tick through the normal host insert. Corrupt frames skip; a layout version change, changed model stamps or a file older than 3600 s refuses the file. The importer unlinks it. Same-host format. Rollback: unset. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_host_handoff) |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_HANDOFF_MB` | `0` (whole resident pool) | Newest-first byte cap in MiB on exported handoff entries. `0` exports everything the tier holds, which `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB` already bounds. With a cap, selection walks the LRU from the most recent entry and keeps the newest entries that fit, skipping oversized ones and continuing; skipped entries are counted (`skipped_over_cap`) and serve cold after the flip. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_host_handoff_mb) |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB` | `0` (OFF) | Prefix-cache host spill tier in MiB of pinned cacheable host RAM. A device capacity eviction demotes the entry's bytes verbatim; a later device miss that exactly matches a host entry re-materializes a device `PrefixEntry` and serves through `prefix_restore`, byte-lossless. Host entries mirror device entries field for field; a layout version mismatch refuses. Host side is plain byte-LRU. On: the budget clamps to MemAvailable x 0.6 with a loud warning, and a pinned-alloc failure latches the tier off, never pageable. Copies run on the CUDA owner thread, timed in `/metrics`. On unified memory (GB10) it adds no capacity. `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_KV_HOST=1` reserves the whole budget instead. Rollback: `0`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_host_mb) |
| `MEMRA_KV_HOST_TENANT_PCT` | `50` | One tenant's maximum share of the `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB` budget, integer percent 1..=100, keyed on the cache-namespace metering row (`t:<tenant>` under a keyring, else the raw `cache_salt`). A demotion that would push the tenant past its share first reclaims that tenant's own host entries, oldest first; only when the image alone exceeds the share, or the tenant's entries cannot cover it, does the demotion evaporate, checked before the D2H copy. `100` disarms the check, leaving the global byte-LRU; single-tenant deployments set it. Malformed or out-of-range values fall back to 50 with a loud warning. Counters: `prefix_host_tenant_reclaims`, `_reclaims_wasted`, `_rejects`. | [DAY12.md](../research/spill-a-20260919/DAY12.md), [REPORT.md](../research/kv-tenancy-20260831/REPORT.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_host_tenant_pct) |
| `MEMRA_KV_PAUSE_DEMOTE` | `0` (OFF) | `1` (needs `MEMRA_KV_HOST_MB > 0`) demotes a session's boundary state to the host tier when its response ends in a completed tool-call block and stays untouched for `MEMRA_KV_PAUSE_DEMOTE_MS`, freeing device cache for the pause. Two shapes demote: the exact-fed plain continuation-pool park, released only once a host copy publishes, and the deepest resident prefix entry prefixing the committed tape, by reference. A request arriving first cancels cleanly. The signal is a worker-side tail predicate (`toolcall::tail_ends_with_tool_call`) after trailing stop ids are trimmed. Spec and DSpark parks and vision sessions never arm. Rollback: unset or `0`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_pause_demote) |
| `MEMRA_KV_PAUSE_DEMOTE_MS` | `5000` | Delay in ms before an untouched tool-call pause demotes (companion to `MEMRA_KV_PAUSE_DEMOTE`); set from a session-gap census so only the long tail of pauses pays the host round trip. The timer rides the worker's tick and recv timeout, no thread. `0` demotes at the first sweep after retire, kept as a gate arm. Malformed values fall back to 5000 with a loud warning. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_pause_demote_ms) |
| `MEMRA_KV_REUSE` | on | `0` disables the KV prefix-reuse pool. |  |
| `MEMRA_KV_VMM_FAULT` | unset (off); arms only where the on-demand KV planes are on (full-power RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Workstation) | Fault door of `tools/health-fault-gate.sh` arm k: a comma list of `build:<n>` (the n-th construction grow fails once), `ensure:<n>` (the n-th owner grow at an ensure point fails once) and `mapper:all` (every mapper grow fails), each a synthetic `CUDA_ERROR_OUT_OF_MEMORY` before any driver call. Any other token refuses the boot on every card, and so does `mapper:all` where the planes grow inline. Where the planes are pooled a valid spec arms nothing (`[kv-vmm] door=OFF`). The planes themselves are a card default with no switch. | [KV-VMM-SERVING-DEFAULT.md](decisions/KV-VMM-SERVING-DEFAULT.md), [DAY37.md](../research/spill-b-20260919/DAY37.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kv_vmm_fault) |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` | derived, boot-clamped: discrete max(2 entries, free after session peak); unified max(2 entries, half the pool) | Cross-request prefix-cache byte budget in MiB (`0` = off). A valid explicit value is authoritative and unclamped; an unparseable one falls back to 256 MiB. Derived: the floor is two full served-context snapshots of the largest model; a discrete card requests the larger of the floor and boot free less the 1.5 GiB reserve, `MEMRA_ADMIT_DRIVER_HEADROOM_MB` and the resident-session peak; unified memory takes half the clamp. One worker-global budget, plain global LRU. A new session sharing a >=64-token prefix skips that prefill (latent caches need `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT`). Restores are whole-entry only. Ring-ON and host-bounce placements refuse snapshots. `cache_salt` isolates entries. | [PREFIX-CACHE-DISCRETE.md](decisions/PREFIX-CACHE-DISCRETE.md), [PREFIX-CACHE-POLICY.md](decisions/PREFIX-CACHE-POLICY.md), [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#unified-memory-devices-gb10-dgx-spark), [prompt-cache-20260802](../research/prompt-cache-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prefix_cache_mb) |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT` | OFF | `1` makes prefix entries carry latent (MLA/DSA) planes for `StatePlan::LatentKvCache` models (glm5_next): the f32 latent rows, the resident k-pool keys with `index_pools_ready` (they cannot be rebuilt under `MEMRA_DSA_INDEX_RING`), and the live `len % pool` tail-ring rows. The derived `MEMRA_PREFIX_CACHE_MB` books the latent per-token term only while it is on. Off, the rollback: capture and restore refuse latent caches loudly and glm5 primes in full. On: restore admits only entries whose latent slots are populated. With `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_PREFIX=1` a glm5 spec session's boundary capture carries the latent tails. Restores are whole-entry only. | [DESIGN.md](../research/glm5-prefix-latent-20260830/DESIGN.md), [FINDINGS.txt](../research/prefix-restore-toolcall-20260828/FINDINGS.txt), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prefix_latent) |
| `MEMRA_PREFIX_STABLE_BOUNDARY` | `0` (OFF) | `1` arms plain prefix-cache capture at the render-stable last-turn boundary (`plain_checkpoint_boundary`: just before the last turn-marker run, grid-aligned) on the miss and shallow-hit paths, deepest-wins against the LCP-taught and first-message arms and `has_key`-deduped, so each cold turn mints an entry the session's next turn can hit on the device tier or promote from the host tier. The prompt-end seed is skipped when the boundary sits within `prefix_seed_deepen_min()` of prompt end. An armed `snapshot_at` excludes the session from in-batch fanout and prime batching. Off is byte-identical. Rollback: unset or `0`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prefix_stable_boundary) |
| `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL` | 2 | Per-`(model, cache namespace)` LRU cap (PC-ISO), applied separately to the continuation, speculative (MTP) and DFlash2/dspark whole-session pools, inside `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL_GLOBAL_CAP`. Recurrent (GDN) sessions park only on a resumable boundary: the dspark pool rewinds to the last prime boundary (with `MEMRA_DSPARK_PREFIX_RESTORE` off), the spec pool resumes only from an on-grid turn checkpoint. At the default, a sequential workload of more than 2 sessions in one namespace evicts each next entry before it returns; raise it only with a matching VRAM budget. `0` parks nothing. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#cache-salt-isolation), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_reuse_pool) |
| `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL_GLOBAL_CAP` | 16 | Process-wide maximum of parked entries across every model, cache namespace and the three whole-session pools (continuation, MTP spec, DFlash2/dspark). Before a park that would exceed it the worker drops the globally oldest entry; if no slot frees, the insert refuses. The per-namespace `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL` cap still applies. Size it as `floor(parked VRAM budget / worst-case entry bytes)` and lower it for long-context entries. `0` parks nothing. | [RESULTS.md](../research/27bab-20260810/RESULTS.md#vram-ledger), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_reuse_pool_global_cap) |
| `MEMRA_SWA_RING` | off globally; armed on at load for the SlidingGatedMoe (step37) family | Bounded KV ring for Step35/37 sliding-window layers: SWA trunk layers and the SWA MTP scratch allocate `min(max_ctx, 512 + 4096 + 31)` rows while full-attention layers keep `max_ctx`. Plan-scoped (`swa_ring_on_for_plan`): only a SlidingGatedMoe plan's cache, admission, MTP scratch and prime schedule take it, so a co-loaded Gemma4 keeps flat planes. With it on, explicit prime chunks cap at 4096, flat-history prefix snapshots, restores and fanout refuse with a log line, and lapped affinity or spec rewinds decline. `1` forces it on without a step37 load, `0` is the kill switch. Native rank-local TP SWA uses its own ring under `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P`. | [RESULTS.md](../research/ringval-20260810/RESULTS.md), [RESULTS.md](../research/newboxgates-20260811/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_swa_ring) |

## Speculative decoding

The K policy and spec gate, MTP heads and chains, the Gemma assistant drafter, the DFlash and DSpark block drafters, and the restore and burst boundaries. Spec serving is on by default when the model has an MTP head or an attached drafter, and K is picked per request; an operator mostly attaches a separate drafter (`MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT` for Qwen and Step MTP heads, `MEMRA_DRAFT` for the Gemma assistant, see the trap below) and leaves the rest alone. Building and attaching a draft is in [DRAFT-REGIME.md](DRAFT-REGIME.md), and the server's spec behavior in [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#speculative-decoding-in-the-server).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT` | on | Adaptive verify width (the MTP accepted+1 recipe): the next round verifies one past this round's accepted run, clamped to [min(3, cap), cap], so a 2-row cap pins the window at 2; `0` fixes the width at `MEMRA_DFLASH_VERIFY_T`. On a head-carrying dspark checkpoint the default window policy is `confidence-slot` (`MEMRA_DSPARK_VT`); `0` counts as an explicit fixed-window request and keeps the ladder-family arm. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dflash_adapt) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_EMB_SCALE` | off (raw embed rows) | `1` feeds the DFlash drafter sqrt(n_embd)-scaled embed rows instead of raw ones. The reference does not show which form the gemma4 drafter was trained on; acceptance decides. |  |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_KV_RING` | on (`0` = rollback seam) | Live draft-KV ring: instead of allocating draft KV for the whole context cap, `1` allocates two sliding windows plus a block (4,144 rows at the q38 geometry) and compacts the trailing window to the front once per window of decode (`ring_plan`, one D2D bounce copy per layer). A pending prefix capture pins its rows until the tail exports. No numeric change: same rows, same relative positions; gated by byte identity. `0` restores the pre-lane program exactly (base 0, `phys_rows == cap + block`, no compaction). Admission still charges draft KV per context token. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dflash_kv_ring) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_PREC` | `q4` | Drafter weight precision: `q4` all q4_0 (default); `q8` all q8_0 (about 1.6 GB, the rollback seam); `mixed` bf16 attention and fc plus q8_0 ffn (about 2.2 GB); `bf16` all bf16 (parity runs, no target); `fc` bf16 `fc.weight` with q8_0 elsewhere. Unknown values, including the defective `q5`, refuse instead of falling back to q8. Boot prints `[dspark] precision=<resolved> (MEMRA_DFLASH_PREC set\|unset)`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dflash_prec) |
| `MEMRA_DFLASH_VERIFY_T` | 8 on the plain DFlash path (clamped to 2..block); `n_drafts + 1` on the DSpark paths | Verify width: the drafter always drafts the full block (its trained mask pattern), but only the first vt rows go through target verify, which keeps verify on the narrower batched tier. Exactness is unaffected: verify still decides every committed token. With `MEMRA_DFLASH_ADAPT` on it caps the adaptive window (`dflash.rs`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dflash_verify_t) |
| `MEMRA_DRAFT` | unset | Gemma assistant-drafter attach: `<assistant.gguf>` with `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC=K` drafts over the trunk's KV (`gspec`); it attaches no MTP head, and on qwen or step35 nothing at all. | `MEMRA_DSPARK_CKPT_GATE=1` | unset (off) | DSpark route only: runs both partial-accept restore arms (column stash and the snapshot-replay oracle) on every partial round and byte-compares the resulting cache state, the same-binary exactness gate for `MEMRA_DSPARK_CKPT`. | `MEMRA_DSPARK_COLD_DECODE_RATIO_DIV` | 8 | DFlash cold-prime vs prefix-hit trade: a request whose clamped decode budget is below `prompt_len / DIV` declines the cold prime, keeps the prefix probe and serves the hit plain (`[dspark] cache-preferred: prompt=... decode_budget=...`). Fires only when a whole-entry full prefix at or above `PREFIX_CACHE_MIN_TOKENS` exists; shorter prompts are exempt. A client that omits `max_tokens` gets the remaining context as its budget, so unbounded long-context traffic still cold-primes. Larger DIV cold-primes more; a very large value is the rollback seam. `0` or a non-number keeps 8. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dspark_cold_decode_ratio_div) |
| `MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC=1` + `MEMRA_DSPARK_DRAFT=<export_dir>` | unset (route absent; never auto-attached) | Arms the DFlash2 (dspark) drafter route for the qwen-hybrid trunk: one `DflashDraft` per non-gemma model, `DsparkSpecSession` bursts for cold, unconstrained, text-only sessions, greedy or sampled with penalties. Admission takes the spec-gate LOW band wave-projected; greedy sessions demote to batched plain decode at `n_live >= HIGH`, sampled never do. `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` or a `MEMRA_SPEC_K>0` pin restores solo-only admission; `MEMRA_SPEC_K=0` refuses it. Disables the MTP arm; boot refuses it with `MEMRA_SPEC_DFLASH`, `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC`, `MEMRA_PP_STAGES>1`, nonzero `MEMRA_STEP_TP` or `MEMRA_STEP_EP`, and refuses `MEMRA_DSPARK_DRAFT` without it. | [COOKBOOK.md](COOKBOOK.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dspark_draft) |

| `MEMRA_DSPARK_GREEDY_PENALTY` | on (`0` = rollback seam) | Admits temperature-0 requests carrying a non-identity repeat, frequency or presence penalty onto the DFlash route and verifies them by penalised argmax: the verify rows get the request's penalties incrementally (`penalize_logits_rows_inc`), the argmax-prefix accept walks them, and cold, restored and resumed boundary tokens take the same penalised argmax (`greedy_penalized_boundary_token`). Only requests that would cold-prime convert; the restore is byte-exact. | `MEMRA_DSPARK_VERIFY_GRAPH` | serve route ON; bin arm OFF (`=1` opts in) | Replays per-(segment, vt) CUDA graphs for the DSpark verify's linear-layer runs, plus a full-verify (vt, rung, hi) graph when a round rides one seqs rung, captured from the eager arm's own layer bodies.
