| `MEMRA_MTP_SKIP` | off (unset or `0`) | `1` skips loading the embedded MTP/NextN blocks (attention, FFN with MoE, nextn glue, per-block head) and reclaims their VRAM, for deployments that draft with dspark/DFlash2 or serve plain. | `MEMRA_SPEC_ADAPT_FLOOR` | per model: n_embd >= 3500 `4`, >= 2500 `2`, else `1`; position-keyed | `=<n>` pins the adaptive-K floor at every position. The floor clamps to K. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_adapt_floor) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST` | 32 | Tokens per spec burst: round-robin and streaming latency against per-burst fixed cost (pending flush, init feed). A sampled burst carries its pending token into the next as a greedy one does, so no solo trunk pass runs per boundary except under `MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY=0`, `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION=0` on a penalised request, or `MEMRA_SPEC_REPLAY=1`. Text flushes per round commit at any burst size, and a pending admit ends the burst at the round boundary. `128` suits throughput tiers (c>=2 batch, judge/harvest, non-streaming). Not the lever for the mixed-tick penalty: demotion (`MEMRA_SPEC_GATE`) is. | [SAMPLED-PENDING-CARRY.md](decisions/SAMPLED-PENDING-CARRY.md), [spec-sampled-pending-carry-20261110](../research/spec-sampled-pending-carry-20261110/), [spec-levers-5090-20260805](../research/spec-levers-5090-20260805/), [sse-cadence-20260805](../research/sse-cadence-20260805/), [admission-20260806](../research/admission-20260806/), [spec-gate-20260806](../research/spec-gate-20260806/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_burst) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_CAPMAX` | `7` | Adaptive-K cap ceiling on the gemma spec paths, and on the MTP path only under adapt (the fixed-K path ignores it). Above 7 opens the b16 verify tier (t=9..16), correct but measured slower than K6; the door is a measurement, not a crash. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_CAPTURE_GATE` | on (`=0` reverts) | Before a draft-graph capture allocates, the device's effective free memory (allocatable free plus async-pool cache; allocatable is the driver's reading, and on unified memory the larger of it and `MemAvailable`, memra#1221) must cover the model's measured per-session draft-state high-water plus a safety floor; otherwise the capture is refused with a loud `[spec] WARN` and the session serves eagerly with headroom intact. When the free reading alone is short but cached pool blocks would cover it, the pool is trimmed first: to zero on a discrete card, and on unified memory only the shortfall, keeping the rest of its cache (memra#1221). `=0` attempts every capture and relies on the OOM path (diagnostics); OOM recovery stays active either way. | [RESULTS.md](../research/step37-vram-admission-20260830/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_capture_gate) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_FIRST_TOKEN_EAGER` | on | The glm5 (`step_glm5_spec`) and dspark/qwen (`step_dspark_spec`) spec routes publish committed tokens at round cadence: the streamed burst twin hands the worker each committed slice as it lands (the prime's anchor alone, then each round's accepted drafts plus bonus). `0` is the rollback seam: one burst up to `MEMRA_SPEC_BURST` tokens, then emit. The token stream is byte-identical either way, greedy and sampled. A step OOM after the first flush takes the honest error, before it still parks. Read per burst. | `MEMRA_SPEC_FLOOR_CTX` | `1024` | Position boundary of the adaptive-K floor: below it the full floor applies; past it floors of 4 or more relax to 1 and milder floors persist. An explicit `MEMRA_SPEC_ADAPT_FLOOR` bypasses the key. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_floor_ctx) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE` | on, placement-aware (`0` reverts) | Placement-aware spec admission gate. On a single card or any placement but sharded PP-2 it admits spec while `active+1 <= LOW` (2) and demotes live unconstrained spec sessions, greedy and sampled, at `active >= HIGH` (4), handing `(cache, next_pred)` to batched decode after flushing carried pending; a sharded cross-device PP-2 placement defaults to LOW=0, HIGH=1 (never admit). Sampled sessions demote with plain draws from the first unused Philox event (`demoted_philox_base`); constrained sessions never demote. Greedy output is unchanged, only the scheduler phase moves. `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` is the rollback seam: spec at every concurrency and placement, no admit gate, no demotion. | [spec-gate-20260806](../research/spec-gate-20260806/), [pp2spec-crash-20260807](../research/pp2spec-crash-20260807/), [specplace-20260808](../research/specplace-20260808/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_gate) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_LOW` / `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_HIGH` | single card / non-PP-2: 2 / 4; sharded PP-2: 0 / 1 | Admit-spec ceiling and demote floor; explicit values override the placement defaults. On a single card `act == 3` is the hysteresis band, and one-way demotion bounds switches to O(load crossings). LOW=0 admits no spec session and skips the spec transient admission reserve. `HIGH <= LOW` clamps to `LOW+1` with a warning. LOW=1/HIGH=2 on PP-2 only to test a c=1-only policy. | [specplace-20260808](../research/specplace-20260808/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_gate_high) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_MODEL` | on | Cost-model spec gate. At boot each MTP-served model measures a `SpecCostTable` on the device (`[spec-cost]` line); the RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Max-Q fits each draft length its own drafting cost, other devices use one K=3 burst for every length. After 64 rounds of per-position acceptance, admission K and tick demotion pick the draft length (0 = plain) that maximizes predicted aggregate tok/s, and the gate decides sampled prefix-cache restores. The LOW/HIGH band still decides for pins, PP-2, explicit `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_LOW`/`_HIGH`, DFlash2 models and models with no verdict yet. `0` restores the band everywhere. | [spec-cost-draft-step-20261109](../research/spec-cost-draft-step-20261109/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_gate_model) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_GRAPH_FILTERED` | on (`=0` reverts) | Sampled draft-graph capture covers truncation-filtered requests (top_k/top_p/min_p): the filter runs in-graph (`filter_stats` + `gumbel_perturb_filtered_ctr`, stats stay on device), with seed, temperature and filters keyed in `SampledGraphKey`, so a regime change drops the parked graph. `=0` drafts filtered requests eagerly and captures pure temperature only. The pure-temperature capture body is byte-identical either way. | [RESULTS.md](../research/step37-draft-graph-serving-20260830/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_graph_filtered) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HPOST` | off | `1` (any value but `0`) feeds the MTP head the post-`output_norm` hidden instead of the pre-norm one. An acceptance lever chosen per model. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_K` | serve: automatic per-request table; `run-spec`: K=1..8 battery | Operator pin and rollback seam for draft depth. A non-negative value pins K for every eligible request (`0` = plain decode) and disables the concurrency gate and automatic demotion; an invalid value warns once and keeps the automatic policy. Unset, serving picks per request: K=0 on losing placement or concurrency cells, cached-long K=2 (5 with a rank-trimmed head) for a greedy request with 1024+ prompt and resumed tokens, cold-long K=2 on the RTX 5090 Laptop GPU and RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Max-Q, K=3 otherwise; the GLM5 route on `100a` builds answers cold-short with K=6. The verify knee clamps afterwards. `run_hy3_local_5090.sh` also accepts `all` for the K=1..8 battery in one load. | [kpolicy-20260808](../research/kpolicy-20260808/), [orndecode-20260822](../research/orndecode-20260822/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_k) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_K_CACHED_LONG` | unset (= 2), decide-by: 2026-11-16 | Override for the cached-long rung (prompt and cache >= 1024 tokens, full-vocab MTP head). Greedy requests only: a sampled request reads no cached length and takes its cold rung. Positive integer; `0` or garbage keeps the shipped rung and warns once (`[spec-k] WARN`). A rung override keeps the concurrency gate, unlike `MEMRA_SPEC_K`; the boot `[spec-k] automatic table` line states the resolved rungs. Unset = the shipped table on every device. | [kpolicy-20260808](../research/kpolicy-20260808/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_k_cached_long) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_K_CACHED_LONG_TRIM` | unset (= 5), decide-by: 2026-11-16 | Override for the cached-long rung when the loaded MTP head is FR-Spec rank-trimmed (prompt and cache >= 1024 tokens). Greedy requests only: a sampled request takes its cold rung. Positive integer; `0` or garbage keeps the shipped rung and warns once (`[spec-k] WARN`). A rung override keeps the concurrency gate, unlike `MEMRA_SPEC_K`; the boot `[spec-k] automatic table` line states the resolved rungs. Unset = the shipped table on every device. | [gb10-tuning-20261102](../research/gb10-tuning-20261102/), [orndecode-20260822](../research/orndecode-20260822/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_k_cached_long_trim) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_K_COLD_LONG` | unset: 2 on the RTX 5090 Laptop GPU and RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell Max-Q, 3 elsewhere; decide-by: 2026-11-23 | Override for the cold-long rung (prompt >= 1024 tokens, cache < 1024). The shipped rung is keyed by device name, one per device and not per model or head: K=2 on the two cards whose cell has it ahead, K=3 on GB10 (its own cell has K=3 ahead) and on every card without a cell. A rank-trimmed MTP head (`MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM`) and the GLM5 route under an operator gate override take it without a cell of their own. `MEMRA_SPEC_K_COLD_LONG=3` is the rollback. Positive integer; `0` or garbage keeps the shipped rung and warns once. A rung override keeps the concurrency gate, unlike `MEMRA_SPEC_K`; the boot `[spec-k] automatic table` line states the resolved rungs. | [SPEC-K-COLD-LONG-K2.md](decisions/SPEC-K-COLD-LONG-K2.md), [spec-k-cold-long-20261109](../research/spec-k-cold-long-20261109/), [spec-k-cold-long-c2-20261109](../research/spec-k-cold-long-c2-20261109/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_k_cold_long) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_K_COLD_SHORT` | unset (= 3), decide-by: 2026-11-16 | Override for the automatic table's cold-short rung (prompt < 1024 tokens). Positive integer; `0` or garbage keeps the shipped rung and warns once (`[spec-k] WARN`). Unlike `MEMRA_SPEC_K`, a rung override keeps the concurrency gate: past `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE_LOW` the request still demotes to plain batching. The boot `[spec-k] automatic table` line states the resolved rungs and marks an override. Unset = the shipped table on every device. | [SPEC-K-COLD-SHORT-5090.md](decisions/SPEC-K-COLD-SHORT-5090.md), [spec-k-short-5090-20261110](../research/spec-k-short-5090-20261110/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_k_cold_short) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_LEAN` | on (`=0` reverts) | Lean verify at t=1: no zeroed verify buffers and no rows dispatch, so an m=1 verify costs the same as an eager decode. `=0` restores the zeroed buffers and the rows dispatch. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_NOGRAPH` | unset (CUDA-graph draft) | Set (any value): eager draft chain, no CUDA-graph draft. The runtime picks this arm itself for non-resident MoE MTP heads and sigmoid-router MoE MTP heads such as Hy3, whose selected-expert path is host-visible and cannot be stream-captured; startup logs `[spec] draft graph unavailable: ...; eager draft chain engaged`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_nograph) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_NOREFRESH` | unset (true-hidden refresh) | Set (any value): draft-KV entries stay chain-approximate instead of being refreshed from the verify's exact hiddens. Multi-head MTP refuses it, since it needs the exact accepted-prefix refresh. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION` | on | Default ON seeds each spec burst's penalty window from `committed ++ prompt` (the last `max(penalty_last_n, 64)` tokens) and counts the burst's boundary token, as the plain sampler's history does, so `repetition_penalty`, `frequency_penalty` and `presence_penalty` hold across burst boundaries and restored sessions. `0` restarts the window from each burst's own `prompt` slice, breaks the MTP route's seeded-text invariance for a penalised request, keeps the solo commit pass at each penalised sampled tail, and brings back `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED`'s penalized-restore refusal, which names this flag. Rollback seam. | [SAMPLED-PENDING-CARRY.md](decisions/SAMPLED-PENDING-CARRY.md), [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#measured-numbers-qwen35-9b-q8_0-receipts-in-research), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_pen_session) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN` | `0.0` (no gate) | p-min confidence gate: the draft chain stops when a slot's confidence falls below the value. The statistic is draw-independent, the slot distribution's max probability (`memra_sampling::spec_stop::row_confidence`, device `max_prob_device`), never the drawn token's, so sampled output keeps the target law and greedy picks are unchanged (`cargo test -p memra-sampling --lib spec_stop`). The glm5 loop gates its native chain on the head's softmax max and cuts a DFlash2 proposal at the first slot under p_min, printing `[glm5-spec] draft confidence gate armed: PMIN=..`. The value is per model and K, unmeasured on GLM5; a sampled PMIN deployment is requalified before promotion. | [spec-levers-5090-20260805](../research/spec-levers-5090-20260805/), [q27-deepdive-p2-20260805](../research/q27-deepdive-p2-20260805/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_pmin) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN0` | off | `1` lets the p-min gate act on slot 0 too, giving zero-draft rounds; it matters only with `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN` set. On the sampled MTP and GLM paths slot 0 reads the same draw-independent row max as every other slot, so a zero-draft round is a plain target sample and the emitted law is exact (`memra_sampling::spec_stop`, PMIN0 arm). On glm5 a zero-draft round verifies the anchor row alone, a plain decode step. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_pmin0) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PMIN_INROUND` | self-keyed cut at `0.7` | Gemma in-round draft-confidence cut, eager arm only. Unset: cut at 0.7, active only at positions past the floor context and in rounds after a miss; the chain stops early and verify runs at the shrunk width, at one small DtoH sync per draft step. `=<p>` pins the cut at every position and round; `0` disables it. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_pmin_inround) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_REPLAY` | unset (replay-free partial accept) | Only a literal `=1` enables the legacy rollback-and-replay partial accept, which also remains the j==0 fallback; unset, empty, `0` and any other value keep the replay-free default. Q35-MoE's architecture-local exactness replay is independent of this flag. | [RESULTS.md](../research/spec-replay-flag-20260814/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_replay) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_LOAD_GUARD` | on | Default ON gates a sampled prefix-cache restore into spec on load. When the spec cost model owns the gate, the restore is admitted exactly when the model would speculate at that demand; otherwise demand (`max(worker-visible, HTTP in-flight)`, read at the refusal site) must be at most the SOLO watermark `min(LOW, 1)`, and a refusal prints `[spec-restore-guard] sampled restore REFUSED: ...`. Greedy restores are not guarded. `0` re-arms spec on every sampled restore regardless of load. A `MEMRA_SPEC_K` pin or `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE=0` bypasses it. Gate: `tools/spec-cache-gate.sh` with `MEMRA_CACHEGATE_TEMP>0`. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#measured-numbers-qwen35-9b-q8_0-receipts-in-research), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_restore_load_guard) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_REPUBLISH` | on | Default ON: a spec session restored from a prefix-cache hit captures its own prompt-end boundary after it feeds the suffix, and the worker's publication sweep publishes it, so each later turn of a growing conversation hits a longer prefix. Whole-entry only (`entry_pos == fed_len`), so the mid-entry partial-restore class stays closed. Costs one `Cache::snapshot` and one cache entry per restored session with a suffix; a failed snapshot publishes nothing, silently. `0` publishes nothing from a restored session. Gate: the growth cells of `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh`. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#measured-numbers-qwen35-9b-q8_0-receipts-in-research), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_restore_republish) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_RESTORE_SAMPLED` | ON | On, a whole-entry prefix-cache hit re-arms a warm spec session for sampled requests too, not only greedy. The restored session's seed rule and Philox counters ((0,0) at admit) equal a cold session's, so per seed a full-cover sampled hit's bytes and accepted/drafted counts equal the cold leader's; penalized sampled restores rely on `MEMRA_SPEC_PEN_SESSION`. `0` serves sampled hits on the plain path and leaves greedy restores untouched; it is the rollback seam. Gate: `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh` sampled cells; its `MEMRA_HITGATE_TEETH=1` arm checks that `0` holds sampled hits plain. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_restore_sampled) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_SAMPLED_BOUNDARY` | ON | On, a sampled spec burst draws its boundary token (its first emitted token, taken from an existing logits row: the prime's last row, the row after the last commit, or a converted hit's boundary row) from the request's filtered and penalized target on the session's own Philox stream, and a burst ending on a pending token carries it into the next burst's round 0. `0` takes the argmax at each boundary (greedy is unaffected either way), commits the pending token with a solo trunk pass, and breaks the MTP route's position key, so seeded text depends on where bursts begin. Oracles: `sample_check` arm 9 and `tools/spec-on-cache-hit-gate.sh`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_sampled_boundary) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_GRAPH` | on for the GatedDeltaNet + routed-MoE family, off for others | Arms the slice-4c verify capture for the MTP spec round (`qwen35_verify_tparallel`), replaying the host-launch-bound verify walk as a graph. Same kernels in the same order, so output is byte-identical. `=1` opts other families in (Qwen3.8-27B, GDN plus dense MLP, needs its own interleave first), `=0` is the kill switch to the eager walk. Declines the pool, riding the eager walk, when a round is wider than the pool, below `GRAPH_LAUNCH_MIN_FREE` (`[spec] graph replay suspended:`), or for a non-resident or host-routed MoE MTP head, even with `=1` (`[spec-vg] MTP verify-graph pool declined (<reason>)`). Read once per process. | [VGRAPH.md](../research/orndecode-20260822/VGRAPH.md), [RESULTS.md](../research/mtp-head-nonresident-capture-20260908/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_verify_graph) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_VERIFY_ROWWISE=1` | unset (t-parallel for the qwen35 batched-serving class at t<=16) | `1` forces Qwen35-class spec verify (dense and MoE) onto the per-row correctness reference (T full weight reads per layer) instead of the t-parallel arm: the exactness oracle and rollback seam. Archs outside the class and t>16 take rowwise anyway (`spec.rs`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_verify_rowwise) |
| `MEMRA_STATE_COPY_BATCH` | on (`=0` reverts); no code read | DSpark verify round: the per-layer GDN snap/commit state copies run as batched pointer-table launches; `=0` was the eager per-buffer dispatch loop. Launch structure only, bytes unchanged. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_state_copy_batch) |
| `MEMRA_VERIFY_WS` | on; `0` on this name or `MEMRA_GLM5_VERIFY_WS` turns it off | Verify-walk allocation workspace: size-keyed device-buffer free-lists pooled per Engine (`VerifyWs`, per stream), capped at 16 buffers per size class and 256 MiB total, overflow to the ordinary async free. Wired only at verify-walk sites: the rows-exact matmul output, the KDA rows-arm scratch and the previous round's KDA stash, and the MoE vrows staging. A pooled buffer keeps the full-overwrite-before-read contract, so reuse moves no bits. Off-wins with the alias `MEMRA_GLM5_VERIFY_WS`. `0` restores the shipped alloc/free program, read per call. Announces `[glm5-verify-ws] engaged` at the first pool hit. Gate: `glm5_spec_session_gpu::gpu_verify_ws_bursts_byte_identical_with_alloc_receipt`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/matvec-20260831/LANE.md), [mv-battery-20260831](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/mv-battery-20260831/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_verify_ws) |

### SEAM TRAP: `MEMRA_DRAFT` is the GEMMA drafter. `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT` is the QWEN one.

`MEMRA_DRAFT` attaches the Gemma assistant drafter and `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT` a Qwen or step35 MTP head from a separate GGUF: for a Qwen or step35 drafter set `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT=<draft.gguf>`, for the Gemma-4 assistant set `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC=K` with `MEMRA_DRAFT=<assistant.gguf>`. The wrong one does not error, the run passes without the drafter you meant, and `MEMRA_DRAFT` on a non-Gemma run still turns off the `wkv_on()` and `fa_f16pv_on()` defaults and changes the MMQ-SK form. Prove the attach from the server log (`[mtp-draft]` or `regime draft attached`), never from `usage.spec.accepted > 0`, which a Qwen trunk's embedded MTP head also produces; gates fail a run without that line (`tools/assert-drafter-attached.sh`).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| gemma-4 assistant drafter over trunk KV | unset | Set `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC=K` plus `MEMRA_DRAFT=<assistant.gguf>`; no `MEMRA_MODELS` equivalent. Proof: the gemma draft-attach line at spawn. An explicit `MEMRA_GEMMA4_SPEC` depth without `MEMRA_DRAFT` refuses the boot. |  |
| qwen / step35 MTP head from a separate GGUF (e.g. the FR-Spec drafter) | unset | Set `MEMRA_MTP_DRAFT=<draft.gguf>`; the equivalent is `MEMRA_MODELS="name=<trunk>+<draft>"`. Proof in the server log: `[mtp-draft] loading external MTP draft: ...` and `[worker] <name>: regime draft attached (...)`. Without those lines the drafter never loaded. |  |

## Sampling and constrained output

Sampler defaults for the command-line bins (`MEMRA_TEMP`, `MEMRA_SPEC_TEMP`, `MEMRA_SEED`, the filters and the penalties), the server's device penalty path, the DSV4 sampler's sort order and constrained decoding. The server never takes the bin sampler rows: each request carries its own temperature, filters, penalties and seed. How `response_format` decoding works is in [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#constrained-decoding-response_format).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_CONSTRAIN_HOST` | off | `1` keeps constrained (`response_format`) sessions on the v1 host mask path: full-row logits D2H, host -inf mask and host O(n_vocab) sample, with no graph promotion and no spec. The default uploads the packed llguidance bitset each step into a per-session device buffer and bans on device (`mask_logits_f32`) before the same device-sample, lean-logits, graph and spec paths unconstrained sessions use. Oracle and A/B seam only. | [constrained-20260803](../research/constrained-20260803/), [constrained-full-20260803](../research/constrained-full-20260803/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_constrain_host) |
| `MEMRA_TEMP` / `MEMRA_TOP_K` / `MEMRA_TOP_P` / `MEMRA_MIN_P` / `MEMRA_SEED` (safetensors path) | greedy (`MEMRA_TEMP` 0; top_k 0, top_p 1.0, min_p 0, seed 7) | On `run-gen`'s safetensors text path, `MEMRA_TEMP>0` arms the serving device sampler inside the async chain (`filter_stats` -> `gumbel_perturb_filtered_col` -> argmax into resident `token_d`), so a sampled stream keeps the no-host-sync chain and each token gets its own draw from the per-step counter; `MEMRA_TOP_K`, `MEMRA_TOP_P`, `MEMRA_MIN_P` and `MEMRA_SEED` shape that sampler. With `MEMRA_HEAD_SPLIT=1` sampling draws from the split path's materialized row. The bench line names the shape it measured. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_min_p) |
| `MEMRA_TOP_K` / `MEMRA_TOP_P` / `MEMRA_MIN_P` | off: top-k 0, top-p 1.0, min-p 0.0 | Sampling filters for run-gen and the spec CLI sampler (`SpecSampling::from_cli_env`); they act only once a temperature above 0 arms sampling. The server takes each request's filters explicitly. |  |
| `MEMRA_PENALTY_REPEAT` / `_FREQ` / `_PRESENT` / `_LAST_N` | off: repeat 1.0, freq 0.0, present 0.0, last-n 0 | Repetition, frequency and presence penalties and their look-back window for run-gen and the spec CLI sampler (`SpecSampling::from_cli_env`). The server takes each request's penalties explicitly. |  |
| `MEMRA_POSTTHINK_CEILING` | `0` (off) | `<tokens>` is a forced-close guard for post-think constrained sessions (`response_format` on think-forced templates, where think runs unconstrained with every end-of-generation id banned and the grammar engages at the think-close sequence). Past this many think tokens the phase-1 mask collapses to the next close token, so the close is forced and the grammar engages; same mask seam on the host, batched-device and graph paths. Operator opt-in for guaranteed-JSON deployments: pair it with a bounded schema and a value at or above the model's natural think length; otherwise raise the request's `max_tokens`. A non-integer value warns and stays off. Runtime parameter. | [step37-postthink-grammar-20260830](../research/step37-postthink-grammar-20260830/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_postthink_ceiling) |
| `MEMRA_SEED` | `0` (run-gen host sampler, spec CLI sampler); `7` on run-gen's device-sampled text path | Sampler seed for the bins: run-gen's host sampler and the spec CLI sampler (`SpecSampling::from_cli_env`: run-spec and the session bins) fall back to 0, run-gen's device-sampled text path to 7. The server takes each request's seed explicitly. |  |
| `MEMRA_SERVE_DEVPENALTY` | ON: dense `Qwen35` on RTX 5090, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell and GB10, `Qwen35Moe` on GB10; OFF elsewhere | `1` moves sampled requests with repetition, frequency or presence penalties onto the device: `Sampler::accept` keeps exact sparse counts over the window, and the batched epilogue uploads unique `(token,count)` pairs and applies every row's coefficients in one ragged launch before the filter and Gumbel draw. Raw logits are copied before mutation and are the only bytes parked, so a resumed request cannot double-penalize. Greedy penalties and constrained composition stay on the host. `1` and `0` force either path on every pair; `0` is the rollback seam. Unset, it resolves per session at admit from the model plan and card class (`HardwareTarget::from_device_name`), not the build arch. | [GB10-DEVICE-PENALTY-DEFAULT.md](decisions/GB10-DEVICE-PENALTY-DEFAULT.md), [devpenalty-qwen35-20260909](../research/devpenalty-qwen35-20260909/), [pro-device-penalty-sampling-20260824](../research/pro-device-penalty-sampling-20260824/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_serve_devpenalty) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_TEMP` | greedy (0.0) | Speculative sampling temperature for the bin entry points (`generate_spec`, `generate_spec_session` through `SpecSampling::from_cli_env`), not `MEMRA_TEMP`; the server passes each request's sampler, so a temperature-0 request stays greedy whatever this says. Above 0 the round runs the position-keyed sampled walk: the token at position p is the plain sampler's draw at Philox event p and a draft is accepted when it equals that draw, so a seeded request writes one text at every K. DFlash2, GLM5, DSpark and gemma assistant routes still verify by modified rejection sampling. Takes `MEMRA_SEED` and the `MEMRA_PENALTY_*` set. Gates: the `run-spec` seeded rerun and `tools/seeded-invariance-gate.py`. | [SAMPLED-VERIFY-POSITION-KEYED.md](decisions/SAMPLED-VERIFY-POSITION-KEYED.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_temp) |
| `MEMRA_TEMP` | unset: greedy (0.0) | run-gen temperature. Above 0 it enables the sampling chain (top-k, top-p, min-p, penalties), drawn on device on the text path. The spec path's temperature is `MEMRA_SPEC_TEMP`, not this one. |  |

## Loading, memory budgets and spill

Checkpoint loaders and pre-tokenizer checks, VRAM and pinned-host budgets, MoE expert residency and caching, CPU experts and disk spill. Each budget has a default or is derived at load; an operator touches the MoE residency rows (`MEMRA_MOE_VRAM_FRAC`, `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_GB`), `MEMRA_MODELS_DIR` for `hf:` specs, and the spill rows when a model does not fit. Running a model past VRAM and host RAM is in [HY3-SPILL.md](HY3-SPILL.md), and per-model launch lines are in the [cookbook](COOKBOOK.md).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| CPU expert cache eviction | LRU | Fixed policy with no switch: the CPU expert projection cache evicts the least recently used entry. `MEMRA_MOE_LFU` acts on the GPU SLRU cache, not this one. |  |
| `MEMRA_ALLOW_UNKNOWN_PRETOKENIZER=1` | unset (an unknown pre-tokenizer is a hard load error) | `1` downgrades the unknown-pre-tokenizer load error to a loud per-load WARN and runs the `qwen35` split anyway, which produces wrong token ids by construction. Supported: `qwen35`, `qwen2`, `deepseek-v3`, `gemma4` (its absent `pre` key allowlisted on a tensor census) and `glm4` (recognised from the tokenizer.json Split regex). Set it only for deliberate bring-up on an unported family, never for a run whose numbers are quoted (`memra-tokenizer/src/lib.rs`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_allow_unknown_pretokenizer) |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_BATCHED_PRIME` | off | Rollback seam: `1` restores batched prompt replay after Hy3 CPU/GPU expert residency freezes. Otherwise, with the CPU companion configured and the cache frozen, prompts replay tokenwise so a prime does not transiently reread the missing expert bank. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_GB` | `16` | Requested normal-RAM ceiling in GiB (`0..=1024`) for CPU-routed expert projection bytes, capped to `MemAvailable - MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_RESERVE_GB` when the companion initializes. It also sizes the `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_SHM` segment. `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` requests 20. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_SHM` | off | `1` backs the CPU expert cache with a named tmpfs segment and keeps its index across restarts, so a clean reopen starts warm. The object is created atomically with mode `0600`; an existing one is adopted only when owned by the effective uid with mode exactly `0600`, otherwise it is refused and the process uses its private cache. An exclusive flock sends a second process to the same private fallback. A crash leaves a dirty header and a cold, correct start; keys pin device, inode, size and ctime, and reload verifies a checksum. tmpfs THP needs `shmem_enabled=advise`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_cpu_expert_cache_shm) |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_SHM_NAME` | `/memra-expert-cache-v1` | POSIX shm name of the persistent cache under `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_SHM=1`. A pre-existing name is used only when its object is owned by the effective uid with mode exactly `0600`; unsafe adoption is refused, not repaired. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_EXECUTOR_CPUSETS` | unset (inherit) | `0-7;8-15` pins each CPU-expert executor to its own core group and sizes its OMP team to the group (`MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_EXECUTOR_THREADS=8;8` overrides per group); the group list sets the executor count. Built for hybrid P/E silicon: one team per group, so a slow group never stalls a fast group's barrier. Use fewer executors than in-flight calls. Requires `GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY` unset and a process taskset covering the groups. Helps only when several expert calls are in flight. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_cpu_expert_executor_cpusets) |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_EXECUTORS` | `1` | CPU-expert executor count without explicit cpusets (`1..=8`); an out-of-range or unparseable value keeps 1. Ignored when `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_EXECUTOR_CPUSETS` is set: the group list sets the count. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_CACHE` | off | `1` freezes HBM expert residency so the CPU/GPU backend assignment cannot drift after warmup; required for exact, repeatable Hy3 hybrid runs. Without `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_WARMUP_TOKENS` the freeze happens at once. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_PROFILE` | unset (off) | Path to a freeze-profile sidecar. A warmup run saves the frozen residency set (a versioned header binds slot geometry); a later run restages it and skips the profiling warmup. A mismatched or stale profile falls back to a normal warmup; the post-freeze argmax gate checks the assignment either way. `run-lockstep` requires it. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_cpu_expert_freeze_profile) |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_PROFILE_ADMIT` | off | `1` lets CPU-routed warmup misses vote for HBM admission before the residency set freezes; the current token still executes on CPU. `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` sets it. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_WARMUP_TOKENS` | unset (`0`, no warmup) | Discarded tokens `run-gen` and `run-spec` use to profile and fill HBM before freezing residency; `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_WARMUP_SPEC_K` selects the speculative warmup depth in run-spec. A saved `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_FREEZE_PROFILE` skips the warmup. `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` uses 128. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_cpu_expert_freeze_warmup_tokens) |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_IO` | `buffered` | CPU expert reads: `buffered` or aligned `direct` positioned I/O. Any other value is an error. `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` sets `direct`. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_IO_CPUSET` | unset (inherit) | Optional CPU list (for example `8-15`) that pins the companion's async read pool off the compute cores. Unset inherits the process affinity mask. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_IO_THREADS` | unset (compute thread count) | Positioned-read workers of the CPU expert backend (`1..=256`). Unset uses the compute thread count; an invalid value is an error. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_LIB` | unset (off) | Path to a trusted build of memra's optional Hy3-only native CPU-expert companion (ABI v2, no external inference runtime), built by `tools/build_cpu_expert_companion.sh`. `dlopen` runs constructors before the ABI check, so point it only at a trusted file. Complete frozen-cache hits stay on CUDA; an expert missing any projection runs wholly on CPU. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_MIRROR_MAP` | unset (off) | Path to a tab-separated inode-to-alternate-path map that splits large direct reads across byte-identical expert mirrors on two NVMe devices. A path that cannot be opened is an error. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_PIPELINE` | off | `1` overlaps companion reads with compute: cached experts compute at once, missing ones in read-completion order, output byte-identical. Requires `OMP_WAIT_POLICY=ACTIVE`: with passive waits decode compute inflates sharply, and with active waits end-to-end is flat against serial. Stays a door until a single-region fix removes the wait-policy dependence. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_cpu_expert_pipeline) |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_RESERVE_GB` | `4` | Live-stack RAM headroom in GiB (`0..=1024`): when the CPU companion initializes it caps `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_CACHE_GB` to `MemAvailable - reserve` and prints the effective budget. An invalid value is an error. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_EXPERT_THREADS` | `8` | OpenMP compute threads for one-token CPU expert work (`1..=256`); a non-integer or out-of-range value refuses. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_cpu_expert_threads) |
| `MEMRA_FP8_BLK_GPU` | off | `MEMRA_FP8_BLK_GPU=1` dequantizes block-128 FP8 weights to Q8_0 on the GPU at load (`cu/fp8_blk_dequant.cu`) instead of host dequant plus host re-encode. Output is byte-identical to the CPU path (kernel-check `fp8-blk-gpu`) and each block keeps its own scale, so decode and dispatch do not change: only load wall drops. It acts only where a block-128 tensor still takes the Q8_0 slab (the native `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` arm declined it). Opt-in; the CPU path stays the default. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fp8_blk_gpu) |
| `MEMRA_FULL_PREC=1` | OFF | Full-precision loader mode for the exactness ceiling. `1` skips the BF16/F8 to Q8_0/NVFP4 re-encodes and the Float-poison tripwire (its warnings are suppressed): everything loads Float, and large 2D bf16 matmul weights stay bf16-resident (`GpuTensor::FloatBf16`) with dequant-on-use, so a 9B fits 24 GB. Compute runs the f32 oracle path (`MEMRA_FAST=0` class). It forces the eager spec draft (graph capture cannot enclose the f32 GEMV or the dequant alloc) and disables `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM`. Slow by design. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_full_prec) |
| `MEMRA_LOCKSTEP_CPU_ROWS=1` | unset (off); decide-by: 2026-10-05 | Engine door on the lockstep MoE path only: `1` puts the CPU experts on the companion's multi-row program (one raw rows call per CPU expert across the streams that routed to it) with the exact per-row re-fold (`fma(y, w * down_scale, sum)` in selection order), bit-identical to M=1 like the default one-job-per-row program (proof: `cpu-native-check` raw arm). Off because the A/B was inconclusive across hosts; missing gate: an N>=5 A/B on a 9950X-class host. | [lockstep-cpu-rows-exact-20260921](../research/lockstep-cpu-rows-exact-20260921/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_lockstep_cpu_rows) |
| `MEMRA_MODELS_DIR` | `~/.cache/memra/models` | Where `hf:owner/repo[:file]` specs download to and are looked up in (`hf/<owner>--<repo>/`); `frspec-owngen` also puts datasets under `hf-datasets/`. The one knob for putting weights on another filesystem: point it at a fast local disk rather than symlinking a cache. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_models_dir) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_CACHE` | on (`=0` reverts) | SLRU residency cache for host-resident MoE experts; with it on, host expert bytes are also pinned. `=0` stages every token's experts with no cache, from paged host memory unless `MEMRA_MOE_PINNED` is set. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_HARD_VRAM_FRAC` | `0.80` | Machine hard ceiling for the SLRU slot bank as a fraction of free VRAM (`0.10..=0.95`). An out-of-range or non-numeric value warns and uses 0.80. Raise it only after an OOM-gated sweep on the card; `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` sets `0.90`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_hard_vram_frac) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_HOST_PINNED` | 1 (pinned host expert bank) | `=0` keeps the gathered MoE expert bank in paged host memory instead of a `cudaHostAlloc` pin of every layer's bank (4 GB per layer on GLM-5.3-Flash, seconds per layer on the load thread). A bank the resident decision uploads once never stages from host again and does not need the pin. Only the safetensors in-RAM gather reads it; the pin also needs `MEMRA_MOE_PINNED` or the MoE cache on. Rollback seam: unset. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_host_pinned) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_LFU` | off | `1` turns on frequency-aware eviction in the GPU SLRU expert cache. Separate from the fixed LRU policy of the CPU expert projection cache. `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` sets it. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_LFU_DECAY` | unset (no decay) | Finite `(0,1]` per-epoch decay for GPU-cache frequency scores under `MEMRA_MOE_LFU=1`; `1.0` keeps counts. Setting it at all turns on forward-epoch accounting. An invalid value warns and disables decay. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_LFU_MTP_WEIGHT` | `1.0` | Finite `0.25..=64` multiplier on GPU-cache frequency updates from MTP and verify epochs. An invalid value warns and uses 1.0. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_MMAP_ADVICE` | `random` | Whole-map madvise for mmap-backed expert data: `random` (default) or `normal`, which restores ordinary Linux readahead. Invalid values warn and fall back to `random`. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_PAGE_PREFETCH` | unset (off) | `=1` issues rolling `MADV_WILLNEED` for the coming experts' mmap-backed GGUF or repack ranges, `MEMRA_MOE_PAGE_PREFETCH_WINDOW` experts ahead; only `1` engages. Experimental: a cold-cache A/B on an RTX PRO 6000 and an RTX 5090 comes before any default. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_PAGE_PREFETCH_WINDOW` | `1` | How many future experts the rolling page-prefetch window keeps; `0` disables the prefetch and unparsable values read as `1`. Read only when `MEMRA_MOE_PAGE_PREFETCH=1`; size it to storage latency and page-cache budget. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_PINNED` | pinned whenever `MEMRA_MOE_CACHE` is not `0` | Set to any value to force pinned host expert slabs even with `MEMRA_MOE_CACHE=0`. Unset, host expert slabs are pinned unless `MEMRA_MOE_CACHE=0`; on the safetensors NVFP4 gather path `MEMRA_MOE_HOST_PINNED=0` keeps the bank paged either way. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_PREWARM=0` | on (one-shot layer prewarm) | `=0` leaves expert SLRU residency organic. On by default: one shot per layer, every block is force-admitted while free slots cover the whole layer; it never evicts. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT` | on (resident if experts fit) | `0` forces the SLRU expert path even when the experts fit VRAM; otherwise each device takes the resident-if-fits decision of `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_GB`. The placement does not change a softmax-routed MoE's prefill program: the SLRU placement runs the same pairs arm (grouped f16 lane on sm_120a) from cache slots. | [MOE-PREFILL-PLACEMENT-INDEPENDENT.md](decisions/MOE-PREFILL-PLACEMENT-INDEPENDENT.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_resident) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_GB` | unset: free minus trunk minus `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_HEADROOM_GB` | Absolute GB budget for resident experts. Unset, each device keeps its expert banks resident if their exact bytes (GGUF header, or the safetensors census) fit free VRAM minus the trunk it still uploads minus the headroom; on unified memory (GB10) free is the allocatable reading, so every bank that fits system memory stays resident. Trunk bytes count each non-expert tensor at its post-conversion device size; a device reserves only its own remaining layers, plus the embedding and globals on the primary. Under PP the expert numerator follows the stage-to-device placement. The vision tower is not reserved and must fit in the headroom. | [residency-cap-20260802](../research/residency-cap-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_resident_gb) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_RESIDENT_HEADROOM_GB` | `2.0` | Headroom in GB the resident-expert decision reserves beside weights: CUDA context, KV, workspace, and a vision tower the server loads after the model. Raise it on rigs co-running other GPU work or holding many long-context sessions. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_resident_headroom_gb) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_SIZE_AWARE` | off | `1` partitions SLRU slots by expert projection size, so mixed-size tiers (Hy3) cannot strand capacity. Ignored when `MEMRA_MOE_SLOTS` forces a count. `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` sets it. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_SLAB_POPULATE` | `fits` | Page-cache population of `.memra-repack` expert slabs at load. `fits` reads each slab file sequentially when that leaves at least 20% of MemTotal available, so serving reads experts from page cache; a slab that fails the floor (larger than RAM) keeps demand faulting. `off` keeps the pure demand-fault tier (`MADV_RANDOM`, page by page). Invalid values warn and use `fits`. Not applied to the GGUF spill tier. | [glm53-flash-bringup-20260827](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_slab_populate) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_SLOTS` | unset (auto) | Forces the SLRU slot count; unset or empty is the auto size from `MEMRA_MOE_VRAM_FRAC`. A value that is not a whole count refuses the load. A count above the hard ceiling (`MEMRA_MOE_HARD_VRAM_FRAC`) or below the eight-slot floor is clamped, and `[moe-cache] MEMRA_MOE_SLOTS=N gives M slots` names both counts. A forced count turns `MEMRA_MOE_SIZE_AWARE` off. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_slots) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_VRAM_FRAC` | `0.85` | Share of free VRAM the SLRU expert cache requests when `MEMRA_MOE_SLOTS` is unset; the `MEMRA_MOE_HARD_VRAM_FRAC` ceiling still bounds the allocation. Lower it on rigs co-running other GPU work; `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` sets `0.90`. A value that is not a fraction in (0, 1] refuses the load. |  |
| `MEMRA_PP_F16_BUDGET_MB` | `32768` | VRAM cap in MiB for the f16 prefill mirrors, per engine (each card of a PP split has its own cap; models on one device share it), filled in layer order. A dropped mirror refunds its bytes, and the first skip announces once per device (`[f16-mirror] budget reached`). Admission runs Q6_K, then Q4_K, then Q5_K, so full Q6_K coverage is the floor. Machine-specific config: on 80 GB cards the mirrors and the KQRP decode mirrors compete for the same VRAM. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_f16_budget_mb) |
| `MEMRA_PP_FP8_BUDGET_MB` | `1536` | VRAM budget in MiB for the duplicate resident e4m3 copies the `MEMRA_PP_FP8` and `MEMRA_FP8_MMQ=1` stash makes. Load order is layer order, so the budget covers a prefix of layers; once spent, later tensors take the old path. Size it to the card. Native e4m3 residency under the default `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` keeps one copy and needs no budget. A value that does not parse uses 1536. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_fp8_budget_mb) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_SLABS=0` | on (resident per-device prime slab pool) | `=0` makes the eager prime allocate its trunk transients per call instead of from the resident per-device slab pool. It also disables the qwen prime graph, which needs the slabs. |  |
| `MEMRA_REPACK_EXTERNAL_ROOTS` | unset (artifact root only) | Machine-specific storage seam for deliberate dual-NVMe symlink views. Unset, repack shards must resolve beneath the artifact directory; a path list admits descriptor-verified external roots. Both arms still require regular single-link files, and unauthorized escapes fail closed. Unsetting it rolls back. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_repack_external_roots) |
| `MEMRA_REWRITE_BUNDLE` | unset (the native policy, no rewrite bundle) | Path to a sealed `memra model inspect`/`verify` bundle. At model load the worker checks the artifact lock, compiled-plan hash, rewrite manifest and every receipt's artifact and plan binding before admitting an optimized rewrite; an invalid bundle fails the load. Batch, graph and prime surfaces without a receipt may use the bundle's native-eager rewrite; required speculative and pipeline selections fail closed. A process where no route consumes the bundle (DSv4-only) refuses to boot before any weight loads, naming the route. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_rewrite_bundle) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_HOST_EMBD` | off | `1` keeps the full token-embedding table in host memory and uploads only the rows each MTP or verify step needs: exact (the same source bits expanded to f32), with O(T x n_embd) bytes crossing PCIe per step. A capacity seam for very large BF16 vocab tables such as the Hy3 spill profile; CUDA-graph draft paths stay off under it. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_DISK` | unset (off) | Set to any value to turn on the NVMe disk tier for MoE experts, for checkpoints whose experts exceed host RAM: experts pin until the `MEMRA_SPILL_PINNED_FRAC` budget is spent, then later experts spill to one shared mmap per shard. Unset keeps the all-host path. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_IO` | `mmap` | Expert storage backend of the spill path: `mmap` (default), `pread` (blocking positioned-read oracle), `worker` (bounded CPU positioned-read prefetch) or `direct` (the worker pipeline through O_DIRECT; an extent that is not 4 KiB-aligned is over-read to its enclosing blocks into a pinned buffer and staged from its head offset, counted in `overread_bytes`). An unrecognized value refuses to start. Only runtime read failures fall back to mmap, counted in the runtime `fallbacks` and error counters. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spill_io) |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_PINNED_FRAC` | `0.60` | Finite `(0,1]` fraction of `MemAvailable` the spill tier may pin. An out-of-range or non-numeric value warns once, uses 0.60 and increments `config_fallbacks`; the load continues. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_PREAD_DEPTH` | `2` | Pinned-buffer count and worker-thread count for `worker` and `direct` (`1..=64`); it also sizes the blocking `pread` pool. An out-of-range or non-integer value refuses to start. `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh` sets 32. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spill_pread_depth) |
| `MEMRA_SPILL_WORKER_EXPERT_WINDOW` | `(depth - 1) / 3`, minimum 1 | Complete experts kept in the rolling positioned-read window of the worker spill backend. Each expert takes three projection buffers, so the auto value derives from `MEMRA_SPILL_PREAD_DEPTH`. An unparseable value uses the auto size. |  |
| `MEMRA_ST_DIRECT` | on (`=0` reverts) | Safetensors NVFP4 loads directly into the split-plane layout. `=0` converts to GGUF layout first. |  |
| `MEMRA_ST_E4M3` | on (unset or any value but `0`) | F8-E4M3 safetensors projections of the per-tensor and block-128 scale classes keep raw e4m3 as the one resident copy: decode dequantizes in-kernel and prefill runs the FP8 GEMM on the same bytes. Per-row F8 still re-encodes to Q8_0; GGUF is untouched. `0` is the rollback seam to the Q8_0 slab, a diagnostic representation. Stacked block-128 MoE expert banks need the per-block FP8 MMQ tile (`fp8_blk_kernels_built`: sm_120a and sm_100a only): on other builds the loader refuses them and names `0`. A native bank runs only resident or through Step EP/TP; a bank left on the host is refused at load. | [fp8dec-20260805](../research/fp8dec-20260805/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_st_e4m3) |
| `MEMRA_ST_PINNED` | off | `1` pins the safetensors expert store in host RAM. Use it only when the whole expert set fits `free_pinnable_ram`: a partial pin evicts the page cache and collapses decode. A full pin is transport only (greedy output byte-identical; staging at PCIe line rate instead of the pageable mmap rate) and costs host RSS equal to the expert set plus a longer load. Default off because the fit guard needs an expert census that safetensors sources lack (`exact_expert_bytes` is GGUF-only); set it explicitly on hosts where the experts fit. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_st_pinned) |
| `MEMRA_ST_REPACK_DISK` | on | The safetensors stream-repack loader writes repacked expert banks to a `.memra-repack` cache beside the checkpoint and mmaps them, so a reload skips the repack. `0` forces the in-RAM gather. |  |
| `MEMRA_TOKENIZER_GGUF` | unset (the checkpoint directory's own tokenizer) | Tokenizer source override for directory-sourced models: `name=path[,name=path...]` takes that model's tokenizer from a GGUF, for checkpoints whose `tokenizer.json` is not byte-level BPE (Gemma-4's SentencePiece class), which `Tokenizer::from_hf_dir` cannot load. `verify_vocab_against_hf_dir` compares every id's piece byte-exactly plus eos/bos against the directory's `tokenizer.json`; any disagreement fails the boot. A bare path, an empty name or path, or a repeated model refuses. Boot line `[tokenizer] <model>: taken from <gguf> ...`. Rollback: unset. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_tokenizer_gguf) |

## Multi-GPU

Pipeline (PP), tensor (TP) and expert (EP) parallelism, automatic placement, transports and peer access. `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` with `MEMRA_PARALLEL_DEVICES` is the family-independent entry: it sizes pipeline or expert placement from checkpoint bytes per card. `MEMRA_PP_STAGES` with `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES`, `MEMRA_STEP_TP` and `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` are the explicit forms, and most other rows here are their parameters and rollback seams; placement behavior is in [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#multi-gpu).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_BATCH_PP=0` | on | With the ppN door open the batched decode step takes its own stage split (`decode_step_batch_ppn`). `0` sends it back through the unsplit body, where a sharded cross-device placement is refused by `refuse_unsplit_if_remote`. Read per step, never memoized, so the bit-identity gate (`decode-batch-gate --mode pp`) A/Bs split vs unsplit in one process. | [pp2-batch-20260806](../research/pp2-batch-20260806/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_batch_pp) |
| `MEMRA_DEV1_ROUTER` | off | `1`: rank 1 routes locally from its own z copy through per-layer router replicas (gate_inp f32, exp_probs_b, active mask, uploaded once over P2P); deterministic kernels on identical input bits give bit-equal selections, so rank 1's sweep starts without waiting for the root router and broadcast. Rides the `MEMRA_ROUTES_PRESTAGE` hook. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dev1_router) |
| `MEMRA_DUAL_PP` | Auto (unset) | Dual-active plain decode for PP-2. Unset (Auto) runs dual only on the validated regime (PP-2 fence, double-slot boundary, peer transport, B>=2) and falls back to the serial PP-N walker elsewhere, including PP-3 and `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1`. `0` is the serial rollback seam and resolves unset overlap off; `1` routes every B>=2 PP-2 call into the dual body, so an ineligible placement refuses loudly before any token or cache advance. The worker schedules up to two per-wave caps per tick, split A=`ceil(c/2)`, B=`floor(c/2)`. `/metrics.dual_pp` is operator-only; `MEMRA_DUAL_PP_TIMING=1` adds CUDA-event spans. | [RESULTS-regate.md](../research/dualpp2-20260811/RESULTS-regate.md), [RESULTS.md](../research/dualpp1-20260811/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dual_pp) |
| `MEMRA_EP_DIET` | off | General EP dispatch-diet door; the glm5 TP-2 walk is its one consumer. `1` changes only data movement, keeping the per-slot expert kernels and slot-ordered fmaf combine: one bulk peer z fan-out per layer call, peer rows computed into a compact block and returned in one bulk DtoH+HtoD, and the `t*n_used` `axpy_f32` launches folded into one `moe_pairs_scatter`. Decode stays byte-identical to the v1 walk. Set without `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` on a glm5-class plan refuses at load (`=0` is a pin); elsewhere it does nothing. Read per call. Two-name law with `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_DIET`: either `=1` arms, `=0` on either pins, a disagreeing pair falls closed with one `[flag-alias]` line. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/ep-diet-20260831/LANE.md), [RESULTS.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tp2-battery-20260831/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_ep_diet) |
| `MEMRA_EP_GROUPED_PRIME` | off | On the glm5 TP-2 EP walk (its one consumer), prefill shapes (t>16) run the family's chunked grouped MoE prefill per rank over that rank's resident slab, then root adds the peer's bulk-returned partial. Routing and per-expert rows are bit-identical to the plain arms; the one reassociation, the root+peer add, is band-gated at 1e-3. Fires only where the plain grouped arm would (f16g-eligible qtypes, n_used<=8, bank<=512, pre clamp); `MEMRA_MOE_GROUPED_PREFILL=0` and `MEMRA_MOE_GATE` runs keep it off, other shapes fall to the sequential EP walk. Set without `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` on a glm5-class plan refuses at load. Read per call; two-name law with `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_GROUPED_PRIME`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/ep-diet-20260831/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_ep_grouped_prime) |
| `MEMRA_EP_MAP` | unset: the even expert split | `=<path>` points the glm5 TP-2 EP shard builders at a measured placement map in the frozen `memra-ep-map-v1` JSON format, minted by `tools/build_expert_placement_map.py` from `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE` traces. Fail-closed at the load preflight, by name: unreadable or malformed file, wrong `format`, rank count != 2, `entry_rank` != 0, expert count != the routed bank, a missing or foreign layer row, an empty rank, or a map set without `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` on a glm5-class plan. Placement changes bytes moved, never bytes computed. The armed load prints the map's sha256 (`[glm5-tp-preflight] ep-map armed`). Alias `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_MAP`; different paths on both refuse naming both. | [ep-place-20260831](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/ep-place-20260831/), [REPORT.md](../research/ep-placement-map-20260831/REPORT.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_ep_map) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_DIET` | off | Family alias of `MEMRA_EP_DIET`: same values and reader; honored alone (refusals then name it), and a disagreeing pair with the general name falls the door closed, naming both. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_ep_diet) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_GROUPED_PRIME` | off | Family alias of `MEMRA_EP_GROUPED_PRIME`: same values and reader; honored alone (refusals then name it), and a disagreeing pair with the general name falls the door closed, naming both. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_ep_grouped_prime) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_EP_MAP` | unset | Family alias of `MEMRA_EP_MAP`: same path value and fail-closed laws, resolved by `ep_map::ep_map_env()`. Set alone it is honored and refusals name it; set beside a disagreeing `MEMRA_EP_MAP` the load refuses naming both. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_ep_map) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` | off (unset, empty or `0`) | glm5_next tensor-parallel seam, TP-2 or TP-4 (TP-3 refuses). Spec `LAYER[-LAYER]@DEVICE,DEVICE[;...]`, `all@0,1[,2,3]` = whole trunk; integer ids, one device group, first device owns. A fail-closed preflight refuses by name: non-glm5 plans, non-dividing head or expert counts, KDA head_dim != 128, `MEMRA_PP_STAGES>1`, `MEMRA_STEP_TP`/`MEMRA_STEP_EP`, and an armed `MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE`, `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS` or `MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT`. KDA and MLA are head-sharded, MoE is EP-N (`MEMRA_GLM5_EP_MAP` or an even split). Serving: plain route only, needs `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0`, refuses `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC=1`, `MEMRA_DSPARK_SPEC=1` and `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH=1`. Rollback: unset. | [tp2-20260831](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tp2-20260831/), [rig-gates](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/composition-20260901/rig-gates/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_tp) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_EXPERT_SPLIT` | split on sm_100a builds, whole experts elsewhere; `1`/`0` pick either on any build | With `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` armed, `1` shards every expert across the ranks (rank r holds out-rows `[r*half, (r+1)*half)` of `gate`/`up` and the matching `down` input columns), so each rank streams half of every routed expert. Not byte-identical: `down` is a column split and ranks add partial sums, one reduction per layer; gated on argmax plus a maxdiff band. Mutually exclusive with whole-expert EP; a placement map with the split refuses. Under it MLA `wo` is sharded by input columns. Refuses mid-block halves, unknown qtypes, per-expert `layouts`, `fp8_blk` scale planes, tiered banks and non-dividing widths. A placement selector, not a rollback seam; announces `[glm5-tp-split]`. | [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_tp_expert_split) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_SYMMETRIC` | on for sm_100a builds when the expert split is on; root-orchestrated walk elsewhere; `1`/`0` pick either | At load each TP layer's glue (norms, hc sites, a router copy, dense FFN, shared-expert halves) is replicated to the peer and the KDA `wo` turns row-parallel; at decode both ranks run `hyper_range_decode_ws_sym` and meet in a one-shot all-reduce fused into the hc post launch. On sm_100a it records per-rank CUDA graphs (halves need `MEMRA_MLA_SEG_WS`, capture needs `MEMRA_HTOD_DIET`; `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_NO_CAPTURE=1` runs eager). Class: the split's plus the KDA `wo` K split; the router stays on root. No transport named arms `device-push`. Declines loudly to the root walk on: split off, partial spec, same-device gate, one card, another transport. A posture selector, not a rollback seam. | [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_tp_symmetric) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_TRANSPORT` | unset: as `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT` | Family alias of `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT`: same values and fail-closed parse, resolved by `tp_transport::resolve_transport`. Set alone it is honored and refusals name it; set beside a disagreeing `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT` the load refuses naming both. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_tp_transport) |
| `MEMRA_LEN_MIRROR_LAZY` | off | `1`: skip the per-token 4-byte in-stream len host-to-device copies (about 3x45 per token): the local device mirror is unread in TP decode, and under `MEMRA_FUSE_ROPE_APPEND` the fused append's atomicInc owns the rank counters; rollback paths keep absolute sets. It must drop, or spec must own its mirrors, once MTP partial accept reaches this path. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_len_mirror_lazy) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_DIRECT` | off | `1`: moe direct join, the o-proj recipe on the expert combine. Peer route accumulators live root-side (`axpy_rows_seq_md` accumulates in registers and stores once, so P2P is a single 16 KB pass) and the model engine adds the shard rows into a fresh output row; WAR safety rides the ev_entry event chain. Bit-identical. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_direct) |
| `MEMRA_OPROJ_DIRECT` | off | `1`: o-proj direct join for the step TP2 fused arm. Peer ranks write their fused b4 O partial over P2P into a root-resident buffer and the model engine adds both partials into its own output row, so the root stream leaves the join (no peer pull copy, no root add, one event hop). Same operands and add order: bit-identical. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_oproj_direct) |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL` / `MEMRA_PARALLEL_DEVICES` | off / unset (required by `auto`) | `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` with `MEMRA_PARALLEL_DEVICES=D,D[,...]` (2 to 4 cards) is the family-independent placement entry: before CUDA allocation the loader compiles `ModelPlan` and sizes each placement from checkpoint bytes per card. Dense plans get memory-balanced PP. Routed W4A16 plans get whole-expert EP (native P2P, BF16 expert boundary, BF16 non-expert rows unless `MEMRA_BF16_MMV=0`) only when every rank keeps `MEMRA_PARALLEL_RESERVE_MB`, else PP; no fit refuses with both byte estimates. Activation-quantized artifacts use PP. Auto PP sets `MEMRA_PP_SPLITS`. `auto` refuses beside `MEMRA_STEP_EP`, `MEMRA_STEP_TP`, `MEMRA_PP_STAGES` or `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES`. Experimental. | [PRO6000-MULTICARD.md](decisions/PRO6000-MULTICARD.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_parallel) |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_DEVICE_ROUTER` | off | Experimental generic W4A16 whole-expert EP route. `1` keeps sigmoid top-k ids and weights on the root GPU, lets rank-local kernels reject non-owned slots, and replaces the input copy, route copies and BF16 conversion with one fused peer-staging launch per rank; every batch width writes one canonical global slot row per routed pair, so decode and verify share one accumulation program. Requires `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto`, the W4A16 NVFP4 expert backend and native P2P. Unset or `0` is the host-visible router rollback; other values refuse. Off until bounded-logit, long-prompt, sampled c1/c4 and MTP gates pass on the exact model x hardware tuple. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_parallel_ep_device_router) |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_PAIR_DOWN` | off | Experimental t>1 W4A16 EP down-projection kernel with one block per global routed pair instead of the owner-filtered slot program. `1` is diagnostic only: it has no end-to-end correctness or performance receipt. Unset or `0` keeps the canonical slot-row oracle; other values refuse. Any other use needs a green baseline and an isolated K=1..8 A/B first. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_parallel_ep_pair_down) |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_ACT` | off | Optional W4A8 numeric class inside automatic W4A16 EP decode. `1` touches only the t=1 routed-expert interior: each rank quantizes the active expert input and SwiGLU output to q8_1 for the NVFP4xQ8 dp4a gate/up/down kernels, while the layer boundary stays f32/BF16, weights stay NVFP4 and the route accumulation order stays pinned. t=2..4 and prefill keep the exact BF16-boundary program. Requires `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_DEVICE_ROUTER=1`; unset or `0` is the W4A16 rollback; other values refuse. Off pending logit, sampled-quality, c1 speed, MTP and repeated-boot gates. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_parallel_ep_q8_act) |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_SCOPE` | unset (`all` inside the Q8 arm) | Diagnostic precision split for `MEMRA_PARALLEL_EP_Q8_ACT=1`: `all` quantizes the expert input and the post-SwiGLU row; `gate-up` uses Q8 only for input, gate and up and returns to the exact BF16 boundary for down; `down` keeps exact BF16 gate/up and quantizes only the down program. Setting it while Q8 activation is off refuses, as does any other value. Not an enable flag and no default claim: it isolates the smallest numeric class that meets the same-artifact logit and sampled-quality gates. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_parallel_ep_q8_scope) |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_RESERVE_MB` | `6144` | Per-card planning reserve in MiB for `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto`: the planner adds it to every candidate rank before admitting EP or PP (CUDA context, loader transforms, boundary buffers, initial workspace). It is separate from request-time KV admission: passing it allows the load, not a context or concurrency target. An unsigned value overrides it for a measured setup, `0` is a diagnostic posture, a non-integer refuses. If neither EP nor PP keeps the reserve, startup refuses before weights move to CUDA. | [PRO6000-MULTICARD.md](decisions/PRO6000-MULTICARD.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_parallel_reserve_mb) |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_TP_ATTENTION` | off | Strict `0`/`1` extension of `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` for a routed W4A16 plan on whole-expert EP. `1` derives a TP-attention sidecar for every trunk attention layer from `ModelPlan` (head ranges, Q/K/V column and O row shards, rank-local KV) over native P2P, while the same group keeps whole-expert ownership. An unsupported attention op, non-divisible heads, a PP-only placement or use without `auto` refuses before serving. Generic ungated decode needs `MEMRA_STEP_TP_DECODE_V2=1`. Unset or `0` keeps root-local attention. Off until exact checkpoint and serve gates, sampled c1 pricing and sidecar-memory admission pass. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_parallel_tp_attention) |
| `MEMRA_PARALLEL_TP_ATTENTION_RANKS` | unset (all automatic ranks) | Strict `2`/`3`/`4` width for `MEMRA_PARALLEL_TP_ATTENTION=1`. A narrower width puts only the owner-first prefix of ranks in TP attention while every routed layer keeps whole-expert ownership over the full rank set (four cards with `=2` compile TP2 attention plus EP4 experts); every `ModelPlan` divisibility check still applies. Setting it without the parent flag, asking for more ranks than the placement, or any other value refuses before weight upload. It exposes the rank-group substrate and carries no performance or default claim. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_parallel_tp_attention_ranks) |
| `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE=0` | on | Diagnostics-only escape hatch for PP-N's byte-integrity gate. By default every cross-device boundary is tested both ways at boot (a 16 KiB preflight, then real `BoundarySlot` copies at 1, 8, 16 and `PRIME_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS` rows) and re-probed at runtime on a staggered copy-count schedule; a mismatch is SECURITY RED and latches native P2P off onto host bounce. `0` refuses a sharded cross-device native placement unless `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1`, where it logs SECURITY RED and counts `peer_probe_bypassed`. Never disable in serving; to recover a deferred probe, drain spec sessions or restart with `MEMRA_SERVE_SPEC=0`. | [DESIGN.md](../research/cx-peerprobe-20260811/DESIGN.md), [sec9-20260812](../research/sec9-20260812/), [probehitch-20260812](../research/probehitch-20260812/), [probeobs-20260812](../research/probeobs-20260812/), [probeready-20260812](../research/probeready-20260812/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_peer_probe) |
| `MEMRA_PEER_PROBE_DEFERRAL_BOUND=<n>` | 4 (one 32,768-copy rotation) | Consecutive deferred runtime-probe copy intervals allowed before `/readyz.peer_probe_integrity` reads `degraded`, `peer_probe_integrity_degraded=true` and SECURITY RED publish, and new speculative candidates serve plain until a completed probe clears the streak. Admission only: it never forces a probe under live UVA state. Read once at worker start; `0` clamps to 1, invalid values use 4. Raise only with an explicit integrity-coverage policy. | [probeobs-20260812](../research/probeobs-20260812/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_peer_probe_deferral_bound) |
| `MEMRA_PP_ALLOW_UNSPLIT_BATCH=1` | unset (refuse) | Measurement door: re-admits the unsplit decode paths (`decode_step_dc`, its graph capture, and explicit `MEMRA_BATCH_PP=0`/`MEMRA_SPEC_PP=0` rollbacks) under an open ppN door with a sharded cross-device placement, which otherwise refuses through `pp::refuse_unsplit_if_remote` because the unsplit trunk peer-reads every remote stage's weights each step. Exactness is unaffected; the cost is a large perf cliff. Non-measurement escapes: `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0`, or close the pp door. | [pp2-hardening-20260806](../research/pp2-hardening-20260806/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_allow_unsplit_batch) |
| `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES=<d0>,..,<dN-1>` | unset (all stages on the primary device) | Stage-to-device placement: stage s runs on device ds. The serving worker and `run-spec` primary Engine follow dN-1, the head stage's device (any other pick peer-reads the head every draft token); non-spec gate and bench binaries follow d0. Remote stages get their own Engine and context, each layer range's KV and weights live on its stage's device, and cross boundaries use `cudaMemcpyPeerAsync`, guarded by `cuDeviceCanAccessPeer` (an inaccessible peer fails loudly). Ordinals are in `CUDA_VISIBLE_DEVICES`' process-local namespace. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_devices) |
| `MEMRA_PP_EXIT_PUBLISH=0` | on | A ppN body returning to its caller publishes every stage stream to the caller's stream (`PpNRt::publish_all_to`, event waits only), so the pool cannot hand the caller a block an earlier stage stream still uses. Wired at the hc prime, the glm5 spec verify walk and the glm5 per-stage rollback. `0` restores the pre-lane program, a known-racy control arm, never a serving configuration. Read per call so a gate A/Bs both arms in one process; gate arm `P0 prime-determinism`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/accrace-20260901/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_exit_publish) |
| `MEMRA_PP_HOST_BOUNCE=1` | unset (off); a runtime probe failure can promote it | Broken-peer fallback for sharded cross-device PP: each cross-stage activation handoff goes D2H into a page-locked host slot, an event, then H2D on the destination stream (two slots per boundary sized from `PRIME_CHUNK_MAX_TOKENS * n_embd * 4` bytes). Serving peer and pool grants are bypassed after a boot diagnostic. The same transport is the automatic one-way runtime fallback after a native re-probe failure. Refuses `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0`, unsplit remote-weight walks and stream-mode spec; serving also disables spec admission and cross-device prefix and affinity snapshots. | [hostbounce-20260810](../research/hostbounce-20260810/), [probehitch-20260812](../research/probehitch-20260812/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_host_bounce) |
| `MEMRA_PP_OVERLAP` | follows `MEMRA_DUAL_PP`: on under the unset (Auto) default | Double-buffered boundary slot alternation: TX(t+1) waits only on the previous use of its own slot, so another wave may enter stage 0 while the prior wave's later stages run; scheduling only, every wave's math is event-ordered. Unset resolves on under Auto, off under `MEMRA_DUAL_PP=0` and under explicit `MEMRA_DUAL_PP=1`; an explicit `1` or `0` always wins. PP-3/4 `MEMRA_PP_WAVE=1` consumes the resolved value, so `0` keeps the serial PP-N rollback. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_overlap) |
| `MEMRA_PP_SHARD=0` | sharded (whenever `MEMRA_PP_DEVICES` is set) | Rollback seam to the bring-up weight placement: every weight uploads through the primary engine and remote stages peer-read weights over NVLink/PCIe. Gated bit-identical both ways at N=2/4/8. Host bounce refuses it (`pp.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_PP_STAGES=<N>` / `MEMRA_PP_SPLITS=<c1,..,cN-1>` (`MEMRA_PP_SPLIT=<layer>` = the N=2 spelling) | unset (off); cuts default to the even split | Pipeline-parallel N-stage runtime: the trunk runs as N stage subgraphs, each on its own CUDA stream, handing the f32 residual through double-buffered boundary slots (dtod same-device, `cudaMemcpyPeerAsync` cross-device). Eager, batched (`MEMRA_BATCH_PP`), spec verify (`MEMRA_SPEC_PP`) and prime take their own stage split; `decode_step_dc` and its graph run unsplit and refuse on a sharded cross-device placement (`pp::refuse_unsplit_if_remote`). An invalid cut list fails the model load by name (`pp::refuse_invalid_fence`). The deferred pipelined arm stays experimental; same-device pipelining refuses (`MEMRA_PP_FORCE_SAME_DEV_PIPELINED=1` is a soak override only). | [m1-pp2-20260801](../research/m1-pp2-20260801/), [RESULTS.md](../research/m2-pp8-20260802/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_split) |
| `MEMRA_PP_STREAMS=0` | per-stage streams (on) | Rollback seam to the increment-1 same-stream boundary: two plain dtod copies per cut, with no per-stage streams, events or devices, generalized to N stages (`pp.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_PP_WAVE` | off (unset or `0`) | Strict `0`/`1` PP-3/PP-4 request and prime wavefront; any other value refuses. `1` splits a decode tick into at most one balanced wave per stage, one host worker per non-head stage, with two explicit slot credits per boundary. Requires distinct devices, resolved `MEMRA_PP_OVERLAP=1`, native peer transport, a gated batched decode rewrite, the stage-split prime and a ModelPlan pipeline program. W4A16 artifacts with preserved BF16 non-expert weights also need `MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1`, else the engine refuses before touching caches. A failed wave taints every participating cache. `0` keeps the serial walker; PP-2 uses `MEMRA_DUAL_PP`. | [PRO6000-MULTICARD.md](decisions/PRO6000-MULTICARD.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_wave) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_PIPE=0` | on | Rollback seam for PP-2 prime chunk pipelining: unset pipelines when the prime stage split has at least two chunks; `0` keeps the serial chunk walk over the same chunk boundaries. Scheduling only; the serial split is the bit-exact oracle. Gate: `ppsplit` serial vs pipelined replay plus `pp::PRIME_PIPE_OVERLAPS` liveness. | [pipeprime-20260808](../research/pipeprime-20260808/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_pipe) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_PP=0` | on | Rollback seam for the prime (chunked prefill) stage split: `0` sends the prime through the unsplit whole-trunk walk. Unlike the other paths it keeps no `refuse_unsplit_if_remote`, because that unsplit walk is `prime-split-gate`'s reference arm and must stay callable (it pays an amortized peer-read cost). Read per call. Gate: `prime-split-gate` bit-identity plus the `pp::PRIME_SPLIT_CHUNKS` liveness counter. | [pp-leverb-20260807](../research/pp-leverb-20260807/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_pp) |
| `MEMRA_ROUTES_PRESTAGE` | off | `1`: stage the routed-expert input to every rank and quantize it before the router runs, so the rank chains overlap the router GEMV and top-k instead of waiting on the entry event. Same copies and quantize kernel: bit-identical. `MEMRA_DEV1_ROUTER` rides this hook. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_routes_prestage) |
| `MEMRA_SEL_MIRROR` | off; decide-by: 2026-11-17 | `1`: the per-rank routed-selection pull runs as one `moe_sel_w_mirror` launch instead of two 32-byte D2D copies, and when every consuming rank shares the model engine's device the staging pair is skipped (ranks read the process-persistent selection rows directly). Bit-identical; refused under the graph door, whose captured copies need fixed staging addresses. Missing gate: the battery on a 2-card pair. A pass by the date makes it the naked default; no battery by the date deletes the door and every kernel only it reaches. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_sel_mirror) |
| `MEMRA_SHEXP_OVERLAP` | off | `1`: issue the shared expert (dual matvec plus SwiGLU, split down) on the model engine's stream from the routes prejoin hook, so it runs while peer ranks drain their routed sweeps; `add_scaled_rows` applies the row after the join. The split program is bit-identical to the fused one, and the hook placement after rank issue is load-bearing. The same hook covers automatic W4A16 whole-expert EP (2 to 4 ranks), unpriced there until its interleaved A/B and full-logit gate land. Unset or `0` restores post-join shared-expert execution. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_shexp_overlap) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_PP=0` | on | With the ppN door open the spec verify forward (`decode_step_t_core_ppn`, T=K+1) takes its own stage split; `0` sends verify through the unsplit trunk walk, caught by the same guard. Read per verify call. Exactness plumbing only: `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE` decides whether PP-2 admits spec and defaults it off. Gate: `decode-batch-gate --mode ppspec`. | [pp2-spec-20260806](../research/pp2-spec-20260806/), [pp2spec-crash-20260807](../research/pp2spec-crash-20260807/), [specplace-20260808](../research/specplace-20260808/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_pp) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP` | unset (off) | Legacy explicit-layer EP diagnostic, `LAYER[-LAYER]@DEVICE,DEVICE[;...]` (for example `24-25@1,2;31@0,2`), kept for focused bisects and partial-layer gates; new families use `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto`. Each selected routed-expert layer drops its single-device slab, gives each native expert one owner rank, and keeps router and shared-expert work on the owning stage. Duplicate assignments or devices, unsupported layouts, non-PRO hardware and a list without the layer owner refuse before CUDA. Host-bounce is the default oracle; BF16-activation NVFP4 artifacts opt into native execution with `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1 MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES=1` once `HostExps` validates the bank. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_ep) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_DEVICE_ARITHMETIC` | off | `1` keeps TP4/TP8 expert-owner SwiGLU activation and slot-ordered route accumulation on the GPUs after native peer dispatch, with memra's host-matching `expf` and explicit round-to-nearest ops; rank 0 accumulates in token/slot order and reads back once. Requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1` and a `MEMRA_STEP_TP` TP4/TP8 expert-owner layer; legacy EP, host-bounce and TP2 sharded experts refuse. Unset or `0` is the host-canonical rollback; other values refuse. A raw-bit exactness path with `performance_claim=false`, kept because the `ep-step-fp8-gate` owner-grouped FP8 gates require it. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_ep_device_arithmetic) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_EP_GROUPED_PREFILL` | off | Strict `0`/`1` Step-3.7 routed-expert prefill door. `1` requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_PREFILL=1`, native P2P, device arithmetic and the official TP4 expert-owner layout. One model-scoped capacity plan is reused across all 42 routed layers for 16 to 4096 active tokens; Step supplies only the layer clamp policy and tensor views. Raw-bit exact on CUDA 13.1 and 12.8. Concurrent requests use the scheduler's per-session grouped path (the cross-request concat path was removed). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_ep_grouped_prefill) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES` | off; `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` selects it for a W4A16 artifact | Strict `0`/`1` (any other value is an error): the device-resident NVFP4 routed-expert program. The TP2 arm shares one q8_1 quantization per rank across the selected experts. The W4A16 EP arm covers 1 to 128 active token rows: each rank owns a contiguous expert range and peer-reads the input once, and one root kernel reduces every token's slots in original order at every width, so decode and verify keep one reduction order; wider batches keep the host-canonical oracle. The explicit route needs `MEMRA_STEP_EP` or `MEMRA_STEP_TP`, `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1` and the owning device first. A performance claim needs the bounded-logit, sampled c=1 and sampled concurrency gates. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_nvfp4_dev_routes) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP` | unset (off) | Step-3.7 TP placement, `LAYER[-LAYER]@DEVICE,DEVICE[;...]`; `all@0,1,2,3` selects the official TP4 trunk (layers 0 to 44). A fail-closed preflight before the first weight upload checks PP owner, attention shards, expert layout, runtime group, uniform PRO hardware and the block-128 FP8 expert census; the spec is read once. With native P2P, attention and KV stay rank-local (SWA layers on a ring of `min(max_ctx, 4639)` rows) and flat-history prefix snapshots, restores and fanout refuse. Routed experts: TP2 shards the 1280-wide projections, TP4 uses expert ownership. One spec selects the whole hybrid: do not also set `MEMRA_STEP_EP`.
