Illegal layouts refuse before execution. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_tp) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_BULK_P2P` | off | Strict `0`/`1` transport shape for Step TP; `1` requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P=1`. It replaces token-row Q/K/V gathers and O scatters with one dense peer transfer per remote rank plus byte-preserving root placement or packing, and assembles F32 projection chunks with one strided placement kernel instead of one D2D copy per token. Projection kernels and the canonical O reduction order are unchanged. The current stage also keeps gathered attention, canonical O and K/V shadow handoffs on the root device; positions, head-gate control, MoE routes and final logits stay host-visible. Experimental: owes dual-CUDA equality, D2H collapse and high-context serving proof. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_tp_bulk_p2p) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_F32_MIRROR` | off | `1` expands each Step TP Q/K/V/O BF16 shard to exact F32 once at load, frees the BF16 TP copy, and reuses the F32 values with the same TP1/TP2/TP4/TP8 canonical chunk shapes. Requires `MEMRA_STEP_TP`; unset or `0` keeps BF16 residency with decode-time expansion; other values refuse. The owning-stage fallback weights are unchanged. An opt-in decode trade of extra rank VRAM for fewer allocations and conversion kernels; native P2P and device arithmetic do not turn it on. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_tp_f32_mirror) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_NATIVE_P2P` | off | `1` enables correctness-first `cuMemcpyPeerAsync` collectives for Step TP. Startup requires directed peer capability, peer and default-pool access, and an exact 16 KiB / 64 KiB / 1 MiB / 64 MiB size-class ladder in every direction. TP attention projections, cache and O, the TP2 expert program and TP4/TP8 expert-owner dispatch and return then use native peer copies; expert activation and route accumulation stay host-canonical unless `MEMRA_STEP_EP_DEVICE_ARITHMETIC=1`, and every marker labels the choice. Any other non-empty value refuses. An exactness path with `performance_claim=false`, not a throughput default. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_tp_native_p2p) |
| `MEMRA_STEP_TP_PREFILL` | off | Strict `0`/`1` Step-3.7 multi-token prefill door. `1` requires native P2P, `MEMRA_STEP_EP_DEVICE_ARITHMETIC=1` and non-FP8 KV on a TP4 placement (TP2 is admitted with no receipts), and keeps Q/K/V, QK norm, RoPE, quantized KV append, attention and canonical O reduction rank-local across all 45 trunk layers. A Step prime-batch call refuses before cache mutation and the server falls back to the per-session path, which declines `MEMRA_STEP_GEMM_PRIME` and takes the walk; with no rank-local Step TP layer that prime refuses and the server refuses to boot. Off: TP4 high-context prefill is a throughput no-go until a bulk peer-copy collective that keeps canonical arithmetic lands. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_step_tp_prefill) |
| `MEMRA_TP_AR_BLOCKS` | unset: 1 block up to n = 8192 floats, `min(72, n/512)` above | Overrides the block count of the one-shot all-reduce kernel and its gather twin (the DSV4 TP/EP joins, the glm5 symmetric TP-2 walk's reduce sites and the glm5 TP indexer's prime candidate exchange). Clamped to 1..=72 and to n/512. Every block pays its own start and end flag round trips over the fabric, so one block wins at decode width. Read per launch. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_tp_ar_blocks) |
| `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT` | unset: `device-push` on the glm5 symmetric posture (real group) and on the DeepSeek-V4.1 route at 2 to 8 ranks, `peer-pull` on sm_100a, else `host-canonical` | Bytes-mover for an armed TP walk's cross-rank hops (`tp_transport.rs`). Two consumers: the glm5 TP loader, and the DeepSeek-V4.1 N-rank route (`dsv41_gpu::tp`), which takes all three values through its own gather and defaults to `device-push` at two to eight ranks (the measured winner on two cards, decode and prefill), falling back to `host-canonical` with a note when the cards grant no peer access; there a gather whose ranks each store at most 224 KiB runs inside kernels and a larger one through kept blocks and the copy engine (`dsv41-bench p2p` measures the crossover). `host-canonical`/`0`: dtoh, host, htod (the gate pins it). `peer-pull`/`1`: consumer-issued device peer copies, one CUDA event per rank. `device-push`/`2`: producer stores on its own stream on a real NVLink group, the one arm proving an SM-issued peer store, which the symmetric posture's one-shot all-reduce needs. All move bytes only: byte-identical. DeepSeek-V4.1 receipts: [tp](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/tp/), [perf](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/perf/). Other spellings refuse. Arming checks peer access both ways and runs a byte-integrity ladder (16 KiB to 64 MiB); one differing word refuses. Read once at preflight. Alias `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_TRANSPORT`; a disagreeing pair refuses. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tp-transport-20260901/LANE.md), [TP-PEER-PULL-DEFAULT.md](decisions/TP-PEER-PULL-DEFAULT.md), [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_tp_transport) |

## Build-time switches

What `build.rs` and nvcc read at compile time: the CUDA target, the toolchain path, optional CUTLASS archives, tile sizes and compile defines, plus the build outputs (fatbin paths, build identity, the built arch) the crates read through `env!`, which are not user settings. Most builds need nothing, since `MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH` is detected from the first GPU; set it to build for another card. Targets, stubs and defines are in [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#build-and-binding), and source builds in [INSTALLATION.md](INSTALLATION.md#build-from-source).

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ACCPROBE_F32_PLAIN` | unset (off) | Research instrument only: `=1` rebuilds the F32 arm of `accprobe-bench`'s kernel (`cu/mmq_q8_0_f32acc.cu`) on the plain `kind::f8f6f4` MMA form, so the FP8-ST v3 gate's published Q1 numbers stay reproducible; the equalized default refutes that gate. No dispatch seam; the TU is never linked into a serving path. | [VERDICT.md](../research/fp8v3-gate-20260805/VERDICT.md), [rp-on-st-20260806](../research/rp-on-st-20260806/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#accprobe_f32_plain) |
| `MEMRA_ARCH_CHECK` | on | `=0` skips the `Engine::new` guard that compares the device's compute capability with the binary's exact SASS target and fails early with a rebuild hint (fatbins are single-arch SASS). A `120a` build refuses a 12.1 device and a `121a` build refuses 12.0. Any other value keeps the guard. |  |
| `MEMRA_BUILD_ID` | set by memra-server build.rs | 12 lowercase hex: the content digest of the workspace's compiled inputs, never empty and never `unknown`. Read through `env!` into `SYSTEM_FINGERPRINT` together with the crate version. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#the-build-fingerprint-lanereal-system-fingerprint-2026-09-01) |
| `MEMRA_BUILD_ID_NOTE` | set by memra-server build.rs | Why the id is degraded, empty when it is not; read as `memra_server::BUILD_ID_NOTE`. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#the-build-fingerprint-lanereal-system-fingerprint-2026-09-01) |
| `MEMRA_BUILD_ID_SRC` | set by memra-server build.rs | `source-tree` (real) or `degraded`; read as `memra_server::BUILD_ID_SRC`, which drives the boot warning when the id is degraded. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#the-build-fingerprint-lanereal-system-fingerprint-2026-09-01) |
| `MEMRA_BUILD_SHA` | set by memra-server build.rs | The build's 12-hex git sha, or `unknown` when no repository is readable; read as `memra_server::BUILD_GIT_SHA`. Extra provenance only, never the identity: it does not survive a history rewrite. | [SERVING.md](SERVING.md#the-build-fingerprint-lanereal-system-fingerprint-2026-09-01) |
| `MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH`, `MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_TARGET`, `MEMRA_PROBE_CUDA_TARGET` | set by build.rs from the build target | Build outputs read through `env!`, never from the runtime environment. `MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_TARGET` is the exact SASS target (`120a`, `121a`, `100a`, `90a`, `89`) the `Engine::new` arch guard compares with the device (`MEMRA_ARCH_CHECK`). `MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH` is the policy family every arch-keyed default reads; a `121a` build reports `120a`. `MEMRA_PROBE_CUDA_TARGET` is memra-probe's target, which the probe asserts against the device's compute capability. | [SM121-SPARK-TARGET.md](decisions/SM121-SPARK-TARGET.md), [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#targets-stubs-and-compile-defines) |
| `MEMRA_CUDA_ARCH` | auto: the first GPU's compute capability (12.0 `120a`, 12.1 `121a`, 10.0 `100a`, 9.0 `90a`, 8.9 `89`); else `120a` | nvcc build target: `120a` (RTX 50-series, RTX PRO 6000 Blackwell), `121a` (GB10), `100a` (B200), `90a` (Hopper) or `89` (portable eval); any other value fails the build, and an explicit value wins over detection. With no GPU or an unmapped capability the build is `120a`, compile-only. `121a` compiles the `120a` sources and defaults with its own SASS (sm_120a SASS does not load on 12.1). `90a` adds the sm_80-class tensor-core paths and the wgmma kernels to the portable path, with sm_120a and sm_100a MMA kinds as fail-closed stubs. `89` is the correctness-first lane: native-FP4 prefill off, generic tiled GDN, prompt attention through f32 SDPA. | [SM121-SPARK-TARGET.md](decisions/SM121-SPARK-TARGET.md), [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#targets-stubs-and-compile-defines), [ARCHITECTURE-H100.md](../ARCHITECTURE-H100.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_cuda_arch) |
| `MEMRA_CUTLASS` | unset (off) | Set (any value) = compile the CUTLASS sm_120a NVFP4 GEMM archive (`cu/cutlass_fp4_sm120.cu` into `libmemra_cutlass.a`) and the `memra_cutlass` cfg that `cutlass_smoke` and the `MEMRA_FP4_CUTLASS` runtime door need. build.rs refuses it for any target other than `120a`. Headers come from `MEMRA_CUTLASS_ROOT`. | [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#optional-cutlass-archives) |
| `MEMRA_CUTLASS_ROOT` | unset: `MEMRA_CUTLASS` uses a flashinfer-venv CUTLASS tree hardcoded in build.rs; `MEMRA_DSV4_CUTLASS` requires it | Root of the CUTLASS header tree (`<root>/include`, `<root>/tools/util/include`) both CUTLASS archives compile against. `MEMRA_DSV4_CUTLASS` panics when it is unset. A change reruns build.rs, so both archives rebuild on the new headers. | [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#optional-cutlass-archives) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_CUTLASS` | unset (off); decide-by: 2026-09-25 | Set = compile `cu/dsv4_dense_cutlass.cu` into `libmemra_dsv4_cutlass.a` and link it whole-archive, so the weak `memra_dsv4_dense_cutlass_fp8` is non-null and the DSV4 prefill's m = 32 dense chunks take the CUTLASS split-K GEMM. Needs `MEMRA_CUTLASS_ROOT`. Its gencode is fixed at sm_120a, so build.rs refuses it for any other target. No runtime read: `arm_dense_cutlass_for_gate` is the gate seam. A new numeric class, never bit-equal to the shipped tree. It stays a build switch because a naked build has no CUTLASS tree. | [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#optional-cutlass-archives), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_cutlass) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_FMAD` | unset (`-fmad=false` on `cu/dsv4_gpu.cu`) | Research instrument, never a shipping default: `=1` drops the blanket `-fmad=false` from `cu/dsv4_gpu.cu` so nvcc may contract mul+add into FMA, to price the oracle-parity law; build.rs prints a cargo warning naming it. An FMA build is not bit-identical to the CPU oracle; batched-vs-sequential bit-exactness holds in both builds. Any other value keeps `-fmad=false`. No decide-by: a diagnostic whose subject is the shipped program. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_fmad) |
| `MEMRA_ENGINE_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_HYBRID_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_KDA_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_QMATVEC_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN_KF8VF8`, `MEMRA_ROUTER_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_SAMPLE_FATBIN`, `MEMRA_FATBIN` | set by build.rs; never set by hand | Build plumbing, not user flags. build.rs compiles each `cu/` unit (`kernels`, `hybrid`, `kda`, `qmatvec`, `flash_attn`, `moe_router`, `spec_sample`) and emits its fatbin path through `cargo:rustc-env`; the engine embeds the bytes with `include_bytes!(env!(...))`, so a binary carries its own kernels. `MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN_KF8VF8` is the second `flash_attn.cu` build, with raw e4m3 K and V, that Gemma's GKV and WKV layers load beside the default module. `MEMRA_FATBIN` is memra-probe's module, which the probe loads from its build directory. A `DOCS_RS` build writes empty placeholders. | [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#build-and-binding) |
| `MEMRA_FA_PP_MINBLOCKS` | unset: kernel default 2 | Build-time sweep of `fa_prefill_f32_pp`'s `__launch_bounds__` minBlocksPerMultiprocessor: `=<n>` rebuilds `cu/flash_attn.cu` with `-DFA_PP_MINBLOCKS=<n>`, trading register spills for more resident blocks. |  |
| `MEMRA_FP8_BLK_UNIT` | set by build.rs; absent on the docs-only build | Build plumbing, not an operator knob: build.rs exports which unit it compiled for the block-128 FP8 kernels, `cu/mmq_fp8_blk.cu` on the `120a` family (including `121a`) and on `100a`, `cu/mmq_fp8_blk_stub.cu` elsewhere. The `block128_fp8_arms_follow_the_compiled_kernel_unit` unit test checks `fp8_blk_kernels_built()` against it. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fp8_blk_unit) |
| `MEMRA_GEMM_FATBIN` | build.rs path of the compiled `cu/qmatvec_gemm.cu` | Set by build.rs like the other fatbin paths and embedded through `include_bytes!(env!(...))`. It is also a runtime override for sweeps: `MEMRA_GEMM_FATBIN=<path>` at run time loads that GEMM fatbin instead of the embedded one, so a sweep can try a `-D`-built module per process without a Rust rebuild. An unreadable path panics; an `89` build refuses the override. | [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#build-and-binding) |
| `MEMRA_GEMM_K1_LAUNCH` | unset: shipped (128,128,8) | `"BM,BN,NWARP"` override of the kernel1 (Q8_0/Q4_K/Q5_K) GEMM launch tile. It must match the `-D K1_BM/K1_BN/NWARP` the swept fatbin was built with, or the host grid and the kernel tile disagree. Unset or malformed values keep the shipped tile. Kernel2 (Q6_K/NVFP4) is untouched. |  |
| `MEMRA_IQEXP_K16` | unset (one `m16n8k32.s8` per k step) | Rollback seam for the MMA depth of the IQ-experts and IQ4_XS-dense MMQ tiles (`cu/mmq_iq_experts.cu`). Unset: one `m16n8k32.s8` per 32-value k step. `=1` rebuilds with the original two-`m16n8k16.s8` form; only `1` engages. Not bit-identical to k16 (the f32 fold rounds once instead of twice); both forms are gated on closeness, argmax and spec, not byte identity. | [iq-k32-20260807](../research/iq-k32-20260807/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_iqexp_k16) |
| `MEMRA_KV_KFMT`, `MEMRA_KV_VFMT` | `0` / `0` (q8_0 K, q5_1 V); the kf8vf8 fatbin builds with `1` / `2` | nvcc `-D` defines in `cu/flash_attn.cu`, not environment variables. build.rs compiles the file twice: the default fatbin with no define (the q8_0 K and q5_1 V trunk cache) and `MEMRA_FLASH_FATBIN_KF8VF8` with `-DMEMRA_KV_KFMT=1 -DMEMRA_KV_VFMT=2` (raw e4m3 K and V, no block scale) for Gemma's GKV and WKV layers. The format is a property of the loaded fatbin; kernel names are the same in both. | [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#cuflash_attncu) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_ARCHIVE_HASH` | set by build.rs | Build-time, not a switch: the content hash of every host-launcher kernel archive in the engine rlib (`libmemra_mmq.a`, plus `libmemra_dsv4_cutlass.a` when `MEMRA_DSV4_CUTLASS` links it), read by `env!()` into `memra_engine::MMQ_ARCHIVE_HASH`. It makes a `.cu`-only edit a rustc input, so a cached build (`RUSTC_WRAPPER=sccache`) cannot hand back an rlib with old kernels. build.rs overrides a hand-set value. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_mmq_archive_hash) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_FOLD_CEILING` | unset (off) | Ceiling probe, not shippable: set rebuilds the NVFP4 W4A8 MMQ (`-DMEMRA_MMQ_FOLD_CEILING=<v>`) with the per-k01 f32 scale fold collapsed to an s32 add. The output is numerically wrong by construction and exactness gates fail by design; it only measures the upper bound of fold-removal levers. | [prefill-gemm-20260806](../research/prefill-gemm-20260806/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_mmq_fold_ceiling) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_FP8BLK_PLAIN` | unset (block-scale identity form) | Rollback seam for the MMA form of the per-block FP8 prefill tile (`cu/mmq_fp8_blk.cu`). Unset: `kind::mxf8f6f4.block_scale` at the ue8m0 identity scale `0x7F7F7F7F`, the bit-identical e4m3 product at half the issue interval on sm_120a. `=1` rebuilds with plain `kind::f8f6f4`; `100a` builds always use the plain form. `MEMRA_MMQ_F8F4_PLAIN=1` is the same seam on the W4A8 F8F4 tile. | [rp-on-st-20260806](../research/rp-on-st-20260806/), [w4a8-prefill-20260806](../research/w4a8-prefill-20260806/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_mmq_fp8blk_plain) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_FP8` | unset: 256 (`FP8_MMQ_X`) | Compile-time seam for the per-block FP8 prefill tile's wide token width: `=<n>` rebuilds `cu/mmq_fp8_blk.cu` with `-DFP8_MMQ_X=<n>`. The launcher picks per call between this wide candidate and `FP8_MMQ_X_SMALL` (128) by wave fill. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fp8_mmq) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_IQEXP` | unset: 128 | Compile-time seam for the expert-segmented IQ MMQ token tile: `=<n>` rebuilds `cu/mmq_iq_experts.cu` with `-DMMQ_X=<n>`. Smaller tiles trade MMA reuse across tokens for more CTAs per SM. |  |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_Q4` | unset: 128 | Compile-time q4_0 MMQ token-tile seam: `=64` or `=96` rebuilds `cu/mmq_q4_0.cu` with `-DMMQ_X=<n>`. Same axis as the q45k and W4A8 seams; the tile is a template constant, so it needs a rebuild. |  |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_Q45K` | unset: the kernel's `MMQ_X` (128) | Compile-time sweep seam for the k-quant MMQ token tile: `=<n>` rebuilds `cu/mmq_q45k.cu` with `-DMMQ_X=<n>`. `=64` gives a 64-token tile (47 KB smem, 2 CTA/SM, against 57 KB and 1 CTA/SM). |  |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_X_W4A8` / `MEMRA_MMQ_Y_W4A8` | unset: 128 / 128 | Compile-time W4A8 MMQ tile sizes for `cu/mmq_nvfp4_w4a8.cu` (`-DMMQ_X=<n>`, `-DMMQ_Y=<n>`). The Y axis halves the row tile and the warp count together without duplicating weight reads. The defaults are the best measured on the RTX 5090; the seams stay as sweep levers for other silicon. |  |
| `MEMRA_MMVQ_ROWS` | `4` (a `#define` in `cu/qmatvec.cu`) | A compile-time constant, not an environment variable: the rows per block (one warp per output row on `threadIdx.y`) of the warp-per-row MMVQ matvec kernels in `cu/qmatvec.cu`. The Rust launch geometry hardcodes the same 4, so a change moves both together. | [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#build-and-binding) |
| `MEMRA_NVCC` | unset: `$CUDA_HOME`/`$CUDA_PATH`/`$CUDA_ROOT` `/bin/nvcc`, else the newest nvcc on `PATH` or `/usr/local/cuda*` | Path of the nvcc build.rs compiles with; a set value wins unvalidated. Unset, build.rs takes `$CUDA_HOME`, `$CUDA_PATH` or `$CUDA_ROOT` `/bin/nvcc`, then ranks every nvcc on `PATH` and under `/usr/local/cuda*` by reported release and takes the newest, so a stale distro nvcc never shadows a toolkit. The chosen path and release are echoed as a `cargo:warning`. |  |

## Command-line tools

Inputs of the command-line bins (`run-gen`, `run-spec`, `run-eagle`, `session-bench` and the probe bins) and the Hy3 single-card launcher: prompts, generation length, timing modes and profiler windows. These are bench inputs, not server settings; server launch lines are in the [cookbook](COOKBOOK.md). For drafter acceptance work, `MEMRA_FULL_PREC=1` gives the full-precision ceiling and `MEMRA_SPEC_STATS=1` prints the per-slot acceptance profile, and `tools/acceptance_battery.sh` drives both on a GPU.

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `run-spec --prime-walker-check` | off (switch omitted); decide-by: 2026-09-22 | Diagnostic for `MEMRA_PRIME_YIELD`; changes no serving dispatch. With the switch, run-spec first compares a supported GDN MTP prime against the unyielded trunk, advancing a separate cache between saved chunks, requires nonzero yields, identical final logits and matching boundary snapshot hashes, and prints `[prime-walker-check] PASS`; then it runs the ordinary spec comparison. Needs a multi-chunk prompt via `MEMRA_PROMPT_FILE`. It goes with its subject door on a no-go. | [MTP.md](../research/prefill-fairness-20260908/MTP.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_yield) |
| `MEMRA_ASYNC_CHAIN` | 0 (off) | `K>=2` enables the eager device-chained decode chunk (`decode_step_chain`) in `run-gen`, its only caller. Eligibility is plan-derived (`device_chain_plan_eligible`): standard residual, no Gemma/E4B, HyperConnections or pipeline cut, only Full/Linear mixers; MLA and KDA fail closed and an ineligible shape takes the single-token step. Greedy writes the tail argmax into resident `token_d`; sampled decode filters, perturbs and takes the argmax inside the chain, so neither host-syncs between tokens. With `MEMRA_HEAD_SPLIT=1` sampling draws from the split path's row (`head_split_sample_device`). `memra-server` does not read it. `0` is the rollback seam. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_async_chain) |
| `MEMRA_BENCH_DUMP` | unset (no files) | Bench instrument of `b200_matvec_bench`, never a serving flag: `=<dir>` writes each family's raw f32 outputs (`gate_up_shipped`, `gate_up_w4`, `gate_up_rp`, `down_shipped`, `down_w4`, `down_rp`, little-endian `.f32`) so two binaries can be compared bit for bit, which the in-process compare cannot do. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_bench_dump) |
| `MEMRA_CHAT` | unset (off) | Set to any value, a text prompt is wrapped in the model's chat template as one user turn plus the generation prompt (run-gen, run-spec, spec-econ, run-lockstep, dspark-q38-gate, verify-shape-bisect). The code tests presence, so `MEMRA_CHAT=0` wraps too. Unset feeds the raw text. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_AFFINITY` | `0-7` (in `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh`) | CPU list the Hy3 launcher passes as `GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY`; the process `taskset` list comes from `MEMRA_CPU_PROC_AFFINITY` (also `0-7`). Read only by the wrapper, never by the engine. A machine topology setting. |  |
| `MEMRA_CPU_PROC_AFFINITY` | `0-7` | `taskset --cpu-list` set for the Hy3 single-GPU launcher `tools/run_hy3_local_5090.sh`; `MEMRA_CPU_AFFINITY` (default `0-7`) is the companion's `GOMP_CPU_AFFINITY`. Shell-side. |  |
| `MEMRA_EAGLE` / `MEMRA_EAGLE_ALIGN=0` | `MEMRA_EAGLE` required by run-eagle; `MEMRA_EAGLE_ALIGN` on (shifted pairing) | EAGLE draft lane: `MEMRA_EAGLE=<eagle3 draft dir or .safetensors path>` is run-eagle's required draft input; `MEMRA_EAGLE_ALIGN=0` switches to the un-shifted MTP-style pairing for an A/B (`eagle.rs`). Experimental, not on the daily path. |  |
| `MEMRA_ECON_N` / `MEMRA_ECON_TMAX` / `MEMRA_ECON_ONLY` | 50 / 6 / unset (all arms) | `spec-econ` probe-bin controls for verify-economics runs: iterations per arm, maximum verify T, and a single-arm mode for nsys attribution (`decode_h` or `verify_tN`). | [verify-economics-20260802](../research/verify-economics-20260802/) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_ONLY` | off | Set to any value, run-spec skips prime-inclusive timing and reports gen-only. The code tests presence, so `0` arms it too. |  |
| `MEMRA_HIST_FILE` | required (unset panics) | `session-bench`: conversation history text file, tokenized with the model's tokenizer. |  |
| `MEMRA_LOAD_MTP` | unset (off) | `run-gen` directory-checkpoint input: `1` loads the embedded NextN/MTP heads instead of the trunk-only loader, so `MEMRA_SPEC_K` can exercise the attached chain (`run_gen.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_MAX_CTX` | unset (the run's own `prompt + generation` need) | `run-gen`: provision the KV and ring caches for N tokens instead of the run's own need; the value is clamped up to that need, so it never under-allocates. A serving-shaped perf number must state the provisioned context, since decode pays for the provisioned footprint. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_max_ctx) |
| `MEMRA_MSCALE_PROFILE` / `MEMRA_MSCALE_NOEAGER` / `MEMRA_MSCALE_INTERLEAVE` | unset (no profiler window, eager reference on, sequential arms) | `verify-mscale` probe-bin controls: `PROFILE` (any value) brackets the measured window in `cuProfilerStart`/`Stop`, `NOEAGER` (any value) skips the eager `decode_step` reference, `INTERLEAVE=1` warms every width and then alternates the arms. |  |
| `MEMRA_NGEN` | bin-specific: run-gen 16, run-spec 32, run-eagle 64; gates and probes 24 to 512 | Number of tokens to generate. Each bin carries its own fallback: run-gen 16, run-spec 32 (256 in prompt-directory mode), run-eagle 64; session gates, benches and probes take 24 to 400, and `gemma_gate`'s corpus mode 512. |  |
| `MEMRA_NMEASURE` | GGUF arm: 32; safetensors arm: 0 (off) | Decode tokens measured after the counter reset in `run-gen`. GGUF arm: the warm residency cache stays and only the KV cache is re-primed, isolating per-token H2D after warmup. Safetensors arm: `N>0` replays the same prompt and generated prefix for a repeatable steady-state run, and a forcing tape must cover decode plus N. |  |
| `MEMRA_NMEASURE_REPS` | 1 | run-gen steady-state benchmark repetitions; values below 1 clamp to 1 (`run_gen.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_PP_ONLY` | unset (any value arms it) | Prefill-only timing mode in `run-gen` (also the `fp8_mmq_stream` probe): warmups, timed reps, exit with no decode. The safetensors arm times `prime_cache`, the pass serving primes with and the only prefill class carrying the m>=16 GEMM/MMQ hooks (see `MEMRA_PP_NLL`); the GGUF arm times `forward_last` unless `MEMRA_PP_PRIME=1`. |  |
| `MEMRA_PP_PRIME` | unset (`1` arms) | GGUF arm of `run-gen`, under `MEMRA_PP_ONLY`: `1` runs the warmups, timed reps and the `MEMRA_PP_LOGITS` dump through `prime_cache` on a fresh cache (chunked over the quantized KV, as a served prompt primes) instead of `forward_last`, so a prime-path kernel is timed and byte-compared on the path it serves. | [gb10-prefill-attn-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-attn-20261107/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_pp_prime) |
| `MEMRA_PP_REPS` | 3 | Timed prefill repetitions under `MEMRA_PP_ONLY` (N-median protocol), in both `run-gen` arms and `fp8_mmq_stream`. |  |
| `MEMRA_PP_WARMUP` | 1 | Untimed prefill warmup runs before the `MEMRA_PP_ONLY` reps. A capped (spill-regime) MoE cache needs at least 2 to reach steady residency, since the SLRU ghost filter admits on the second miss. |  |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE=0` | on (run-gen runs the batched-prime gate) | `=0` skips run-gen's batched-prime gate, which compares `prime_cache`'s last-position logits (the config that seeds generation) against the tokenwise reference. A near-tie argmax flip is reported non-fatally; a wide-margin flip or out-of-bounds drift fails (bounds: `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE_MAXDIFF` / `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE_MARGIN`). Also skipped under `MEMRA_PRIME_TOKENWISE` or frozen CPU experts. Multi-prompt battery: `prime-gate <model> --prompts-file f [--chat] [--strict]`. | [prime-gate-coverage-20260802](../research/prime-gate-coverage-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_gate) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE_MAXDIFF` / `MEMRA_PRIME_GATE_MARGIN` | 8.0 / 1.0 | Verdict bounds of the batched-prime gate (run-gen and `prime-gate`): a logit maxdiff above `MAXDIFF` (or non-finite) is a structured failure; an argmax flip with tokenwise margin above `MARGIN` fails, below it is the accepted near-tie class. Unparsable values use the defaults. Recalibrate when the kernels under them move. | [prime-gate-coverage-20260802](../research/prime-gate-coverage-20260802/) |
| `MEMRA_PRINT_TEXT` | off | `1` makes run-spec decode the gold tokens between markers (and the sampled stream on a sampled run), for the agent-loop harness. Other values do nothing. |  |
| `MEMRA_PROFILE_GEN` | unset (no profiler brackets) | run-gen profiler window: `1` brackets the timed `generate_with` (prime included) in `cudaProfilerStart`/`Stop`; `2` starts capture at the decode loop (prime excluded). Pair with `nsys -c cudaProfilerApi`; cutting a whole-run capture by window misattributes the argmax-gate loop into decode shares. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_profile_gen) |
| `MEMRA_PROFILE_SPEC` | unset (no profiler brackets) | run-spec profiler window: `1` brackets `generate_spec` (prime included) in `cudaProfilerStart`/`Stop`; `2` starts capture at the round loop (prime excluded). Pair with `nsys -c cudaProfilerApi`. Phase isolation by subtraction does not work on MoE because the first prime cold-stages the expert cache. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_profile_spec) |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT` | unset: token ids from the command line, else `[55]` (run-gen, run-spec) | Prompt text, tokenized with the model's own tokenizer; `MEMRA_PROMPT_FILE` wins over it, and `--prompt` wins in run-gen. Unset, run-gen and run-spec take raw u32 ids from the command line. Probes and gates (`spec-st-probe`, `run-lockstep`, `verify-probe` and others) carry their own default text or ids. |  |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_DIR=<dir>` | unset | run-spec bench-sweep mode: iterates every `*.txt` prompt in the dir with one model load. `dspark_q38_gate` reads it too; `local-ci.sh` and `release-battery.sh` set it. |  |
| `MEMRA_PROMPT_FILE` | unset | Reads the prompt text from a file and wins over `MEMRA_PROMPT` (run-gen, run-spec, spec-econ, spec-serve-gate, fp8-mmq-stream). `replay-acceptance` requires it and also takes a directory. An unreadable file fails the run. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPEECH_THREADS` | `1` (single-threaded) | Worker threads for `memra-reference`'s CPU speech matrix product (the `whisper-stage`, `speech-text` and `rnnt-stage` reference executor), read once per process as a decimal count clamped to 1..64; garbage reads as 1. `1` runs every product on the calling thread. Above 1 each worker takes a disjoint block of output columns in the same ascending-k four-bank order, so the count cannot move a number (`column_blocking_is_bit_identical_so_thread_count_cannot_move_a_number`). A CPU work knob, not a serving door, no decide-by. Rollback: unset. | [REAL-AUDIO.md](../research/asr-modality-20260909/REAL-AUDIO.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_speech_threads) |
| `MEMRA_STOP` | EOS only | Comma-separated stop strings for run-gen; empty entries are dropped. Unset stops at EOS only. |  |
| `MEMRA_TURN_TOKENS` | 512 | `session-bench`: suffix length per turn in tokens, for the turn N+1 cost on a long-history session (suffix prime vs full re-prime). |  |

## Kernel arms and rollback seams

Arms the engine picks per model, card and shape for attention, GEMM and MMQ, MoE, the recurrent mixers (GDN, KDA, MLA, DSA), norms and fused ops, and CUDA graphs and streams. The default is the measured winner; a rollback seam keeps the reference path in the same binary behind its documented value (usually `=0`), and `MEMRA_FAST=0` takes the f32-dequant matvec class, the correctness oracle. What each kernel computes, its arch guard and the gate that pins it are in [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md).

### Attention

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_FA512_HP` | on (`=0` reverts) | The hd512 FA prefill runs the head-pair kernel. `=0` returns to the 2-warp sp16 kernel. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA512_MIN` | `512` | t_kv floor for the head_dim 512 vec lane, in eager and decode-graph dispatch: below it the scalar kernel's extra blocks hide latency better. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA512_SP` | on (`=0` reverts) | The hd512 FA prefill runs single-pass, with GEMM0 split-K across the two warps. `=0` takes the z=2 kernel that recomputes GEMM0 per O-half. Off whenever `MEMRA_FA512_STAGE=f32`. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA512_STAGE` | unset (bf16 staging) | Only `=f32`: the hd512 FA prefill stages in f32 with no bf16 pre-convert, which also turns off the `MEMRA_FA512_SP` single-pass arm. Bit-identical either way. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_BF16KV=0` | on (pre-converted bf16 K/V mirrors) | `=0` returns the hd128/hd256 prefill FA to in-kernel scalar K/V staging instead of pre-converted bf16 mirrors. Bit-identical: the same `__float2bfloat16` values reach the same MMA. The mirrors are also skipped under `MEMRA_FA_FLOOR`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fa_bf16kv) |
| `MEMRA_FA_COMBINE_S` | off; decide-by: 2026-11-17 | `1`: split-combine twin that stages `partM`/`partL` into dynamic shared memory once, cooperatively, instead of every thread walking them from global twice. Same `s`-ascending order for the max, the `l` sum and the `o` sum: bit-identical. No launcher or gate in this repo sets it. Missing gate: the battery on a 2-card pair plus a three-boot interleaved A/B. A pass by the date makes it the naked default and removes the door; no battery by the date deletes the door and its twin kernel `fa_decode_combine_gate_f32_s`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fa_combine_s) |
| `MEMRA_FA_EMIT=0` | on (bf16 emit straight from norm at `t >= 16`) | `=0` feeds f32 Q/K/V operands into prefill FA instead of the bf16 emit-straight-from-norm staging. The emit path needs `t >= 16` and is never used by island or vision primes or the portable MMA lane, which keep f32 regardless. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_F16PV` | plain serving: on; spec serving (`MEMRA_DRAFT` set): off | f16-P/V prefill class. Serving-keyed: off under spec serving, because f16 prime numerics shift the sub-argmax logit space the drafter feeds on. Any value other than `0` forces it on; `=0` forces it off. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fa_f16pv) |
| `MEMRA_FA_FLOOR=1` | unset (off) | Selects the prefill-FA floor kernel variant for the `fa_hd128_check` gate binary; it also skips the bf16 K/V mirrors (`MEMRA_FA_BF16KV`). Any set value arms it. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_I2` | on (`=0` reverts) | The Gemma global (hd512) rows kernel walks keys two at a time (i2 interleave). `=0` takes the single-key walk. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_PP_W2=1` | unset (off) | `1` uses a 2-warp, 32-row CTA tile for the f32 pp prefill FA: grid.x doubles with bit-identical per-row math, a pure coverage trade for low-occupancy shapes. While set it also disables the batched varlen FA arm (`lib.rs`, `hybrid_forward.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_ROWS_OFF` | unset (fused rows kernel) | Set (any value): verify FA runs the per-row loop instead of the fused rows kernel; the A/B and fallback seam. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_SMEM_TKV` | `1024` | t_kv at which FA decode switches to the smem-broadcast twin; `0` = never. fp8 layer classes (gemma GKV/WKV) are excluded because the smem V stage is q5_1-only. A value that does not parse keeps 1024. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_SP16=1` | unset (mixed ladder) | `1` switches the gemma FA-decode split ladder to t_kv-keyed {16, 64, 128} instead of the mixed default, where the model profile sets `fa_sp_gemma` (`lib.rs fa_split_keys`). Any value also makes the Step-3.5 FA fast path on 128+ SM cards decline (`hybrid_forward.rs`). Measured on 188-SM cards only; the 5090 ladder keeps its default. A probe seam. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_SP512` | per-model profile: `16` (base, gemma MoE), `32` (dense gemma) | Split count for gemma global attention (head_dim 512), sized for occupancy of the nkv=2 dpl16 grid. A value of 8 or more wins for every model; otherwise the model's numeric profile applies. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_SP_SHORT` | unset (the ladder's 16-key short rung) | Split-keys override for the short rung only (t_kv <= 2048 on parts with at least 128 SMs), which a global `MEMRA_FA_SPLIT` cannot isolate. Takes a multiple of 8, at least 8; other values are ignored. Numeric class (key partition): a default change needs a fresh tape plus the battery. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fa_sp_short) |
| `MEMRA_FA_SPLIT` | unset: ctx-adaptive, SM-aware split ladder | A whole number of keys, at least 8 and a multiple of 8, forces that FA-decode split at every depth; any other value is ignored and the ladder stays live. Unset, parts with at least 128 SMs take 16 keys up to t_kv 2048 (`MEMRA_FA_SP_SHORT` overrides that rung), 64 up to 16384 and 128 beyond; smaller parts start at 8 or 32 by KV-head count, then 64 and 128, and GB10 adds a 512-key deep rung. The split moves the combine's summation order, so near-tie greedy streams differ across GPUs: re-gate run-spec under a forced value. Any set value keeps `fa_decode_dcw_rows` on the per-row path. The k27 fast gate pins `MEMRA_FA_SPLIT=8` for a golden that holds on every GPU. | [FA-SPLIT-DEEP-RUNG.md](decisions/FA-SPLIT-DEEP-RUNG.md), [VERDICT.md](../research/k27-divergence-20260805/VERDICT.md), [KERNELS.md](KERNELS.md#cuflash_attncu), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fa_split) |
| `MEMRA_FA_SPW` | per-model profile: `32` (base, gemma MoE), `64` (dense gemma) | Split width for gemma windowed attention. A value of 8 or more overrides the model's numeric profile for every model; smaller or unparseable values keep the profile. It must stay uniform across decode and verify: a t-keyed split breaks combine-order parity. The optimum tracks SM count, so sweep {16, 24, 32, 48, 64} on a card with many more SMs than the 82-SM 5090 Laptop. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fa_spw) |
| `MEMRA_FA_SPW2` | on (`=0` reverts) | Warp-1 staging helper on the windowed v4 FA kernel, engaged at GQA up to 4 where v4 applies. `=0` disables it. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_TB512` | on (`=0` reverts) | The globals rows kernel runs t-batched: one block per (kv_head, split) stages its tile once and loops the verify rows, removing the per-row DRAM re-read. Its fixed absolute split grid is a numeric config shared by every hd512 caller. Engages at up to 32 splits and GQA up to 16. `=0` takes the per-row (grid.z) kernel. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fa_tb512) |
| `MEMRA_FA_V4` | on (`=0` was the rollback); no code read | Key-per-lane v4 FA decode lanes (with format-aware e4m3 arms) are the only path. `=0` used to select the pre-v4 lanes; the env read is gone. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_V4_MAX=<n>` | per numeric profile: unbounded on gqa <= 8 (v4 at every t_kv), 0 on gqa > 8 | `=<n>` overrides the FA decode v4/v3 crossover: v4 runs while `t_kv < n`, v3 at and above; `0` turns v4 off. A large value forces v4 at every t_kv for the fp8 lane matrix. It cannot arm v4 past the q-array capacity: a gqa > 8 launch stays on v3 whatever the value. Read once per process. |  |
| `MEMRA_FA_VEC_MIN` | `96` (per-model profile; gemma `1`) | t_kv floor for the FA vec lane. The per-model value, with `MEMRA_RMS_BLOCK` and the gqa>8 `MEMRA_FA_V4_MAX` threshold, lives in the model's numeric profile (`numeric_profile.rs`), installed on each model's thread, so every loaded model reads its own value in any load or call order. A parseable env value wins for every model. The hd256 v4 pick still checks the launch's gqa against the v4 capacity (`FA_V4_GQA_MAX` = 8), so no override can send a gqa > 8 model to the v4 kernels. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_fa_vec_min) |
| `MEMRA_FAW_HP` | on (`=0` reverts) | The SWA FA prefill runs the head-pair kernel. `=0` returns to the per-head p1 stamp. |  |
| `MEMRA_FAW_STAGE` | unset (bf16 staging) | Only `=f32`: the SWA windowed FA prefill stages in f32 with no bf16 pre-convert. Bit-identical either way. |  |
| `MEMRA_NO_FA_VEC` | unset (vec FA decode) | Set (any value): FA decode takes the scalar `fa_decode_f32` bit-reference instead of the vec class, in the eager, rows and graph paths in lockstep, so a graph captures the same path eager runs. |  |
| `MEMRA_NOFA` | unset | Set (any value): prefill attention uses naive SDPA instead of the hand-written FlashAttention; head dims with no stamped FA kernel take that fallback anyway. It also keeps the qwen prime graph off, and decode paths over a quantized KV cache refuse it by name. |  |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_APPEND_LOOP` | unset (batched `_rows` append) | Set (any value): prime KV appends run per row instead of the batched `_rows` kernel, and so does the MTP draft fill (`mtp_kv_fill_at`; its concat stays two strided copies). It also keeps the qwen prime graph off. |  |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_DEQW` | on (`=0` reverts) | Prefill FA dequantizes KV once into a bf16 workspace. `=0` takes the inline-dequant kernel and keeps the qwen prime graph off. |  |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_DEQW_DB` | on (`=0` reverts) | The prime's dequant-once FA workspace stages with a cp.async double buffer. `=0` takes the single-buffer twin and keeps the qwen prime graph off. |  |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_KV_H2` | unset follows the device: on for a unified-memory 120a-family device (GB10), off elsewhere | `1`/`0` decide on any device; any other value reads as unset. Selects the two-head (h2) quantized-KV prime attention twin at head-dim 256, t>=128 and even query-head groups: two query heads of one KV head share each staged K/V tile in an 8-warp CTA (64 KB smem, 1 CTA/SM), each row keeping its tile, QK/softmax/PV order and RN conversions, so outputs are bit-identical to the db twin. It is a db-twin variant, so `MEMRA_PRIME_DEQW_DB=0` turns it off too. On GB10 the route runs the h2p ring by default; plain h2 is `MEMRA_PRIME_KV_H2P=0`. Read once per process. Discrete cards are unmeasured and keep the db twin. Rollback: `0`. Gates: kernel-check ARC B cells at t>=128, `tests/prime_kv_h2_gpu.rs`. | [gb10-prefill-20261106](../research/gb10-prefill-20261106/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_kv_h2) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_KV_H2P` | unset follows the device: on for GB10 (unified-memory 120a), off elsewhere; decide-by: 2026-11-21 (discrete cards) | `1`/`0` decide on any device; another value reads as unset. Inside the h2 route only (`MEMRA_PRIME_KV_H2=0` turns both off) it selects `fa_prefill_qw_h2p`: h2's row program on a three-stage K/V ring handed over by per-stage full/empty mbarriers, the second head's warps one QK behind the first so one head's softmax overlaps the other's MMAs, `ldmatrix.shared` on precomputed per-lane offsets (98,368 B dynamic smem, 1 CTA/SM). Bit-identical to h2 and db. Read once per process. Rollback: `0`. Gates: kernel-check ARC B cells (the 4096 x 36864 ring cell included) in h2p, h2 and db modes; `MEMRA_PP_PRIME` prime-logit byte compare. | [gb10-prefill-attn-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-attn-20261107/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_kv_h2p) |
| `MEMRA_QKV_APPEND=0` | on (m=1 norm+rope+append fold) | `=0` runs the fused-norm-rope kernel plus a standalone KV append instead of the m=1 norm+rope+append fold: the exact-oracle bisect arm. Read once per process (`qkv_append_on()`). |  |
| `MEMRA_ROWS_TAB_RESTAGE` | on | On: `decode_v2_rope_fa_rows` builds its per-rank pointer table `{k, v, len, base, ctr, back}` from the live distributed cache and stages it into a persistent per-rank `[32,6]` u64 slab before every launch (one 96-byte `memcpy_htod` per rank per layer per verify round). This is the arm `MEMRA_FUSE_ROPE_APPEND=1` runs on sliding layers. `=0` restores the retired `ws.rows_tabs` memo, whose key cannot see the V and LEN pointers and is never invalidated when `MEMRA_REUSE_POOL` evicts a session: a proven use-after-free (non-finite verify rows or `CUDA_ERROR_ILLEGAL_ADDRESS`), not a slower correct path. Only exact `0` turns it off. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_rows_tab_restage) |
| Qwen FA2 prefill attention, no runtime door | on at `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK=1024` on a 170-SM sm_120a build (RTX 5090); any other chunk keeps the existing kernel | Memra-owned GQA-packed FA2-class quantized-KV prefill for 24 Q / 4 KV / d256, causal t=16..1039, short restored suffixes and widened 1024-row tails included (`Engine::prime_attn_fa2_enabled`). BF16 MMA, FP32 direct PV accumulation and a BF16-rounded softmax denominator make it its own numerical class, so it engages only at chunk 1024: a wider chunk would mix classes inside one prime and break cold/restored identity. Other builds, SM counts and shapes keep the existing kernel, with no environment read off sm_120a. Decode is unchanged; graphs key the class. The former door `MEMRA_PRIME_ATTN_FA2` is in the Removed doors ledger. | [QWEN-PRIME-FA2-DEFAULT.md](decisions/QWEN-PRIME-FA2-DEFAULT.md), [FA2.md](../research/qwen-prefill-attn-20260909/FA2.md), [fa2-receipt.json](../research/qwen-prefill-attn-20260909/fa2-receipt.json), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_chunk) |

### GEMM, MMQ, FP8 and NVFP4

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Dsv4Gpu::set_dense_wo_a_grouped_for_gate()` (process API) | ON; gate seam, decide-by: 2026-10-07 | DSv4 FP8 plain t=1 wo_a groups run as one launch instead of eight, with the same per-output accumulation and 128-leaf reduction; the grouped launch takes the grouped dense-fast twin whenever the per-group slices would take dense fast. `false` selects the eight per-group launches, which gates that pin the per-group program set. BF16, prefill and t>1 keep the loop. No environment or serving switch. | [RESULTS.md](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/latency/RESULTS.md), [wo-a-20260907.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/wo-a-20260907.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#dsv4gpuset_dense_wo_a_grouped_for_gate) |
| `MEMRA_B567` | on (`=0` reverts) | Verify matvecs at T=5..7 launch at exact width (b5/b6/b7 plus dual gate+up twins, NVFP4 split-plane trunk), bit-identical to the b8 launch by construction (columns past m never execute). `=0` takes the 8-wide b8 tier there. Needs the split-plane trunk and `MEMRA_B8` on. | [vt-fixes-20260803](../research/vt-fixes-20260803/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_b567) |
| `MEMRA_B8` | on (`=0` reverts) | Verify at m=5..8 uses the b8 batched tier. `=0` returns it to the per-m grid.y=m dispatch. |  |
| `MEMRA_BF16_MMV` | off; on for a W4A16 artifact that `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` places expert-parallel (per model) | At load, 2D BF16 checkpoint tensors of at least 2M elements stay bf16-resident (`GpuTensor::FloatBf16`: half the VRAM and decode read traffic); the MoE router stays exact f32. At decode (m=1, non-exact, non-canonical) they ride `matvec_bf16_f32acc` (f32 accumulate, deterministic tree reduce); m>1 prefill takes the chunked expansion, and step TP canonical-chunk ranks are excluded. Set, the env wins for every model (`1` on, anything else off); under `MEMRA_PARALLEL=auto` only `0`/`1` are accepted and `0` is the logged rollback. Numeric class: reduction order moves, weights do not; acceptance is the run-gen argmax gate plus a 12-boot battery. Load prints `[bf16-mmv] RESIDENT` per tensor. | [step37-bf16-mmv-20260828](../research/step37-bf16-mmv-20260828/), [BRINGUP.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/BRINGUP.md), [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_bf16_mmv) |
| `MEMRA_BF16_TCOLS_RED_FUSED` | off | `1`: every tcols dispatch (both grid forms and `matvec_bf16_tcols16_into`) launches its `_rf` fused-reduce-tail twin: the t trees share one barrier sequence in one shared region per token column, and levels s<=16 run as an intra-warp shuffle chain. Every level adds the same index pairs in the same operand order and the main loop is verbatim, so it is bit-identical to the standing twins. Needs a power-of-two `MEMRA_MMV_BLOCK` (the default 128 passes); any other block falls through to the standing tree. Read per call; unset or `0` is the standing program. Announces `[bf16-tcols-red-fused] engaged`. Gate: `glm5_matvec_doors_gpu.rs::gpu_red_fused_tcols_matches_standing_tree_bitwise`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/door-r-20260831/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_bf16_tcols_red_fused) |
| `MEMRA_BF16_TCOLS_WIDE` | on (any value but `0`) | `matvec_bf16_rows_into` at t=2..=16 rides the weight-once t-column class instead of the per-row `_rows` kernel that re-reads the weight for every token row: t<=8 through `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x4_tcols`, t=9..=16 through the separate `..._tcols16` twin (acc[16], so the t<=8 class keeps its register footprint). Placed after the W8-mirror intercepts. Bit-identical per (row, token) to the t=1 program. `0` restores the `_rows` program, read per call. Announces `[bf16-tcols-wide] engaged:` once per boot. Gate: `tests/glm5_matvec_doors_gpu.rs`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/matvec-20260831/LANE.md), [mv-battery-20260831](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/mv-battery-20260831/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_bf16_tcols_wide) |
| `MEMRA_BF16_TCOLS_X1` | on (any value but `0`) | The tcols dispatch (`matvec_bf16_tcols_into`, both the verify-walk route and `MEMRA_BF16_TCOLS_WIDE`'s) launches the one-row-per-block grid twin `matvec_bf16_f32acc_x1_tcols` (grid.x = out_f) instead of the 4-rows-per-block form, giving the trunk KDA shapes wave depth. Per-row body and red[256] tree verbatim: bit-identical per (row, token). `0` restores the x4 grid, read per call. Announces `[bf16-tcols-x1] engaged` once per boot. | `MEMRA_BVAR=<variant>` | unset (auto pick); decide-by: 2026-11-17 | Forces the batched-variant pick for the whole process across qtypes; the sm_90a re-tune sweeps this seam. A forced variant applies only when its layout matches the weight (rp variants on rp bytes, the rest on GGUF-layout bytes); a mismatch takes the auto pick. | `MEMRA_FP4_CUTLASS` (+build `MEMRA_CUTLASS`) | unset (off); needs an sm_120a build with `MEMRA_CUTLASS` | Routes m>=128 NVFP4 prefill inside the `MEMRA_FP4` path to the CUTLASS sm120 NVFP4 GEMM, folding the macro-scale into the epilogue alpha. Same W4A4 exactness block. `MEMRA_CUTLASS` is a build input, sm_120a only; other arches refuse at build. |  |
| `MEMRA_DOWN_X4` / `MEMRA_MMV_BLOCK` | `MEMRA_DOWN_X4` unset (off); `MEMRA_MMV_BLOCK` 128 | `MEMRA_DOWN_X4=1` assigns four short BF16 output rows per block when `in_f <= 2048`. `MEMRA_MMV_BLOCK` sets the BF16 matvec block size (64..256, a multiple of 32; anything else keeps 128). Both change kernel scheduling; promotion needs a fresh tape and the battery (`lib.rs`, `cu/qmatvec.cu`). |  |
| `MEMRA_DP4A_TILED` | GB10: on; elsewhere off; decide-by: 2026-11-21 | From 32 rows, below 128 outputs and for `in_f % 64 == 0` up to 8,192, `qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_rp_t4` runs one block per four tokens over every output row instead of the grid (out_f, m) launch; this covers the GDN beta/alpha projections. | `MEMRA_DSV4_DENSE_EXACT_TAIL` plus `memra_dsv4_dense_exact_tail_set_for_gate()` (process API) | on (unset), decide-by: 2026-09-22 | Unset or any value except exact `0` selects the M=1 exact-tree twins for admitted aligned operands; exact `0` keeps the original FP8 GEMV and F32-dot kernels. Per-leaf arithmetic, 128-thread geometry and reduction tree are unchanged. Engages on eager DSV4 M=1 calls and new graph captures, serving included; grouped or M>1 tails and unsupported shapes keep the control. Read once per host thread before its first enqueue: set `0` before workers start and build fresh graphs to roll back. Thread-local gate overrides take precedence; enqueue counters are not device replay proof. | [dsv4f-dense-exact-tail-20260908](../research/dsv4f-dense-exact-tail-20260908/), [dsv4f-compose-cadence-dense-20260908](../research/dsv4f-compose-cadence-dense-20260908/), [DESIGN.md](../research/dsv4f-cadence-dense-default-on-20260908/DESIGN.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_dense_exact_tail) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DENSE_FAST` | on (unset), decide-by: 2026-09-23 | Unset or exact `1` selects two-row FP8 LUT sharing and four-iteration dot load scheduling inside admitted dense exact-tail calls; `0` or any other string restores the previous exact-tail kernels. `MEMRA_DSV4_DENSE_EXACT_TAIL=0` still selects the legacy control. Same numeric class, token-identical to the prior default. Read once per host thread: to roll back set `0` before process start and build fresh uncaptured states, retained graphs keep their captured functions. | `MEMRA_E4M3_BATCH_R2` | off; decide-by: 2026-11-17 | `MEMRA_E4M3_BATCH_R2=1` runs the `_r2` twins of the batched F8-E4M3 matvec tier at mcols 2, 4 and 8 (`qmatvec_e4m3_mmvq_b{2,4,8}`): each warp owns two consecutive output rows and converts the q8_1 activation once for both, halving activation traffic per row; the weight stream is unchanged. Bit-identical by construction (gate `tests/e4m3_batch_r2_gpu.rs`); other widths fall through. Read per call; counter `E4M3_BATCH_R2_DISPATCHES`, announce `[e4m3-batch-r2] engaged`. | `MEMRA_F2B` | on (`=0` reverts) | Segmented-grid fusion of b-tier verify launches: qkv (all three on SWA layers, q and k on globals) and gate+up each run as one batched launch. `=0` takes separate per-tensor launches. Bit-exact per row (VERIFY-GATE 0.000e0). |  |
| `MEMRA_F32_GEMV_KERNEL` | on on sm_100a builds, off elsewhere | Every f32-resident `linear` at the decode and verify tier (`m <= 16`, `in_f % 1024 == 0`, `out_f <= 65535`) takes the native `gemv_f32_rows` kernel instead of cuBLASLt's dot plus reduce pair, and `linear_decode_exact` (verify rows, m > 1) takes one m-row launch instead of m per-column launches. | `MEMRA_IQ_FAST` | on (`=0` reverts) | Non-expert IQ4_XS matmuls ride the dp4a program at every m, so decode, verify and prefill share one program and dispatch parity holds by construction. `=0` returns them to the Stage-A f32 oracle. | `MEMRA_MMQ_F8F4_V3` | on for a unified-memory `120a` build (GB10), off elsewhere | Rollback seam of the F8F4 v3 mainloop: `1`/`0` decide on any device; unset follows the device like `MEMRA_MMQ_F8F4_V2`. | `MEMRA_MMQ_RESIDUAL_K=<k>` (default 0) | 0 (off) | Keeps the k largest-amax activation channels of the W4A4 path in f32: they are zeroed before quantization and their exact contribution is added back as a rank-k update. Any value other than `0` forces entry; `=0` forces tiling. `MEMRA_MMQ_SK_FORM` pins the numerical form. A hardware-specific SK default needs its own exact interleaved gate. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_mmq_sk) |
| `MEMRA_MMQ_SK_FORM=sk\|tile` | unset (xy-tile form on every device) | Pins the q4_0 MMQ numerical form for measurement or rollback: a value starting with `s` selects stream-k, anything else xy-tile. | `MEMRA_MMVQ_TC` | unset: the boot wave probe picks; decide-by: 2026-11-17 | Exact tensor-core b32 matvec tier. | `MEMRA_PP_Q4MMQ` | on (`=0` reverts) | Q4_0 prefill uses the int8-MMA MMQ. `=0` takes the hand-rolled `qmatvec_gemm_q4_0[_rp]` tiling GEMM. |  |
| `MEMRA_PP_Q8MMQ` | on (`=0` reverts) | Q8_0 prefill uses the int8-MMA MMQ. `=0` takes the hand-rolled `qmatvec_gemm_q8_0` tiling GEMM. |  |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_RESID_EPI` | on (`=0` reverts); decide-by: 2026-11-21 | Eager fp32 prime: where an output projection (attention `wo`, GDN `ssm_out`, the dense FFN down on the `MEMRA_FFN_GATEUP_FUSE` path) takes the F8F4 v2 tile, its epilogue adds the residual (`memra_mmq_nvfp4_f8f4_v2_preq_resid`, the `add_f32` bits) and the following norm reads the sum, so the projection's f32 output is never written. Off on the f16 prime and under the carried-prime graph. Bit-identical (kernel-check `MMQ-F8F4-V2-RESID`). `=0` restores the f32 write and the `add_rms_norm` read-back. | [gb10-prefill-next-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-next-20261107/), [GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_resid_epi) |
| `MEMRA_Q40_MR=1\|2` | mr1 at the plain m=1 site; fused t=1 per numeric profile (dense gemma mr1, MoE mr2) | Overrides q4_0 rows-per-thread at both the plain m=1 site and the fused t=1 site; the env wins over the numeric profile's `fused_mr1` (`lib.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_Q4F16` | capacity-keyed auto | f16 prefill mirrors on the cuBLASLt lane for dense gemma (12B/31B geometry), the sm_120a sibling of the Hopper `MEMRA_PP_F16` class. Unset: mirrors are admitted when free VRAM covers the f16 mass of every 2D tensor `build_q8_f16` admits (Q8_0/Q4_0/Q6_K/Q4_K/Q5_K) plus 8 GiB headroom, so 24 GB cards refuse by construction; `MEMRA_PP_F16` also turns them on. `1` forces them (the f16 lane must be compiled in), `0` turns them off, and any other value refuses the load because it selects prefill arithmetic. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_q4f16) |
| `MEMRA_Q4RP` | on (`=0` reverts) | Q4_0 decode reads split-plane repacked mirrors built at load. `=0` keeps the GGUF layout. |  |
| `MEMRA_Q5K_ISSUE` | on (`=0` reverts) | The m=1 q5_K MMVQ runs the interleaved-issue bodies (at mr=1 above 65,536 outputs). `=0` takes the reference bodies; `=2` forces the interleaved body at the current mr for every shape (A/B probe). |  |
| `MEMRA_Q80_BATCH_R2` | off; decide-by: 2026-11-17 | `MEMRA_Q80_BATCH_R2=1` runs the `_r2` / `_r2_rp` twins of the batched Q8_0 matvec tier at mcols 2, 4 and 8 (`qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq_b{2,4,8}` and their `_rp` split-plane forms, the MLA/DSA projections at the verify width): each warp owns two output rows and loads the q8_1 activation once for both dp4a chains. Bit-identical by construction (gate `tests/q80_batch_r2_gpu.rs`); other widths fall through. Read per call; counter `Q80_BATCH_R2_DISPATCHES`, announce `[q80-batch-r2] engaged`. Owner's call by the date: promote or delete. Rollback: unset. | [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_q80_batch_r2) |
| `MEMRA_Q80_G2=0` | on (`_rp_g2` 2-rows-per-block twin) | `=0` uses the one-row-per-block q8_0 `_rp` matvec instead of the `_rp_g2` twin. The twin engages at m=1, `out_f >= 64`, when the halved grid still fills the SMs. Bit-identical. |  |
| `MEMRA_Q80_MR=2` | 1 (mr1) | `2` selects the q8_0 rp 2-rows-per-thread mr2 kernel. Measured negative on H100: halving the grid on 132 SMs costs more than the 2-row ILP buys. Kept for the corpus (`lib.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_Q8_DUAL` | on (`=0` reverts) | q8_0 gate+up matvecs fuse into one launch. `=0` takes two separate launches. |  |
| `MEMRA_Q8_FFN_FUSE2` | on (`=0` reverts) | The Q8_0 dense-FFN gate+up pair at m=1 runs as one `fused2` launch in `matmul_pre_dual_noscale`, bit-identical (its body is `qmatvec_q8_0_mmvq` per tensor and row). `=0` takes two `matmul_pre_noscale` launches. It bails when a `MEMRA_Q8RP` split-plane mirror exists, since fused2 has no `_rp` twin. | [q27-deepdive-20260805](../research/q27-deepdive-20260805/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_q8_ffn_fuse2) |
| `MEMRA_Q8RP` | capacity-keyed: on for `90a`; elsewhere on iff free VRAM covers the mirror mass + 8 GiB | Q8_0 split-plane decode mirrors built at load, a bandwidth lever only. Unset, a `90a` build always builds them and other builds only when free VRAM (engine 0's on sharded trunks) covers the mirror mass plus 8 GiB of serving headroom, so 24 GB cards refuse by construction. `=0` forces off, any other value forces on. A no-op on FP8-ST checkpoints: `build_q8_rp4` accepts only `QT_Q8_0`. | [rp-on-st-20260806](../research/rp-on-st-20260806/), [batched-tick-inc3-20260801](../research/batched-tick-inc3-20260801/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_q8rp) |
| `MEMRA_RP` | on (`=0` reverts) | NVFP4 weights load as A6 split-plane repacks. `=0` keeps the GGUF layout; it is also required for the W4A4 door, since an rp weight always takes W4A8 (`w4a4-gate` refuses without it). |  |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_DUAL_T` | on (`=0` reverts) | The NVFP4 verify FFN gate+up pair at t=2..4 runs as one dual-twin launch, bit-identical per (tensor, token, row) by construction (kernel-check DUAL-BATCHED, both layouts). `=0` takes two single launches. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_spec_dual_t) |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_FUSED_T` | on (`=0` reverts) | The verify trunk at t=2..4 fuses the per-tensor decode-exact calls into one launch, bit-identical per (tensor, token, row) by construction (kernel-check Q8-FUSED2-B/FUSED3-B). `=0` takes the per-tensor calls. |  |
| `MEMRA_SPEC_M2` | on (`=0` reverts) | Verify at t=2 uses the small-m batched twin. `=0` takes the per-m grid.y=m dispatch. |  |
| `MEMRA_TCOL_TWINS` | off, decide-by: 2026-11-18 | Strict, read once: unset or `0` is off, `1` is on, any other value refuses the load of a model with step TP layers. `1` sends the step TP T-column bf16 launchers (`MEMRA_STEP_TP_BATCH` walk and verify walk) to the compile-time-T twins `matvec_bf16_qkvg_tcol_t{2,4,8}` / `matvec_bf16_b4_tcol_t{2,4,8}` at t = 2, 4 and 8; other widths keep the runtime-T kernel. The `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8` family default takes the q8_0 mirrors first, so a step37 model reaches the twins only with `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8=0`. The first twin launch prints `[tcol-twins] ENGAGED`. Off because the kernel would change as a request's batch width crosses 2, 4 or 8, which has no serving-shape proof. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_tcol_twins) |
| `MEMRA_TK_FA_GROUP` | on (`=0` reverts) | qwen35 t-parallel full-attention q/k/v projections run in one group4 grid (n3=0) instead of three single batched launches, bit-identical per (tensor, token, row) like the group4 door, with the same eligibility mirror. `=0` takes the three launches. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_tk_fa_group) |
| `MEMRA_TK_FFN_DUAL` | on (`=0` reverts) | The qwen35 t-parallel verify FFN runs the q27 verify chain: `matmul_decode_exact_dual_pre` + `silu_mul_scaled_q8_1` + `matmul_decode_exact_pre`, each door bit-identical or value-exact on its own receipts. `=0` takes two singles plus `silu_mul` and a standalone quantize. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_tk_ffn_dual) |
| `MEMRA_TK_GDN_GROUP` | on (`=0` reverts) | The qwen35 t-parallel GDN in-projection 4-tuple (wqkv, wqkv_gate, ssm_beta, ssm_alpha) runs in one `qmatvec_nvfp4_mmvq_group4_b*_rp` grid, bit-identical to the four singles. Split-plane NVFP4 only, `out_f % 8 == 0` for every member, m=2..16; the b16 form also serves the exact-16 decode-batch trunk. `=0` takes four single launches. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_tk_gdn_group) |

### MoE

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `set_moe_f16g_gu_half2_for_gate()` / `set_moe_f16g_down_m1_half2_for_gate()` (process API) | OFF, decide-by: 2026-09-21 | Measurement-only ModelOpt packed half2 stores in GU and M1-down. OFF keeps the scalar dequant loader; clearing the overrides is the rollback. The plain `half2` cell checks both enqueue counts, tokens, final logits and committed KV. Kept for controlled composition, not a serving flip. No environment or serving switch. | [expert-half2-20260907.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/expert-half2-20260907.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#set_moe_f16g_gu_half2_for_gate) |
| `set_moe_m1_splitk_for_gate()` (process API) | OFF, decide-by: 2026-09-21 | M=1 ModelOpt GU/down with adaptive 1-16 K slices and fixed-order f32 reduction (`moe_m1_adaptive_splitk_f32_fixed_order`). ON selects the host-adaptive two-pass family ahead of the graph default; OFF releases the override. Rollback: drain ranks and call `Dsv4Gpu::set_grouped_m1_splitk_for_gate(false)`. No environment or serving switch. | [RESULTS.md](../research/dsv4f-moe-m1-splitk-20260907/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#set_moe_m1_splitk_for_gate) |
| `MEMRA_EXACT16_MOE` | unset (off) | `1` admits the exact-width-16 MoE decode tier. It is byte-safe but slower than two width-8 waves, so it stays an explicit measurement door; without it the tier refuses at the MoE FFN layer (`decode_batch.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_F16G_BDB` | OFF | Double-buffered B tile in the direct-from-quant 128-row MoE GEMM (`moe_kq_sk128v_kernel<QT, BDB>`). `1` uses two B buffers and one barrier per k-block, issuing the next block's dequant behind this block's mma. Same tiles and accumulation order, so it gates bitwise through kernel-check. Smem 63 to 72 KB with occupancy unchanged. Both instantiations stay compiled. Rollback: unset or `0`. Off until its A/B and bitwise gate land. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_f16g_bdb) |
| `MEMRA_F16G_DIRECT=0\|kq` | on for Q4_K, Q6_K, IQ4_XS, IQ3_S and NVFP4 | Rollback for the direct-from-quant sk tile loaders, which dequant weight tiles in-register from the quant superblocks so the per-(layer, projection) dequant pass never runs. `0` returns every class to the dequant-workspace path; `kq` keeps only the Q4_K/Q6_K loaders. Bit-identical to the workspace path (kernel-check `f16g-kq-direct`): a data-movement change, not a numeric class. Grid-scan (`MEMRA_F16G_SK=0`) and Q3_K/Q4_0 keep the workspace path. | [kquant-tile-loaders-20260802](../research/kquant-tile-loaders-20260802/), [h100-sk-direct-20260802](../research/h100-sk-direct-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_f16g_direct) |
| `MEMRA_F16G_GU_FUSE` | OFF (research-only) | DSV4 matrix plain-decode gate/up plus weighted SwiGLU fusion for one-row CSR transactions, keeping the ModelOpt NVFP4 f16-MMA chain and the FP8 down and scatter. `1` arms it; unset or `0` runs the two-projection rollback. `set_moe_f16g_gu_fuse_for_gate` overrides it in-process for ABBA gates. The GU-M1 work-elision and GU-half2 candidates are separate process-local overrides; with both on, the `<108,true,true>` composition runs once and both counters record that enqueue. Serving admission pending. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_f16g_gu_fuse) |
| `MEMRA_F16G_M1_TC` | OFF (research-only) | DSV4 matrix plain-only m_e=1 tensor-core tail candidate. `1` arms it. `set_moe_f16g_m1_tc_for_gate` is the gate-only setter `dsv4_decode_rate_gate m1-tc-compose-ab` uses: it holds GU fusion on and toggles only this work-elision arm, with the FP8 mirror, macro2 and scatter in both arms. Not the removed scalar visitor. Off pending identity, sanitizer, engagement and interleaved rate gates. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_f16g_m1_tc) |
| `MEMRA_F16G_SK=0\|32\|128` | unset: hybrid split at `MEMRA_F16G_SK_CROSS`; DSV4 loads: the `32` form | Mode-2 sk kernel form: a persistent problem-visitor over the real CSR tiles with a 32x64x32 2-stage form and a 128x64x64 3-stage cp.async form (sm_80-portable mma.sync, no wgmma or TMA). `0` = the round-49 grid-scan kernel (rollback seam; it refuses the DSV4 matrix program); `32` or `128` forces one form; unset splits at the crossover. The 128 form's dynamic smem opt-in falls back to the 32 form when the device cannot fit it. Every form is byte-identical (kernel-check `f16g-sk`). | [sk-bm128-20260801](../research/sk-bm128-20260801/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_f16g_sk) |
| `MEMRA_F16G_SK_CROSS=<m>` | 64 | Visitor crossover: groups with `m_e >= m` ride the 128x64x64 form, smaller ones the 32x64 form. A tune seam; ignored under an explicit `MEMRA_F16G_SK` (`0`, `32` or `128`) and on DSV4's default `32` form (`lib.rs`). | [sk-bm128-20260801](../research/sk-bm128-20260801/), [h100-flip-full-20260802](../research/h100-flip-full-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_f16g_sk_cross) |
| `MEMRA_F16G_TAIL=0` | on (deep tail) | Rollback: sub-crossover sk visitor groups go back to the round-51 2-stage 32x64x32 tile. The default deep tail is 32x64x64 3-stage cp.async with the same 32-row tile, in both the workspace and direct-from-quant variants; static smem, no opt-in, 2 CTA/SM on sm_120a. `in_f % 64 != 0` falls back in the launcher. Byte-identical to every other sk form (kernel-check `f16g-sk`, `f16g-kq-direct`). | [sk-tail-form-20260802](../research/sk-tail-form-20260802/), [h100-flip-full-20260802](../research/h100-flip-full-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_f16g_tail) |
| `MEMRA_FUSED_ROUTER=0` | on (fused device router) | `=0` falls back to host softmax plus sort routing through the single-sync pinned readback (softmax routers only), and also disables `MEMRA_MOE_DEV` resident dispatch. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_DEC` | on (`=0` reverts) | Expert dots decode each weight row group once and dp4a across the expert's tokens. `=0` takes the `_em` per-token re-decode dot. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_DEV=0` | on (zero-DtoH resident expert dispatch) | `=0` disables zero-DtoH resident expert dispatch: softmax routers return to host routing; enabled sigmoid routers keep device selection but read `[sel,w]` through the pinned host stage. `MEMRA_FUSED_ROUTER=0` also turns it off. Read once per process. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_dev) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_DEVQ8_GU` / `MEMRA_MOE_DEVQ8_DOWN` | unset (auto: GU `v`, down `w8h2v` at `in_f == 512`, `n_used <= 8`) | Force a dev-q8 kernel variant by name for the expert gate-up (`v`, `s2`, `s2z`, `j8`, `vsm`, `vsm2`, `sg`, `j8sg`, `u64`, `gs4`, `1`/`2`/`4`) and down (`w8h2v`, `w8h2`, `w8h2r2`, `w8h2r2v`, `h2`, `1`/`2`/`4`) passes. Unset picks the measured winners; an unknown or shape-ineligible name falls to the generic kernel. Read once per process. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_DEVQ8_WPB` | 4 (clamped to 1..16) | Warps per block for the dev-q8 gate-up `_r` twins (`MEMRA_MOE_DEVQ8_GU` set to `1`, `2` or `4`). Probe knob; read once per process. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_EXPERT_RP` | unset (off) | Strict `=1`: at resident-slab upload a layer's three NVFP4 expert slabs are repacked on device into the slot-major `QT_NVFP4_V2` layout; expert dots (`expert_dot_g` on the pinned `expert_dot_nvfp4_core`) and the grouped prefill read it bit-exactly. Readers not handed the V2 qtype refuse by name (`moe_rp_refuse`). A layer is refused unless gate/up/down are plain NVFP4 with no block-FP8 scales and `in_f % 256 == 0`. Kept as infrastructure for a slot-major re-mint, not a speed lever. Bit gate: `b200_matvec_bench` `w4_v2` arms with `MEMRA_BENCH_DUMP`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_expert_rp) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_F16G=0\|1\|2\|3` | mode `2` on every arch (silu sites); the gemma (gelu) site off unless set | Grouped f16 expert prefill: dequant the active experts once per (layer, projection), one grouped GEMM over the CSR groups. `2` (unset) is the single-kernel grouped m16n8k16 GEMM on the engine stream with the sk visitor forms and direct tile loaders, no syncs; `1` is cublasGemmGroupedBatchedEx with a per-projection sync, declined by name on the glm5 sigmoid grouped prefill; `3` is auto-kquant, mode 2 only where the int8-MMA MMQ arm rejects the layer (rollback seam); `0` kills; other values mean `1`. A t floor of 16 keeps decode and verify on dp4a. Covers IQ4_XS, IQ3_S, Q4_0, Q3_K, Q4_K, Q6_K and NVFP4 (block 64). | `MEMRA_MOE_FUSED_EPI` | unset (off) | glm5_next fused MoE epilogue: the sigmoid `noaux_tc` router's host-selected `[sel,w]`, the pre-clamped SwiGLU and the per-expert NVFP4 `weight_scale_2` fold run as one launch pair per token-layer (`moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8` + `moe_down8_fma_q8`), on a resident slab or through SLRU, which fails closed to the sequential loop when the cache holds fewer than `3*n_used` slots. Any value other than `0` arms it; read per call. Exact against `memra-reference`; no throughput receipt yet. Gate: `crates/memra-engine/tests/glm5_moe_epilogue_gpu.rs`. | [moe-epilogue-receipts](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/moe-epilogue-receipts/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_fused_epi) |

Blocked and known unsafe: with it on, `serve-smoke` fails the Q35 mixed c=4 exact-token gate (early-EOS truncation), and the resident-KAT transfer gate is much slower on the RTX PRO 6000. Kept only because `tools/step35-b2-geometry-gate.sh` boots with `MEMRA_MOE_GROUPED=1`; no decide-by while that gate sets it. Resident uniform banks keep the exact expert-major q8 research arm; mixed and spill banks keep metadata-aware grouping. | [groupedregate-20260813](../research/groupedregate-20260813/), [leverC-20260808](../research/leverC-20260808/), [grouped-serve-20260810](../research/grouped-serve-20260810/), [27bab-20260810](../research/27bab-20260810/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_grouped) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_GROUPED_PREFILL` | on (`=0` reverts) | glm5_next grouped MoE prefill: tokens sorted by expert (host CSR), then one grouped NVFP4 tensor-core GEMM per projection per layer-chunk over the local resident slab (`moe_f16_grouped`), with routing bit-identical to the sequential arm, the pre-clamped SwiGLU and the per-expert `weight_scale_2` fold. Engages only at prefill with t > 16, a local resident slab and f16g-walkable projections; every other shape fails closed to the per-token loop. `=0` reverts, read per call. Gate: `crates/memra-engine/tests/glm5_moe_grouped_prefill_gpu.rs`. | [moe-grouped-prefill-receipts](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/moe-grouped-prefill-receipts/), [PREFILL-GAP.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/prefill-gap-20260829/PREFILL-GAP.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_grouped_prefill) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_MMA=0` | on (int8-MMA expert MMQ at `t >= 16`) | `=0` keeps expert prefill on dp4a. The int8-MMA expert MMQ engages only at `t >= MEMRA_MOE_MMA_T` (default 16, so verify stays on dp4a) on an MMA-capable shape. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_MMA_T=<n>` | 16 | MMA t-floor override for the int8-MMA expert MMQ and the grouped f16 lane; a bisect seam. Below 16 it puts spec verify on MMA, which breaks dispatch parity (verify must stay dp4a), so measurement only (`hybrid_forward.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_PAIRS=0` | on (pair-batched expert launches) | `=0` runs the per-expert loop for real prefill instead of pair-batched launches. The pair path also stands down while `MEMRA_MOE_STATS` is set. |  |

| `MEMRA_MOE_Q8_KQ` | on (`=0` reverts) | The k-quant arms are in the q8 expert dot set. `=0` excludes them. |  |
| `MEMRA_MOE_Q8_NVFP4=0` | on for activation-quantized NVFP4 artifacts | Rollback: activation-quantized NVFP4 experts leave the q8 dp4a dot for the f32 arm. An artifact declaring ModelOpt `W4A16_NVFP4` never takes the q8 arm: its experts use BF16-rounded activations through the model-scoped `qmatvec_view` wrapper, since q8_1 there would turn W4A16 into W4A8. | `MEMRA_MOE_SLAB` | on (`=0` reverts) | When local resident slabs exist, dispatch takes the slab-local sequential arm, which also serves sigmoid-router archs (step35, M3, Hy3).
