Slab and SLRU dispatch are bit-identical. `=0` takes SLRU dispatch. | [pp-leverb-20260807](../research/pp-leverb-20260807/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_slab) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_DEV_TABLES` | off | `1`: the verify-rows MoE pair builds its `[3*n_pairs]` pointer/scale tables on device (`moe_vrows_tables_from_sel`) from the sigmoid router's device selection, and the layer routes through `moe_router_sigmoid_topk` instead of the host router, dropping a stream sync, 2 DtoH and 2 HtoD per MoE layer call. Exact term for term with the host build. Arms only where the vrows arm fires, `sigmoid_router_enabled()` holds (`MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER=0` keeps the host oracle) and no host reader of the selection is on (`moesd` capture, `hidden_trace`, `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE`, `MEMRA_MOE_STATS`, `MEMRA_MOE_WEIGHT_TRACE`, `MEMRA_MOE_INPUT_TRACE_DIR`). Read per call; unset or `0` restores the host build. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/moe-loc-20260831/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_vrows_dev_tables) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_DOWN_TMAJ` | off | `1`: the verify-rows MoE down launch takes `moe_down8_fma_q8_rows_tmaj`, grid transposed to `(t, out_f)` with the token fastest, so a repeated expert's down row is read once for every token sharing it. The slot-ordered `__fmaf_rn` chain keeps its original slot order inside the block; only the grid moves, so it is bit-identical and takes no order plane. `out_f > 65535` falls closed. Read per call at the launcher. Announces `[moe-vrows-down-tmaj] engaged`; counter `MOE_VROWS_DOWN_TMAJ_DISPATCHES`. Gate: `glm5_dedup_sched_gpu`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/dedup-20260831/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_vrows_down_tmaj) |
| `MEMRA_MOE_VROWS_ILP` | on for `100a` builds, off elsewhere (`MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH`); `1`/`0` override either way | The verify-rows MoE pair (`moe_gate_up_preclamp8_q8_rows` + `moe_down8_fma_q8_rows`, also the served t=1 MoE arm under `MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV`) launches its `_ilp` twin: the same per-warp program with each lane's next groups' loads issued ahead of their math. Exact by construction: same accumulation order, same bytes, one pinned core. NVFP4 layouts only (`QT_NVFP4`, `QT_NVFP4_V2`); other qtypes refuse by name (`[moe-vrows-ilp] REFUSED`) and keep the shipped kernel. Read per call. The receipt is GLM-5.3-Flash on sm_100a only; another sm_100a family inherits it without one. Announces `[moe-vrows-ilp] engaged`. Gate: `glm5_matvec_doors_gpu`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_moe_vrows_ilp) |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM` | on | Strict `0`/`1`; any other value keeps the default and prints `[nvfp4-door] WARN`. ON builds the step TP NVFP4 expert banks (gate, up, down) in the slot-major row layout and dispatches `qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel_v2`, a byte permutation of block_nvfp4 that is bit-identical per (expert, row); OFF is the v1 banks with `qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel`. One coupled default with `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8`, which engages only on slot-major banks, so `0` (the rollback seam) disarms both. The layout is read once at bank build; the grouped prime refuses if gate, up and down disagree. Gate: `nvfp4-bank-oracle` plus the greedy tape (decode-only byte gates prove nothing here). Sub-door: `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_SM_STREAM`. | [RESULTS.md](../research/step37-bankv3-20260901/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_nvfp4_bank_sm) |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_DOWN8` | on | Strict `0`/`1`: fuse the routed DOWN sweep with the route-weight combine in one launch (`qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel_v2_down8`, one warp per routed slot), bit-identical to `qmatvec_nvfp4_sel_into` plus `axpy_rows_seq_md_into`. Engages only on the device-routed arm (`MEMRA_STEP_NVFP4_DEV_ROUTES=1`) with a slot-major down shard (`MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM`), `nsb = local_in >> 5 <= 32` and `n_sel <= 8`; default ON and engaged are two facts, and the `[nvfp4-sweep] down8=... door=... door_source=...` line settles engagement. Rollback: `=0`, or `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM=0` for both. Successor to `MEMRA_SEL_DOWN8`, which refuses boot. | [RESULTS.md](../research/step37-bankv3-20260901/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_nvfp4_sel_down8) |
| `MEMRA_NVFP4_SEL_SM_STREAM` | off | `1` runs the 8-rows-per-block streaming twin (`qmatvec_nvfp4_dp4a_sel_v2s`): each row is one contiguous stream with next-row register prefetch, and the per-row program equals `_sel_v2`, so it is bit-identical. The launcher gates it on `row_bytes % 16 == 0 && in_f <= 4096`; other geometries fall back to the single-row kernel. Sub-door of `MEMRA_NVFP4_BANK_SM`, inert without the slot-major layout (on by default). Unpriced: do not arm it without its own measurement. Unset is the single-row `_sel_v2`. Related names: `MEMRA_SEL_V2S`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_nvfp4_sel_sm_stream) |
| `MEMRA_ROUTER_BATCH=0` | on (8x8 register-tiled batch router at `t >= 8`) | `=0` runs the per-(expert, token) router GEMV at every t instead of the 8x8 register-tiled batch twin at `t >= 8`. Perf-only rollback seam, not a numeric config: the twin is bit-identical per row to `router_gemv_f32_w8` (kernel-check m=1..2048 sweep), so it never changes output bits. | [fast-router-20260802](../research/fast-router-20260802/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_router_batch) |
| `MEMRA_ROUTER_V2=0` | on (8-warp smem-reduce router GEMV) | `=0` uses the lone-warp router GEMV instead of the 8-warp smem-reduce twin; any other value forces the twin. Numeric-config class: the twin has a different FP order, arbitrated per model on the battery. | [g26-decode-20260801](../research/g26-decode-20260801/) |
| `MEMRA_SHEXP_DOT=0` | on (`sigmoid_dot_rows_f32`) | `=0` computes the MoE shared-expert gate as a cuBLASLt m=1,n=1 GEMM plus a separate sigmoid. The default is one fused 8-warp block-reduce dot plus sigmoid launch, wired into every decode-class arm so dispatch choice cannot change bits; prefill rides it too (see `MEMRA_ROUTER_PREFILL_EXACT`). Numeric-config class, as `MEMRA_ROUTER_V2`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_shexp_dot) |
| `MEMRA_SIG_EXPF_DEV` | unset (off) | Router numeric-class probe: `1` makes the sigmoid router use the device-libm `expf` twin instead of the host-transcribed sigmoid (`lib.rs`, `cu/moe_router.cu`). A new tape plus the full battery come before any promotion. |  |
| `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER=0` | device sigmoid routing | `=0` is the host oracle: full-logit DtoH plus host sigmoid, bias and sort routing for Step-3.7, M3, Hy3 and GLM-DSA. The default runs sigmoid, active-mask filtering, stable top-k, normalization and scaling on device; Step-3.7's uniform local resident-q8 arm consumes `[sel,w]` with zero router DtoH, other cases keep the pinned `[sel,w]` readback. Boot probe of host `expf`: a mismatch is fatal under `=0`, a warning on the device default (device uses a vendored scalar `expf`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_sig_router) |

### GDN, KDA, MLA and DSA

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_GDN_CHUNKED` | on (`=0` reverts) | GDN prefill runs the chunked WY scan; `MEMRA_GDN_CHUNK` sets the chunk size (default 32, clamped to 32..128 in steps of 32). `=0` takes the sequential scan. |  |
| `MEMRA_GDN_CONV_L2` | GB10: on; elsewhere off; decide-by: 2026-11-21 | Inside the `MEMRA_GDN_TRAFFIC` set, on the compact layout at d_state 128: the GDN state conv runs the q/k L2 norms in the same pass (`ssm_conv1d_gdn_state_l2_f32`, one warp per 128-channel row, 16-byte loads at d_conv 4), so raw q/k rows are never stored. Bit-identical (kernel-check `GDN-CONV-L2`, both load paths). `1`/`0` decide on any device, read per call. Keyed on GB10: it changes the kernel's parallelism and is unmeasured on discrete cards. | [gb10-prefill-next-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-next-20261107/), [GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gdn_conv_l2) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_K2_32` | GB10: on; elsewhere off; decide-by: 2026-11-21 | K2 of the chunked GDN prime at C = 32 runs `gdn_chunk_attn32_f32` on a 33,280-byte shared layout (the chunk's k rows serve as both tiles), so two blocks share an SM where the 67,072-byte layout fit one. Same loads, dot order and stores: bit-identical (kernel-check `GDN-DB-GQA`). `1`/`0` decide on any device, read per call. | [gb10-prefill-next-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-next-20261107/), [GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gdn_k2_32) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_K45` | GB10 (unified-memory 120a family): on; elsewhere off; decide-by: 2026-11-21 | `gdn_chunk_state_output_mma`: the GDN mma chunk path's state pass computes the chunk output too, at 64 state columns per CTA, so Y and the chunk-start states never reach memory; on this route the prime's seeded out-norm also runs inside the pass (`gdn_chunk_state_output_norm_mma`). Bit-identical (kernel-check `GDN-DB-GQA`). `1`/`0` decide on any device, read per call; `0` restores the K4/K5 pair and the separate `gated_rmsnorm_e4m3v2_f32`. The prime-table graph keeps the pair. | [gb10-prefill-next-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-next-20261107/), [GDN-OUTNORM-DESIGN.md](../research/gb10-prefill-20261106/GDN-OUTNORM-DESIGN.md), [GB10-PRIME-GDN-GLUE.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GDN-GLUE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gdn_k45) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_MMA` | ON on sm_90a and sm_120a builds; OFF elsewhere | GDN K4/K5 chunk pair on mma tensor cores with bf16 W/k mirrors, at chunk size 32. One const default (`gdn_mma_default_on`) feeds every read site, because a partial flip arms the mma pre-work while the scan runs scalar. `=0` reverts, `=1` forces. kernel-check pins both configs. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gdn_mma) |
| `MEMRA_GDN_TRAFFIC` | on (`=0` reverts); decide-by: 2026-11-21 | GDN prime traffic set: the compact `[T, num_k, 128]` q/k layout whenever num_v is a multiple of num_k, K2/K3/K5 run heads fastest so value heads sharing a key head read it from L2, and K3 stores only W's bf16 twin where the mma K4 is its sole reader. Bit-identical (kernel-check `GDN-DB-GQA`). `=0` restores all three; read per call. On GB10 `MEMRA_GDN_CONV_L2` rides inside this set. | [gb10-prefill-next-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-next-20261107/), [GB10-PRIME-GDN-GLUE.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GDN-GLUE.md), [GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gdn_traffic) |
| `MEMRA_KDA_CONV3` | `100a` builds: on; elsewhere off | The T=1 KDA conv+SiLU decode step runs its q, k and v planes in one launch (`memra_kda_conv_silu_decode3_f32`, `plane = blockIdx.y`) instead of three; bit-identical by construction (gate `tests/kda_conv3_gpu.rs`). Prefill keeps the per-plane loop. Keyed on `MEMRA_BUILT_CUDA_ARCH`, not model family, so every sm_100a family inherits it; `1`/`0` override, read per call. Announce `[kda-conv3] engaged`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kda_conv3) |
| `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ` | `100a` builds: on; elsewhere off | glm5_next KDA stage 1: the six same-input matvecs per layer (wq/wk/wv + f_a/g_a/b_proj) run as one launch. Q8_0 arm (`qmatvec_kda6_q8f32_mmvq`): Q8_0 rows bit-identical, f32 rows on a deterministic warp tree (a reduction-order class), at t 1..15 on plain-layout Q8_0 where the unfused arm rides MMVQ (`MEMRA_FAST`, `MEMRA_MMVQ`, `MEMRA_NO_BATCHED`, `MEMRA_B8`). BF16 arm (`qmatvec_kda6_bf16f32`) under `MEMRA_BF16_MMV=1`, declined when `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8` and `MEMRA_W8_HYBRID` are both on. Composes with `MEMRA_GLM5_TP`. `1`/`0` pick on any build, read per call. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kda_fused_proj), [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/launch-diet-20260830/LANE.md), [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/decode-diet-20260831/LANE.md) |
| `MEMRA_KDA_ONORM_ZQ8` | on on sm_100a builds, off elsewhere | On the decode KDA core (t=1, not verify rows), the o_norm launch (`memra_kda_gated_rmsnorm_zq8_f32`) also emits the q8_1 pair of its output and hands it to the `wo` projection through `matmul_q8_fast`, removing the standalone `quantize_q8_1` before `wo`. When `wo` is not MMVQ-fast-eligible the pair is not requested and `matmul` runs unchanged. Bit-identical to norm then quantize; gate `tests/kda_onorm_zq8_gpu.rs`. The decode-graph fixture arm is still owed (the mini model's Float `wo` leaves the door inert). Counter `KDA_ONORM_ZQ8_DISPATCHES`, announce `[kda-onorm-zq8] engaged`. `=1`/`=0` override; `=0` is the rollback seam. Read per call. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_kda_onorm_zq8) |
| `MEMRA_MLA_ABSORB_BF16` | unset (off) | Strict `=1`: the MLA decode `_wp` kernels read the absorb planes `wk_b`/`wv_b` as BF16 (`memra_mla_absorb_q_wp_bf16`, `memra_mla_decompress_v_wp_bf16`) instead of the loader's f32 copy. Exact where it applies: the BF16 copy is built at load only when every f32 element round-trips, otherwise the layer keeps f32. The f32 copy stays resident for prefill and TC paths, so it costs extra VRAM (369 MB per device on GLM-5.3-Flash). Gate `tests/mla_absorb_bf16_gpu.rs`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_mla_absorb_bf16) |
| `MEMRA_MLA_DECODE_SPLIT` | on (`=0` reverts) | The glm5_next MLA absorb/decompress `_split` twins divide each (token, head) block's output range across blocks: pure launch geometry, so bytes are identical for every split value. Engages under 1024 (token, head) blocks (decode and short verify), targeting about 1024 blocks with a floor of 32 outputs per block, inside the `mla_absorb_q`/`mla_decompress_v` wrappers. Only an explicit `0` disarms; read per call. glm5 TP head shards never take the split. Gate `crates/memra-engine/tests/mla_decode_split_gpu.rs`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_mla_decode_split), [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/decode-diet-20260831/LANE.md) |
| `MEMRA_MLA_SEG_WS` | `100a` builds: on; elsewhere off | The T=1 MLA core writes its PRE segment (q/kv projections, norms, latent splits, rope) into the session's stable handoff buffers instead of fresh allocations, and MID/POST read them there, so PRE and POST can be captured as separate graphs. One set serves every MLA layer sharing the `MlaGeom`; a layer with another geometry refuses by name. Byte-identical: same kernels, values and order at a different address. `1`/`0` override, read per call. Prerequisite of `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_MLA`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_mla_seg_ws) |
| `MEMRA_MLA_TC_PREFILL=1` | on (any value but `0`), read per call | glm5_next tensor-core MLA prefill: absorb and decompress run as one strided-batched bf16 cuBLASLt GEMM each (`memra_bf16_gemm_sb`), attention as `fa_mla_gathered_bf16` (K tiles gathered through the query's DSA index list, V aliasing K, bf16 MMA f32 accumulate). Band-gated (8e-3), never bit. Engages only with a gathered selection, `d_rope == 0`, `kv_rank == 512`, `t >= 16` and a non-portable-MMA arch; a cuBLASLt decline or a GLM5-TP head shard falls back to the f32 kernels with a `[mla-tc-prefill] DECLINED` line. Decode is untouched. `=0` is the rollback seam. Gate: `crates/memra-engine/tests/mla_tc_prefill_gpu.rs`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/mla-tc-prefill-20260830/LANE.md), [CELLS.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/composition-20260901/box/CELLS.md), [box-window](../research/glm5-prefix-latent-20260830/box-window/), [WINDOW-20260830.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/launch-diet-20260830/WINDOW-20260830.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_mla_tc_prefill) |

### Norms, HC and small fused ops

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DSV4_HC_DOT_SPLIT` | on, S=16 (unset), decide-by: 2026-10-07 | Unset, `1` or `16` selects HC24 S16 split dots; `0` restores sequential dots; `8` and `32` are opt-in numeric classes; any other string is off. Eligible: f32 device HC pre-attention and pre-FFN sites with N=24, K=16384. Every row count takes the split, so a verify, prefill or batched row carries the bits of the same row decoded alone. Each S is its own numeric class, not token-identical to the sequential dot. Read once per host thread: to roll back set `0` before process start and build fresh uncaptured states, retained graphs keep their kernels. Gate override admits 0/8/16/32 before capture. | [RESULTS.md](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/spec-identity-660/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_hc_dot_split) |
| `MEMRA_F16OUT=0` | on (fused `_f16out` epilogue twins) | `=0` runs separate epilogue launches instead of the fused fp16-operand `_f16out` twins (silu_mul, gated_rmsnorm, sig_mul) on the f16 prefill lane. Engages only at `t >= 16` with the `MEMRA_PP_F16` mirrors live; off under verify-exact. Bit-identical class: the twins emit the cvt kernel's exact halves. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_f16out) |
| `MEMRA_G4_PNFOLD=0` | on (single-phase `rms_pre_add_scale_rms_norm_q8_1` fold) | `=0` restores the unfused two-launch rms chain everywhere. Changes bits between seam values (the single-phase reduction rounds differently); decode, verify and graph parity hold within either value. Read once per process (`g4_pnfold_on()`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_g4_pnfold) |
| `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS` | unset (off) | Strict `=1`: the T=1 hc decode walk (`hyper_range_decode`) lands its glue transients in one per-engine `HyperDecodeWs` instead of fresh allocations per layer per token; the stream state swaps ownership with the workspace slot, so the stage boundary is untouched. Same kernels, order and bytes: byte-identical. Read per call. `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` refuses it. Batched glm5 serving reaches it only at B=1 through `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH_SOLO=1`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/decode-diet-20260831/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_hc_decode_ws) |
| `MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE` | `100a` builds: `2`; elsewhere `0` (unfused chain) | glm5_next fused mHC site pre-chain: rowsq-scale, Sinkhorn and collapse run as one `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused` launch per (site, token), each body verbatim, so bit-identical; a bit-preserving Sinkhorn stationarity exit fires only when an iteration changes no bit. Engages when streams <= 8. `=1` the fused kernel, `=2` the same with a warp-0 Sinkhorn for hc <= 4 (the `=1` kernel above that), `=0` the unfused chain; read per call. `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` refuses an explicit value. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_hc_fused_pre), [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/decode-diet-20260831/LANE.md), [LANE.md](../research/b200-sinkhorn-fusion-20260902/LANE.md) |
| `MEMRA_HC_MIXES_KERNEL` | on on sm_100a builds, off elsewhere | Runs the hyper-connection mixes projection at t=1 (`y[r] = sum_k W[r,k] x[k]`, 24 x 16384 on GLM-5.3-Flash, every hc pre site) through the native `hc_mixes_gemv_f32` kernel (one block per output row, 1024 threads x 16 contiguous float4 elements, pinned `__fmaf_rn` order, fixed reduction tree) instead of cuBLASLt's dot plus reduce pair. Numeric class: the tape moves against cuBLAS but is deterministic run to run; gate `tests/hc_mixes_gemv_gpu.rs` (tolerance, determinism, red arm, shape refusal). Falls back to cuBLASLt when `in_f != 16384`. Announces `[hc-mixes-kernel] engaged`. `=1`/`=0` override; `=0` is the rollback seam. Read once per process. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_hc_mixes_kernel) |
| `MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK` | `100a` builds: `512`; elsewhere `128` | CUDA block width of the fused hc pre-chain on the `MEMRA_HC_FUSED_PRE=2` arm; 128 is v2 verbatim, other widths take the v3 kernel (`memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused_v3`). Powers of two in 32..1024; anything else is refused by name with one `[hc-pre-block]` line and falls back to the build default. Widths other than 128 change stage 1's reduction order: named numeric class `hc_pre_rowsq_blockwide`. hc > 4 falls back to `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_fused`. Read per call. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_hc_pre_block) |
| `MEMRA_HC_PRE_SINK_REG` | `100a` builds: on; elsewhere off | The fused hc pre-chain's Sinkhorn runs in registers with `__shfl_sync` instead of shared memory, on the v3 kernel (this door or a non-128 `MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK` selects v3). Bit-identical by construction: one `comb` element per lane and sums gathered in the shared loop's order, so it is not a numeric class and a differing tape is a defect. Falls back to the shared path when hc*hc > 32. `1`/`0` override, read per call; keyed on arch, not model family. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_hc_pre_sink_reg) |
| `MEMRA_HC_PRE_V4` | on on sm_100a builds, off elsewhere | Runs each hc site's pre-chain on the v4 register schedule (`dsv4_hc_pre_v4_e16_kernel`, 1024 threads x 16 elements whatever `MEMRA_HC_PRE_BLOCK` sets v3 to; launcher `memra_dsv4_hc_pre_v4`) instead of `_v3`: x loaded once into registers, two barriers instead of twelve, warp 0 publishes `spre` before its Sinkhorn so the other warps combine underneath. Same arithmetic in the same order, bit-identical (`tests/hc_pre_v4_gpu.rs`). Engages only under the V2 arm at a v3 width (block != 128 or sink_reg); shapes it does not fit (hc != 4 or w != 16384) return 40025 and run v3. Announce: `kernel=hc_pre_v4` on the `[hc-fused-pre] engaged` line. `=1`/`=0` override; `=0` is the rollback seam. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_hc_pre_v4) |
| `MEMRA_NO_FUSE_NORMQ` | unset (fused norm-quantize) | Set (any value): decode and verify run an unfused `rms_norm` plus quantize instead of the fused norm-quantize kernel. |  |
| `MEMRA_NORM_ILP` | on (`=0` reverts) | The `_v2` norm twins unroll the element loop 4-deep: `rms_norm_f32`, `add_rms_norm_f32` (including the 1024-thread `rms_norm_decode` sites) and the glm5_next FFN-input `rms_norm_zq8_f32` (`MEMRA_GLM5_Q8_FUSE`). Bit-identical per row: the same per-thread order into one accumulator, reduce verbatim. `=0` reverts every twin to v1, the zq8 one included. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_norm_ilp) |
| `MEMRA_NORM_ILP_ZQ8` | on (`=0` reverts) | Per-kernel seam of the `rms_norm_zq8_f32_v2` twin: `=0` reverts it alone to v1 while the other `MEMRA_NORM_ILP` twins stay, so one binary can price this twin. `MEMRA_NORM_ILP=0` implies it. Bit-identical by construction (`tests/norm_zq8_ilp_gpu.rs`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_norm_ilp_zq8) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_NORM_FUSE` | on (`=0` reverts); decide-by: 2026-11-21 | Eager prime, non-f16 path: each layer's two residual sites run one `add_rms_norm` that also writes the F8F4 v2 activation scratch of the norm output (and the q8_1 pair before a GDN mixer) for the GEMM group that reads it, when that group takes the v2 tile; the GDN out-norm and the attention out-gate write their GEMM's v2 activation the same way. Bit-identical (kernel-check `ADD-RMS-NORM-E4M3V2`, `GATED-RMSNORM-E4M3V2`, `SIG-MUL-E4M3V2`). `=0` restores the separate `add` then `rms_norm` at both sites. | [gb10-prefill-next-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-next-20261107/), [GB10-PRIME-GDN-GLUE.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GDN-GLUE.md), [GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_norm_fuse) |
| `MEMRA_PRIME_Q_FUSE` | GB10: on; elsewhere off; decide-by: 2026-11-21 | The eager prime's gated-attention q path runs `q_split_norm_rope_f32`: the q/gate split, the per-head q-norm and q's RoPE in one kernel instead of three, so split and normed q never round-trip through memory. Even rotary widths only. Bit-identical (kernel-check `Q-SPLIT-NORM-ROPE`). `1`/`0` decide on any device, read per call. | [gb10-prefill-next-20261107](../research/gb10-prefill-next-20261107/), [GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md](decisions/GB10-PRIME-GLUE-2.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_q_fuse) |
| `MEMRA_QKVNORM_W` | on (`=0` reverts) | At prefill depth `rms_norm_qkv_f32` runs the warp-per-row float4 twin. `=0` takes the block-tree kernel. |  |
| `MEMRA_RMS_BLOCK` | per model: 256; 1024 for gemma4 and for GLM-5.3-Flash on `sm_100a` builds | rms_norm kernel block size. A parsable value overrides the model's numeric profile (`rms_block`) for every model; an unparsable one is ignored. Numeric-class change (reduction stride): a non-default value needs a fresh-tape identity gate plus the 12-boot battery at the pinned value. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_rms_block) |

### CUDA graphs, launch and streams

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| Carried-prime graph, no runtime door | on for the 170-SM sm_120a Qwen dense geometry (n_embd 5120, 64 layers); eager program elsewhere | Captures carried prime chunks (128 to 1024 rows, after the first chunk) as a CUDA graph; a device replay table refreshes session state and true KV depth every chunk, including after a park/restore or state swap. Unsupported shapes, one-off restored suffixes or boundary tails and low launch headroom run the identical eager chunk. The graph keys the FA2 numerical class. Chunk 1024 and PrimeWalker yields are unchanged. No environment door. Related names: `MEMRA_PRIME_CHUNK_GRAPH`. | [MECHANISM.md](../research/qwen-prefill-attn-20260909/MECHANISM.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_prime_chunk_graph) |
| `MEMRA_EVT` | off (`Engine::new` disables cudarc event tracking) | Only `1` keeps cudarc's implicit cross-stream event tracking (the escape hatch); any other value leaves it disabled. The default is safe because single-stream ordering is total and the memory pool reuses allocations in stream order. With tracking on, CUDA graph capture that would wait across streams refuses; the glm5 decode graph names `MEMRA_EVT` in its refusal. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_evt) |
| `MEMRA_G4PLAIN_EAGER=1` | unset (off) | `1` runs the gemma4 plain-serving graph loop's step body eagerly every step (no capture or replay), splitting body-semantics bugs from replay-mechanics bugs. A bisect seam, not a config (`gemma_spec.rs`). |  |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_DRAFT_GRAPH=1` | unset (off) | `1` replays the gemma draft chain as one captured CUDA graph keyed by (kr, rung, window regime); position slots and `g_seed` are eager-filled graph inputs. Flat in steady state (the launch tax is already hidden); it exists as the burst enabler and ships with the capture-retain allocator mode (`gemma_spec.rs`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gemma_draft_graph) |
| `MEMRA_GEMMA_ROUND_GRAPH=1` | unset (off) | `1` captures the whole gemma spec round (draft chain, stream verify, device accept/seed/rollback/commit, `spec_adapt_k` adaptive depth) once per regime: one graph launch per round. Exact with better round dynamics, but perf-negative at fixed shapes because the replay pays fixed `k_cap` drafts and fixed-width verify. Refused on non-resident weights (`gemma_graph_refuse_non_resident`). Kept for an exec-update-width phase 2 (`gemma_spec.rs`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gemma_round_graph) |
| `MEMRA_GEN_GRAPH` | ON at budget >= 48 on cards with >= 180 SMs, >= 256 elsewhere | Hybrid graph door in `generate_with`: capture and replay decode over the batched-prime cache, bit-identical to eager (graph-decode-gate). `1` forces it at any budget, `0` disables it. The 48-token key applies only to cards with at least 180 SMs; the rest keep 256 until an on-box crossover sweep sets a lower key. | [q27-deepdive-20260805](../research/q27-deepdive-20260805/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_gen_graph) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_HTOD_DIET` | as `MEMRA_HTOD_DIET` (on for sm_100a builds, off elsewhere) | Family alias of `MEMRA_HTOD_DIET`: same values and reader; honored alone (refusals then name it), and a disagreeing pair with the general name falls the door closed, naming both. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_htod_diet) |
| `MEMRA_GRAPH_WARMUPS=N` | 1 (minimum 1) | Eager warmup runs before `capture_graph` captures. `=2` is the rollback seam. A single warmup is safe because address stability is guarded structurally (balanced in-graph alloc/free pairs, retire-on-grow pools, capture-retain on the draft path) and checked by `tools/graph-warmup-stress-gate.sh` (pool-growth cycles, overlap arm, forced recaptures over freed blocks, bit-identity against eager). Values below 1 or unparsable fall back to 1. Read once per process. | [graph-warmups-5090-20260805](../research/graph-warmups-5090-20260805/), [graph-allocfree-20260805](../research/graph-allocfree-20260805/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_graph_warmups) |
| `MEMRA_HTOD_DIET` | on for sm_100a builds, off elsewhere; `1`/`0` on either name pick explicitly | Removes two per-round HtoD classes that move nothing the device needs: the ungated shared-expert add (`moe_shexp_add`, MoE plans without `ffn_gate_inp_shexp`) reads a resident ones buffer (`Engine::shexp_ones`) instead of re-uploading `vec![1.0f32; t]` per layer call, and the MLA latent `len_d` mirror takes the async `Engine::i32_set_k` instead of a synchronizing pageable 4-byte copy. Same values into the same buffers: bit-identical. Every glm5 decode graph refuses capture without it, so off sm_100a the default-on plain decode graph still refuses capture on a naked command. Read per call; `=0` restores both forms. Two-name law with `MEMRA_GLM5_HTOD_DIET`; counter `HTOD_DIET_AVOIDED`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/moe-loc-20260831/LANE.md), [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_htod_diet) |
| `MEMRA_PDL` | on (`=0` reverts) | Master seam for programmatic dependent launch: `=0` launches every PDL-attributed kernel with plain `cuLaunchKernel` (the glue kernels and every later wave, the DSV4 path included). Entry syncs are SASS-audited. |  |
| `MEMRA_PDL_MMVQ` | on (`=0` reverts) | `=0` reverts PDL wave A alone: the four decode-hot mmvq matvec kernels (`q4_0_mmvq_rp`, `fused2_mr1_rp`, `fused3_mr1_rp`, `q6_K_mmvq`) launch without PDL. |  |
| `MEMRA_PDL_NVFP4=0` | on (PDL on the gemma NVFP4mix decode chain) | `=0` reverts programmatic dependent launch on the gemma NVFP4mix decode chain's hot kernels (nvfp4 fused2/mr2 and the q8_0 `_rp` singles) alone. Scheduling only, bit-identical by construction. Read once per process (`pdl_nvfp4q8_on()`). |  |
| `MEMRA_PDL_WB` | on (`=0` reverts) | `=0` reverts PDL wave B alone: the dense-glue quartet (`rms_norm_f32`, `add_rms_norm_f32`, `add_scale_rms_norm_q8_1`, `quantize_q8_1`) and the flash trio (`append_kv` dc, the two q8 rows-combines) launch without PDL. |  |

### Other kernel arms

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_TOPK_SHARDS` | on (any value but `0`) | `topk_rows` (the DFlash2 candidate selector, k=16 over the vocab) runs the exact two-launch shard split (`topk_rows_shard_f32` per row and 16-way column shard, then `topk_rows_shard_merge_f32` per row) instead of one block per row. Engages only at n_cols >= 16384. Top-k under (value desc, column asc) is a discrete selection, so the (value, index) list is identical, NaN exclusion kept. On sm_100a builds the warp-merged twin `topk_rows_wshard_*` takes k in {8, 16, 32} first, so this split serves the other k there. `0` restores the standing kernel, read per call. Announces `[topk-shards] engaged:`. Gate: `glm5_matvec_doors_gpu`. | [LANE.md](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/matvec-20260831/LANE.md), [mv-battery-20260831](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/mv-battery-20260831/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_topk_shards) |
| `MEMRA_WPF` | `1` (on) | Weight prefetch: during the E4B dc attention window, which reads KV and not weights, the next matvec's decode-plane bytes are prefetched into L2. Value-free scheduling, numerics untouched. `=0` disables it. Wired only on the E4B attention arm; the 26B, 31B and FFN cascade are not wired. |  |

## Model-family doors

Doors that exist for one model family: DeepSeek-V4, GLM-5, Step, Gemma, Qwen (with Hyper and Ornith), MiMo and others. They select a family's program, topology or gate arm; a naked run takes the family's default, and the DSV4 doors print their resolved state at load (see the registry row in the DeepSeek-V4 table). Model cards are indexed in [MODELS.md](MODELS.md) and launch lines are in the [cookbook](COOKBOOK.md).

### DeepSeek-V4

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Dsv4Gpu::arm_full_token_replay` (process API) / sampled gate `--full-token-replay` | on for TP/EP plain requests (greedy, vendor-default device sampler); not an env door | The served TP/EP route arms each admitted plain request (greedy, or the device sampler at temperature 1, top-p 1, no top-k, no penalties); a session under 512 positions is rounded up to 512 so it can arm. Graphs replay forward and commit/head/sample per rank with live token, position, uniform and fault inputs. Coverage is positions below `min(capacity, 16384)`: past it `full_token_replay_covers` turns false, the route calls `disarm_full_token_replay` and continues on the eager step, the same numeric program (`dsv4_tp_replay_long_gate` crosses it with `DSV4_REPLAY_GATE_LIMIT`). A refused arm stays eager and logs `TP/EP replay not armed: <why>`. | [tp-replay-long](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/tp-replay-long/), [tp-replay-stream](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/tp-replay-stream/), [tp-replay-serving](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/tp-replay-serving/), [tpep-default](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/tpep-default/), [RESULT.md](../research/dsv4f-full-token-replay-20260908/RESULT.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#dsv4gpuarm_full_token_replay) |
| `Dsv4Gpu::load(.., placement)` / gate `--placement=tp_ep\|tp_ep_replicated\|pp` | required, no default; a placement argument, not a door | Every DSv4 load names its placement and logs `[load] placing <name>` first. memra-server passes `MEMRA_DSV4_TOPOLOGY`'s placement (`tp_ep` unless `pp`). A gate with one placement states it in its module doc; one with cells on several takes `--placement=<name>` (`Dsv4Placement::take_gate_arg`). `tp_ep` (served): all-layer expert-ID EP with exact attention TP2, rank-local Q_b rows and wo_a groups, row gathers before HC/FFN, latent KV, compressor and indexer replicated; needs FP8 projections and native matrix/device math. `tp_ep_replicated` (gate-only): replicated attention with a rank-order FP32 slot sum. `pp`: PP-2. Serving-qualification gates run `tp_ep` first. | [TESTING.md](TESTING.md#dsv4-gate-placement), [RESULTS.md](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/tpep-serve/RESULTS.md), [attention-tp-runtime-20260907.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/attention-tp-runtime-20260907.md), [attention-tp-sampled-20260907.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/attention-tp-sampled-20260907.md), [tp-ep-sampled-perf-gate-20260907.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/tp-ep-sampled-perf-gate-20260907.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_topology) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_*`, `MEMRA_F16G_*` (DSV4 door registry) | each name's own row | `crates/memra-engine/src/dsv4_doors.rs` declares every DSV4 door with its default and disposition, and `Dsv4Gpu::load` prints one `[dsv4-doors]` line per door (declared default, resolved state, disposition). `cargo test -p memra-engine --lib dsv4_doors::` fails when a declared default or served-path state stops matching the admission code, or when an engine-read name under either prefix, or `MEMRA_MOE_F16G`, is neither a declared door nor an exempt non-door. The `MEMRA_F16G_*` names are the grouped f16 expert lane's knobs, listed under MoE. |  |
| `set_index_topk_radix_for_gate()` (process API) | off (process-local gate setter) | Plain t=1, K=512, 2048<=N<=4096 exact radix-cut twin for `dsv4_topk_idx_numeric_kernel`: same numerical-zero-normalized f32 order key and index-ascending ties, persistent decode-workspace scratch, no score or index D2H. Other K/N, batched verification, host-math and stream-selector paths are unchanged. No environment variable or serving default selects it; `clear_index_topk_radix_for_gate` rolls back and `index_topk_radix_dispatches` counts accepted enqueues. Gate: `tools/dsv4-index-topk-gate.cu`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#set_index_topk_radix_for_gate) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_AR_PUSH` | ON on TP/EP; `0` is the rollback seam, decide-by: 2026-10-10 | The TP/EP walk's four joins (attention wo_a gather, attention-out gather, expert reduce, vocab-head gather) use the push kernels in `cu/tp_ar.cu`: each rank writes its operand into the peer's output buffer, fences, raises one flag in the peer's signal block, then waits on its own flag and reads only local memory. Values and bits equal the pull joins. `TpEpArState` refuses a push join that writes the previous push join's output; under `tp_ep_replicated` the expert reduce alternates two output planes. The DSpark EP combine and the armed AR phase instrument stay on pull joins. An expired bounded wait writes 40043. Read once at load; other values refuse load. | [levers-20260926](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/levers-20260926/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_ar_push) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_C4_HOST_MB=N` | `0` (OFF) | Active C4 host-history budget for dsv4 in MiB, separate from the parked-prefix `MEMRA_DSV4_KV_HOST_MB`. Nonzero needs the matrix program and a nonzero prefill chunk: fresh states and warm restores keep C4 history in cacheable pinned host RAM, while SWA rows, indexer keys, C128 and pending compressors stay on the GPU with unchanged bits. Admission sums the full-capacity C4 forecast over both stages and returns overload before consuming a prefix when it exceeds the budget; no GPU-history fallback. The budget excludes parked snapshots. TP/EP snapshot, restore and B-row steps refuse host C4. Rollback: `0` or unset. Gate: `dsv4_c4_direct_gate`. | [active-c4-host.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/active-c4-host.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_c4_host_mb) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DENSE_ARM=fp8` | `fp8` on the device decode path; `bf16` on the legacy path | `fp8` reads the FP8-block dense linears (attention wq_a/wq_b/wkv/wo_a/wo_b, shared experts, indexer) as stored (e4m3 codes plus f32 128x128 block scales) on the device decode and batched-verify paths through `dsv4_gemv_fp8{,_m}`, bit-identical twins of the bf16 GEMVs. The trunk bf16 slabs then drop to host staged residency (`DenseBf16::Host`), a VRAM saving; prefill stays byte-identical and the drafter/MTP blocks keep resident bf16. `fp8` without `MEMRA_DSV4_DECODE_PATH=device`, or an unknown value, refuses at load; `bf16` works everywhere. `dsv4-decode-probe` refuses `fp8`. Boot receipt: `[load] dense arm:`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_dense_arm) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DEVICES=a,b` | `0,1` | The two CUDA devices that carry the dsv4 serve stack (`Dsv4Gpu` is two-card by construction). An unparseable list refuses boot. |  |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DRAFTER=dspark` | unset (trunk only, plain routes) | `dspark` loads the checkpoint's own DSpark drafter (about 10.7 GiB resident) and arms the DSv4 spec routes. Unset, empty or `off` leaves the drafter absent and serves plain decode only; route keying reads drafter residency. Any other value refuses the load by name. Read once at load. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_drafter) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DSPARK_FUSED_MOE` | `0` (OFF) | DSpark-only selected-expert dispatch arm. `1` keeps the host-oracle router values and expert order but replaces each MTP block's per-expert projection loop with the exact indirect FP4 projection kernels batched verification uses. Trunk prefill and decode are untouched. `1` needs `MEMRA_DSV4_DECODE_PATH=device` and refuses load otherwise; unknown values refuse. Rollback: `0`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_dspark_fused_moe) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_DSPARK_PROPOSAL` | `greedy` (coupled OFF) | Sampled DSpark proposal policy. `greedy` (also unset or empty) keeps the point-mass draft. `coupled` samples each draft row with the request sampler at the target's absolute position key and conditions the next row on the chosen token. The accept walk is unchanged: a proposal is accepted only if it equals the target's own draw, so seeded output does not move. Proposal rows skip repetition penalties (the target keeps them), and the arm adds host sampling cost. Unknown values refuse load. `set_draft_proposal_for_gate` is exclusive; `coupled_draft_draws` proves engagement. Gate: `dsv4_coupled_proposal_gate`. Rollback: unset or `greedy`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_dspark_proposal) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_EP` | `off` on PP-2; implied `pair` on TP/EP, where an explicit `off` refuses | `pair` shards the trunk's whole NVFP4 experts between two stages with a checked peer link (first half of global ids on stage 0, second on stage 1), preserving global routing and scales; peer-owned slot contributions overwrite their slots before the ordered combine. Attention, shared experts and DSpark keep their layer owners. Requires native device math and FP8 dense. Prefill-only grouped probes and single-stage graph capture refuse EP. Unknown or non-Unicode values refuse; load-time only. `ep_calls` and verifier bytes expose engagement. Rollback: a fresh load with `off` (PP-2). | [matrix-ep-chain.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/matrix-ep-chain.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_ep) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_GROUPED_ROUTE` | `device` (unset); `host` is the seam | Metadata realization in the grouped-prefill probe and the whole-request matrix program. `device` builds the stable expert-major CSR, weights and per-projection scale metadata on the GPU, and persistent matrix visitors derive tile counts from device offsets; `host` keeps CPU grouping (rollback). Part of the matrix program's admission minimum, and the matrix EP storage-validity term requires it. The correctness arm keeps scalar invalid-id/live-count and FP8/half checks host-visible. No arithmetic or format change. Unknown values, empty included, refuse. Component gate: `tools/dsv4-grouped-route-gate.cu`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_grouped_route) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_INDEXER_SCORE` | knee (unset or empty); decide-by: 2026-10-07 | Per-model scorer arm. Unset is knee dispatch per launch: the tiled scorer when one query row meets >= 1152 candidates or a multi-row launch reaches >= 8192 row-candidates, the ordered scalar scorer below. The arms are bit-identical, so the knee only picks speed. `scalar` forces scalar (rollback); `tiled` forces tiled (measurement), needs native device f32x and a 64-head x 128-dim indexer, and refuses elsewhere, where the knee resolves scalar. Full-token replay stays scalar. Unknown values refuse; read once at load. Gate: `tools/dsv4-indexer-tiled-gate.cu` (`--knee`). | [RESULTS.md](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/indexer-knee/RESULTS.md), [indexer-tiled.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/indexer-tiled.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_indexer_score) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_KV_HOST_MB=N` | `0` (OFF) | Parked-session host tier for dsv4, in MiB: a byte-budgeted LRU of exact-token prefixes inside the request's cache namespace. An entry keeps only live compressed state in one pinned slab per stage (SWA rings, CSA/HCA rows to their high-water marks, indexer rows, pending compressor state; DSpark adds its three rings and newest tap). A hit is consumed, restored at the request's planned capacity and must feed a non-empty suffix. Entries under 128 tokens are skipped; a snapshot or copy failure latches the tier off, with no pageable fallback. Separate from `MEMRA_DSV4_C4_HOST_MB`, whose warm states restore straight to host C4. Rollback: `0`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_kv_host_mb) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_PDL` | ON; `0` is the rollback seam, decide-by: 2026-10-10 | Programmatic dependent launch on the DSv4 kernel chain (`cu/memra_pdl_chain.cuh`). Every chain kernel launches through `memra_chain_launch` with the programmatic stream serialization attribute and opens with `MEMRA_PDL_CHAIN_ENTRY()` (`griddepcontrol.wait`, then `launch_dependents`), so only block dispatch moves earlier and each kernel computes what the OFF program computes. `tools/check-pdl-chain.py` refuses a chain-launched kernel without the entry. Read once at load, before replay capture; `MEMRA_PDL=0` also turns it off; other values refuse load. With it on, nsys kernel durations include the predecessor's run, so attribute kernels with `MEMRA_DSV4_PDL=0`. | [levers-20260926](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/levers-20260926/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_pdl) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_PREFILL_CHUNK=N` | unset = `min(512, MEMRA_CTX)` (the kernel width); `0` refuses boot | Bounded-memory dsv4 prefill: after a one-token canonical prime the prompt is teacher-forced through width-N device transactions, every row committed, keeping only the compact persistent cache plus O(N) scratch. With DSpark resident, target-layer taps advance its rings at absolute positions. Restored suffixes take the same path. Every served prompt runs chunked; the monolithic prime exists only in the gate-only reference executor. The state reserves N transient rows per trunk layer. Malformed values, `0`, N above `MEMRA_CTX` and N above the 512-row kernel ceiling refuse at boot, before any weight loads. Widths 1..32 use register-specialized exact twins; 33..64 tile them by eight. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_prefill_chunk) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_PREFILL_DRAFT` | `all` (tail OFF) | Chunked DSpark-prime policy. Unset, empty or `all` computes drafter KV for every suffix position. `tail` computes only the final sliding-window positions of the suffix at their absolute positions and skips unused trunk-tap captures, since DSpark's persistent rings read only those rows and the newest tap. Suffixes shorter than the window keep every row and their restored slots. The one-token prime and decode/spec are unchanged. Unknown values and `tail` with legacy decode refuse load. `set_prefill_draft_for_gate` is exclusive and drains stages. Gate: `dsv4_prefill_work_gate`. Rollback: unset or `all`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_prefill_draft) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_PREFILL_HEAD` | `all` (last OFF) | Chunked-prefill head policy for DSv4. Unset, empty or `all` keeps full vocabulary projection and argmax on intermediate chunks and full logits on the final chunk. `last` skips head output on intermediate chunks and computes only the final hidden row through the single-row head; trunk, cache and tap work are unchanged. `verify_batch_dev` ignores the policy. Unknown values and `last` with legacy decode refuse load. `set_prefill_head_for_gate` is exclusive and drains stages; `prefill_head_stats` counts head calls. Gate: `dsv4_prefill_work_gate`. Rollback: unset or `all`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_prefill_head) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_PREFILL_MOE` | `reference` (grouped OFF) | Routed-prefill arm for DSv4. Unset, empty or `reference` keeps the native path. `grouped` runs the grouped f16 tensor-core visitor on ModelOpt split planes, moving the same FP8-QAT values through a checked power-of-two half mirror, and refuses unrepresentable or NaN rows before GEMM; its GEMM reduction is a separate numeric class. Device only; needs the mode-2 grouped visitor and the direct quant loader. Both arms feed the common combine and the full shared expert. Unknown or non-Unicode values refuse load. `set_prefill_grouped_for_gate` is exclusive and drains streams; no request-time switch. Rollback: unset or `reference`. | [grouped-corrected.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/grouped-corrected.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_prefill_moe) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_REPLAY_CADENCE` | on (unset), decide-by: 2026-09-22 | Acts only inside armed, admitted full-token replay. Unset or any value except exact `0` selects the cadence forward variants (ordinary, C4, C4+C128) with a shared commit; exact `0` selects original full replay. Read when a request arms: set `0` before arming, retained graphs do not change in place. It is the served cadence on the TP/EP plain route (greedy and vendor-default sampled requests); a drafter-resident route sends those requests to DSpark. Admission and refusal protections are unchanged; explicit gate overrides keep the full-replay, cadence-only and composition A/B instruments. | [dsv4f-replay-cadence-20260908](../research/dsv4f-replay-cadence-20260908/), [dsv4f-compose-cadence-dense-20260908](../research/dsv4f-compose-cadence-dense-20260908/), [DESIGN.md](../research/dsv4f-cadence-dense-default-on-20260908/DESIGN.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_replay_cadence) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_ROWS=B` | TP/EP: the lane count (runtime parameter); PP-2: OFF (unset, `0` or `1`), decide-by: 2026-11-18 | B-row decode across requests. `2..=16` coalesces the lanes' plain steps into one `decode_rows_greedy` (or `decode_rows_full` when a row needs logits) under the launch turn: weights are read once for all rows while each row uses its own cache, and each row is bit-identical to its one-row step (`dsv4_rows_gate`). A batch targets `ceil(lanes / groups)` rows; on PP with `2 * B <= MEMRA_DSV4_SESSIONS` two groups run in flight. Needs the PP matrix or TP/EP attention-TP2 program and at least B lanes, else refuses load; TP/EP refuses B below the lane count. DSpark, the device sampler and restored-prefix continuation keep their own steps. The PP-2 arm awaits a served A/B. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_rows) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_SAMPLE_SORT` | `radix` (unset) | Sort order of the DSV4 sampler. Unset or `radix` replaces full-vocabulary comparison sorting with four stable byte passes over descending IEEE-f32 keys (signed zeros share a key, ties keep ascending token id); `comparison` is the explicit legacy arm and byte-identity oracle; any other value refuses at DSV4 load. NaN-bearing rows keep the comparator in both arms. Softmax, nucleus threshold, draw and CDF arithmetic are unchanged, so sampled tokens are identical by construction. Read once per process; serving inherits it through `dsv4_sample_row`, and gates override it per thread with `set_dsv4_sampler_order_for_gate`. Gate: `dsv4_sampler_sort_gate`. | [DSV4-SAMPLER-RADIX-DEFAULT.md](decisions/DSV4-SAMPLER-RADIX-DEFAULT.md), [attention-tp-sampled-20260907.md](../research/dsv4f-2card-1m-20260904/attention-tp-sampled-20260907.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_sample_sort) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_SAMPLER` | `device` on TP/EP, `host` on PP-2; a gate-set program selector | Unset takes the placement default (`Dsv4Gpu::plain_sampler`, named in the boot log); `host` or `device` overrides. Different numeric programs: `host` runs the CPU radix oracle; `device` keeps decode logits on the head stream and returns one u32, class `device-f64-exp-tree-cdf-v1` (draws at rounding boundaries can diverge). Both keep temperature/top-k/top-p and the position-keyed SplitMix64 mapping; penalties need finite logits. DSV4 gate binaries set or assert it, so it stays. DSpark keeps its host sampler. Unknown values refuse at load; rollback: `host` and a restart. | [RESULTS.md](../research/dsv4f-gpu-sampler-20260907/RESULTS.md), [RESULTS.md](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/sampler-door/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_sampler) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_SESSIONS=N` | unset: 16 on plain TP/EP, 2 on the plain PP matrix program over 2+ stages, 1 with a drafter or elsewhere | Serving lanes on the dedicated DSv4 route, `1..=16`; a runtime parameter, not a door. `1` is the serial route. On TP/EP the lanes coalesce plain steps into B-row graph steps, and an explicit N > 1 needs `MEMRA_DSV4_ROWS >= N`. On PP the lanes share one queue and launch turn: a plain step queues its PP-2 step, gives the turn up while it waits, then commits, so card 0 runs one session's stage 0 while card 1 runs another's stage 1, every token equal to the serial stream (`dsv4_pipeline_gate`); chunked prefill yields after each chunk. The device sampler, restored-prefix continuation and DSpark hold the turn. N > 1 on a load whose steps do not pipeline refuses load; other values refuse load. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_sessions), [pp-pipeline](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/pp-pipeline/), [tp-rows](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/tp-rows/), [lanes16](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/lanes16/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_SPEC_DEPTH=N` | unbounded (the drafter's `block_size + 1`) | Serve door: caps the per-round draft depth on the dsv4 spec route, the bench drivers' depth policy exposed at the serving seam. Every depth reproduces the plain route token for token (identity-gated). Resolved at load: a value that is not a positive integer refuses the boot (`dsv4_serve.rs`, `dsv4_gpu.rs`). | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_spec_depth) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_TOPOLOGY=tp_ep\|pp` | `tp_ep` (unset or empty); a placement selector, no decide-by | Load-time placement of a two-card DSv4 load. `tp_ep` puts every layer on both cards: the expert-ID EP pair (experts 0..128 on card 0, 128..256 on card 1), attention split by head (exact attention TP2), head and small chains replicated, DSpark drafter experts split the same way. Its lanes (`MEMRA_DSV4_SESSIONS`, 16) share B-row steps (`MEMRA_DSV4_ROWS`); plain requests ride full-token replay graphs up to position 16384, then the bit-identical eager step. `pp` loads PP-2, an explicit placement. Any other value refuses the boot; the boot log names placement and source. Gates do not read it. | [DSV4-TPEP-DEFAULT.md](decisions/DSV4-TPEP-DEFAULT.md), [tpep-default](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/tpep-default/), [tp-rows](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/tp-rows/), [kv-split](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/kv-split/), [TESTING.md](TESTING.md#dsv4-gate-placement), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_topology) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_VERIFY_TOPK` | `device` (unset or empty); `legacy` is the seam | Narrow (T<=8) device-math verification. `device` replaces the short CPU read/sort/upload with an exact CUDA selector (non-NaN scores descending, numerical-zero ties by index ascending); `legacy` keeps CPU sorting at <=4096 candidates and the device stream selector above that. Part of the matrix expert program's admission minimum. Unknown values, and `device` on a non-device-math load, refuse at load. Gate setter `set_verify_topk_for_gate`; `device_verify_topk_calls` counts the short device dispatches. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_verify_topk) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_VOCAB_HEAD` | ON on TP/EP; `0` is the rollback seam, decide-by: 2026-10-10 | Splits the TP/EP decode head across the ranks: rank r computes logits for vocab rows `[r * V/2, (r + 1) * V/2)` from its replicated final residual and head copy (`head_logits_tp_ep`), and one row gather lays both halves out in vocab order on both ranks, so the argmax and device sampler read full rows computed by the one-rank head's dots kernel. Covers the one-row eager step, the full-token replay commit graph and the B-row steps; prefill and verify keep the one-rank head. The gather's bounded-wait refusal is read before a token leaves (`vocab_head_refusal_check`). Read once at load; values other than `0` and `1` refuse load. | [levers-20260926](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/levers-20260926/), [CEILING.md](../research/dsv4f-bringup-20260923/ceiling/CEILING.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_vocab_head) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_VT=off\|slot` | `off` | Serve door for the dsv4 confidence-window verify policy: `off` is the full-depth ladder verify; `slot` is the per-slot confidence window at `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_TAU` (the head emits marginal accept probabilities, so per-slot is the correct reading). Any other value, including the retired `fixed@K` and inline `slot@TAU` forms, refuses the boot. Stays `off` until the owner ratifies a flip. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_dsv4_vt) |
| `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_TAU=T` / `MEMRA_DSV4_VT_FLOOR=F` | 0.5 / 0 | Serve door parameters for `MEMRA_DSV4_VT=slot`: tau in (0,1) and the minimum drafts forwarded per round. Set without `slot`, or out of range, they refuse the boot rather than sit inert (`dsv4_gpu.rs resolve_vt`). |  |

### DeepSeek-V4.1

The deepseek-v4.1 route (`dsv41_serve` over `dsv41_gpu`): its placement, its batched step, its host issue, its graph door and its draft head. Every row here leaves the route's tokens as they are, except `MEMRA_DSV41_TRUNK_LAYERS`, a diagnostic whose answers are not the checkpoint's.

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| `MEMRA_DSV41_DEVICES=a[,b,...]` | `0` | deepseek-v4.1 serve route (`dsv41_serve`): the CUDA device of each rank, rank r on the r-th entry. N devices run the N-rank split of `dsv41_gpu::tp` (heads, output groups, `wo_b` rows, routed experts and head rows; gathers only, so the one-card bytes). Residency follows the cards, with no switch: where every rank's routed experts fit its card's free memory (four ranks on four 96 GB cards) the whole trunk and the head are resident; where they do not (one card) every layer's own weights and the head stay on the card and the routed experts live in pinned host memory behind each rank's device LRU cache of its own expert range (`dsv41_gpu::experts`). A degree the partition laws refuse (`Part::check`) or an unparseable list refuses the boot. Placement parameter, no decide-by. | [tp](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/tp/), [tp4](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/tp4/), [serve](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/serve/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV41_BATCH_ROWS=N` | unset (8, the small-M GEMM bodies' limit) | deepseek-v4.1 serve route, runtime parameter: the running requests' one-token steps that share one decode call (`decode::Runner::forward_batch`: each row its request's own call at its own position against its own state, through shared launches; `dsv41-spec batch-rows`: a row's bytes are those of its call run alone). More running requests than N are consecutive calls. `1` runs every call alone through each block: the measurement seam of the batched step. It never changes a token. Outside 1..8 refuses the boot. | [batch](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/batch/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV41_TP_ISSUE` | **unset: `parallel` at N > 1** (2026-11-09) | DeepSeek-V4.1 N-rank route (`dsv41_gpu::tp::Group`): how the host issues the ranks' work between collectives. `parallel` = one host thread per rank (the group's own issue threads, `tp::Crew`: the calling thread issues rank 0 and N - 1 threads spin on a section counter for the others; each rank's kernels, its routing read-back and its host plan on its own thread, the collectives on the calling thread); `serial` = the calling thread issues every rank in turn (the shape the N=2 gates of `research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/tp/` ran). A rank only ever queues on its own stream, so both arms write the same bytes (the `dsv41-gate tp` byte gates run under either). Measurement and rollback seam for the 4-card window; any other value refuses the load. decide-by: 2026-11-23 (an interleaved A/B of the two arms on four cards keeps the winner and deletes this read). | [tp4](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/tp4/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV41_DECODE_GRAPH` | unset (off) | DeepSeek-V4.1 N-rank route (`dsv41_gpu::graph`): `1` replays spans of a decode call of at most 8 rows as one CUDA graph a rank instead of issuing their kernels one by one. Captured today: the half of a layer that holds no position (the post-mix of the attention output, the FFN's mixes, norm, router, plan, expert tiles, shared expert, the MoE's gathers, combine and post-mix; the second stream's work is a branch of the graph). The first call of a (layer, rows) runs launch by launch, the second records it, and a later call is one graph launch a rank: the capture knows which node arguments hold each input buffer's address and rewrites them first when the call's inputs sit elsewhere (0.08 us a node; `moved` in the `[dsv41-graph]` line a process prints at its end counts those replays). The first two replays of a span run it both ways, launch by launch on the call's inputs and as the graph on copies of them, and compare the stream and the new pre-mix byte for byte; a difference fails the call. Measured on two cards, 20 layers: a one-row step 7.183 -> 7.033 ms, a 2-row call 8.08 -> 7.89, 4 rows 9.26 -> 9.17, 8 rows level. Needs the in-kernel gathers (the default transport at two to eight ranks, or one rank), no trace, no expert cache (the served one-card residency resolves its misses on the host) and no route log; a group without them, or a capture that recorded anything but kernels and zero fills, keeps that span launch by launch. Same kernels, same arguments, same order on each stream, so the bytes are those of the launch-by-launch call (`dsv41-gate tp` under the door repeats every decode step through the capture and compares each repeat with the single-card block). Transition door: the attention half joins when its position arguments are read from the card; decide-by: 2026-11-24 (default on when the whole step is one graph and the four-card A/B holds, else removed). | [perf](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/perf/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV41_CALL_FAULT=<steps>` | unset (off) | Fault door of the deepseek-v4.1 serve route's step-fault smoke: a comma list of 0-based step numbers (counted over the steps that ran) whose call flag is set on every rank before the step's calls, as a router row naming no expert would (`Runner::inject_call_fault`). That step's requests fail with the route flag's error, the route probes the ranks (`Runner::probe`) and serves on; a lost transport or a failed probe would stop it. Two consecutive steps in the list are the case a request's data can cause. Any value that is not a comma list of numbers refuses the boot. | [review-1281](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/review-1281/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV41_DRAFT=0\|1` | **unset: `0`**; decide-by: 2026-11-24 | deepseek-v4.1 serve route: the checkpoint's DSpark draft head (`dsv41_gpu::draft`). `1`: a decode call carries its next token and drafted ones, each row run as the one-token decode program of its position (`dsv41-spec verify-rows`: the rows' bytes are those of feeding the tokens one call at a time), and the route keeps the drafts that equal its own per-row choices, so the tokens are the undrafted route's, greedy or sampled. The depth verified per step is priced from the confidence head and the route's own row-cost table (`draft::price_depth`), and a request whose drafts do not pay stops drafting for a stretch; a request whose draft block would pass the context decodes plainly. Default off while its gates are missing: the chat-gate cells and `verify serve` with drafting on, and the interleaved A/B on the layout the route serves from; engine-level gates: byte identity, layer parity, drafted greedy == plain greedy. Any other value refuses the boot. | [dspark](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/dspark/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV41_DRAFT_DEPTH=N` | unset (the head's block, 5) | deepseek-v4.1 serve route with `MEMRA_DSV41_DRAFT=1`, runtime parameter: the most drafted tokens a step verifies, 1 to the block; the priced depth never exceeds it. It never changes a token. Outside the range refuses the boot. | [dspark](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/dspark/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV41_TRUNK_LAYERS=N` | unset (the checkpoint's trunk) | deepseek-v4.1 serve route, diagnostic: run only the first N trunk layers. The answers are not the checkpoint's (the boot log says so in capitals); it exists to rehearse the resident N-rank route's memory book, admission and serving loop on fewer cards than the full residency needs (four ranks on two cards with 20 layers hold a four-card rank's bytes per card). Never set on a route whose answers are read. decide-by: 2026-11-23 (delete once a four-card box runs the serve gates). | [serve](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/serve/) |
| `MEMRA_DSV41_TOKEN_MAP=<file>` | dsv41-run, memra-server | The DeepSeek-V4.1 Engram compressed token map (raw little-endian i32 per vocabulary id): `dsv41-run` reads it when `--token-map` is absent, so `memra model verify checkpoint` can call the runner with only the checkpoint and the probe tokens, and the server's deepseek-v4.1 route (`dsv41_serve::load`) needs it to hash Engram n-grams. The vendor builds the map from tokenizer normalizers at load and memra does not port that yet; both refuse without it. A model input until the port lands, not a tuning knob. | [README](../research/modelplan-onboarding-dsv41-20261109/README.md) |

### GLM-5

| Flag | Default | What it does | Record |
|---|---|---|---|
| GLM TP-2 device sampler, no runtime door | on for sm_100a builds; the host sampler on every other build | GLM TP-2 plain only, on the symmetric TP-2 walk (`HybridModel::glm5_tp_sym_walk_rt`: glue on every layer, the expert split, two devices on `device-push`); a load that declines into the root-orchestrated walk keeps the host sampler. Reuses the DSV4 temperature/top-k/top-p kernels, one u32 per draw; temperature <= 0 or top-k 1 uses raw argmax, lowest id on ties. Position-keyed SplitMix64, numeric class `device-f64-exp-tree-cdf-v1`. Penalties, min-p, constraints and full-row capture keep host sampling; logprobs reject at HTTP admission. A first-draw error recovers on the host; a later one fails the request. | [GLM5-TP-DEVICE-SAMPLER-DEFAULT.md](decisions/GLM5-TP-DEVICE-SAMPLER-DEFAULT.md), [RESULTS.md](../research/glm5-tp2-gpu-sampler-20260908/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_tp_symmetric) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` | on (any value but `0`), read per call | glm5_next T=1 decode as per-stage CUDA graphs: each contiguous run of KDA layers is captured once per session per ping-pong phase and replayed. MLA/DSA layers, prefill, the logits DtoH, the batched tier and spec verify stay eager. Engages on the per-session serial hc walk (B=1 via `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH_SOLO=1`, or without `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH`). Falls through to the byte-identical eager walk by name when `MEMRA_HTOD_DIET` is off, `MEMRA_SIG_ROUTER=0`, `MEMRA_MOE_TRACE` or `MEMRA_MOE_STATS` is armed, NVMe H2D promotion, `MEMRA_EVT`, a `MEMRA_GLM5_TP` shard, or no KDA layer. `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_SELFCHECK_N` checks first replays; a mismatch or moved signature latches eager. `=0` is the rollback seam. | [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [LANE.md](../research/b200-glm5-graph-20260902/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_decode_graph) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_DFLASH` | unset (native MTP draft source) | `<dir-or-hf-spec>` loads the sha-pinned GLM-5.3-Flash DFlash2 block-diffusion drafter on the head engine as the draft source of the `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC` loop; the native MTP head is then neither required nor loaded. Drafter context is the stream mean of trunk layer outputs at its `target_layer_ids`. Verify, accept, rollback, K policy and receipts are shared with the native source, so it moves acceptance only. K clamps to block_size-1 = 7. Set, it wins over a co-loaded MTP head; boot prints `[glm5-spec] draft source = dflash2 @ <sha8>`. A load failure fails the boot. `MEMRA_FRSPEC_TRIM` reaches this head too. Gate: `glm5_dflash_session_gpu`. Rollback: unset. | [3way-decision-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/3way-decision-20260830/), [dflash-draft-src-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/dflash-draft-src-20260830/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_dflash) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_DRAFT_PRIME_LAZY` | unset (off); host-tap arm only, inert when `MEMRA_GLM5_DRAFT_TAPS_DEVICE=1` | `1` is a rollback seam: the eager drafter arm's prompt ingest runs inside round 1 of the first burst instead of at session creation. Default: the ingest runs at creation, before the session returns and before any token is emitted. Same rows through the same GEMMs into the same position-addressed KV either way; only when it runs moves (second-token latency vs TTFT). Read per session creation. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_draft_prime_lazy) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_DRAFT_TAPS_DEVICE` | unset (off); `=1` arms; flip blocked on two named defects | Device-resident drafter prime. `=1`: the trunk prime stays one whole-prompt `prime_cache` call; each range's five tap planes stage on the writing stage's device in a chunk-sized ring and, at every range boundary, are interleaved into the drafter fc layout by device copies, run through `ctx_features` and `ingest_ctx` and appended to the drafter KV, with no DtoH or HtoD. Defects: `MEMRA_HYPER_PRIME_V2` arm 2 ignores the range hooks, so that pair refuses by name; the ingest can race the next range's ring rewrite, tearing drafter KV silently. Unset keeps the host-tap program and its `MEMRA_GLM5_DRAFT_PRIME_LAZY` sub-arm. VRAM: one 5 x ring x 4096 f32 ring per device. Rollback: unset. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_draft_taps_device) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_MLA` | `100a` builds: on; elsewhere off | Lets the decode graph door (`MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH`) capture MLA/DSA layers in halves: the attention-site hc pre, norm and PRE segment close the current graph piece, an eager middle (append, k-pool selection, attention core, decompress, `wo`) runs on the stage workspace, and the second half opens the next piece of the same run. Bit-identical: the same kernels replayed. Needs `MEMRA_MLA_SEG_WS=1`, without which MLA/DSA layers stay eager. The engaged line carries `mla_halves=N`. `1`/`0` override. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_graph_mla) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_RECAPTURE` | unset (off) | Strict `=1`: when a captured `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` stage is invalidated (its `cache.pos` moved, or a recurrent-state buffer was re-seated), rebuild it on its own buffers instead of latching that stage eager for the session; the rebuild drains the stream before dropping the stage and latches if the drain fails. Off by default because the eager fallback is byte-identical. `glm5-decode-graph-gate` arms it for its forced re-seat arm. Counter `GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH_RECAPTURES`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_graph_recapture) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_MTP` | off | `1` loads the glm5_next NextN/MTP block as the embedded draft head (MLA, its own k-pool indexer, MoE, serial residual); the checkpoint ships no private lm_head, so the draft projects through the trunk `lm_head`. Off by design: the head is a full MoE layer of VRAM and load time, and only `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC=1` gives it a consumer. The armed load prints `[mtp-glm5] MEMRA_GLM5_MTP=1: loading the glm5_next NextN block`; off is byte-identical plain serving. Gate: `glm5_mtp_head_gpu`. Rollback: unset (load time only). | [mtp-draft-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/mtp-draft-20260830/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_mtp) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_Q8_FUSE=1` | unset (off); decide-by: 2026-11-18 | In the two glm5_next T=1 hc decode walks (`hyper_range_decode`, `hyper_range_decode_ws_body`), one `rms_norm_zq8_f32` launch computes the FFN-input norm and emits both the f32 row and its q8_1 quantization, removing one `quantize_q8_1` launch per layer per token. Bit-identical to `rms_norm` then `quantize_q8_1`. Verify rows and prefill are unwired. Only `1` arms it; read once per process. Gate: `crates/memra-engine/src/bin/q8_fuse_gate.rs`. Missing gate: a single-door interleaved A/B on an sm_100a pair, three boots per arm. | [GLM5-B200-DOORS.md](decisions/GLM5-B200-DOORS.md), [LANE.md](../research/b200-q8-fuse-20260902/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_q8_fuse) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC` | off | `1` is the one master flag for glm5_next speculative decoding: it arms the engine route (`generate_spec` on an hc trunk with a draft source runs the T-parallel loop) and the serving route (`glm5_spec_capable`). A round drafts K (native MTP, or DFlash2 under `MEMRA_GLM5_DFLASH`), verifies in one t=K+1 walk (K+1 <= 15), accepts greedy or rejection-sampled, and rolls back. Serving also needs a gated placement (one engine, or ppN at 2 or 3 stages), the `GLM5_SPEC` manifest class and a `glm5-spec.v1` receipt for a sealed bundle. K follows `MEMRA_SPEC_K` and `MEMRA_SPEC_GATE*`. Penalized (unless `MEMRA_SPEC_PENALTY=1`), constrained, vision and warm sessions serve plain. Rollback: unset. | [3way-decision-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/3way-decision-20260830/), [spec-battery-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/spec-battery-20260830/), [tparallel-verify-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/tparallel-verify-20260830/), [spec-routing-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/spec-routing-20260830/), [ppn-verify-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/ppn-verify-20260830/), [LANE.md](../research/spec-exclusions-20260902/LANE.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_spec) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_FULLCOVER` | off | `1` on top of `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_PREFIX=1` admits the glm5 spec route on a full-cover prefix hit, where no suffix is left to prime; without it such a hit serves plain for the whole generation. The restored state equals a cold session's: trunk cache restored, drafter KV rebuilt from the entry tail, no prime, anchor drawn from the entry's boundary logits. `dflash2_guard_candidates` refuses a request on an unfilled selector slot. Every `route=plain` admission line carries `reason=<token>` whatever the flag. Read per call. Rollback: unset. A flip needs gate 13 of `glm5_dflash_session_gpu.rs` green, an armed six-identical-prompts cell and the 8-turn cache twin. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_spec_fullcover) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_PREFIX` | off | `1` with `MEMRA_PREFIX_LATENT=1` (both required; the first read warns if latent is missing) arms both halves of glm5 spec x prefix cache. Capture: DFlash2-source spec sessions take a prompt-boundary capture and publish once the drafter KV covers the boundary, with the drafter's readable tail. Restore: a whole-entry hit with a suffix >= PRIME_MIN_T on a spec-capable request rebuilds the drafter KV from the tail and runs a restored spec session (suffix primed, fresh Philox, republished deeper). Full-cover hits are `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC_FULLCOVER`; shorter suffixes keep the plain hit; native-MTP restores refuse. Off: no capture, hits demote to plain. Rollback: unset. | [glm5-prefix-latent2-20260901](../research/glm5-prefix-latent2-20260901/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_spec_prefix) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_INDEXER_SPLIT_CHECK` | unset (off; diagnostic) | `1` also scores and selects the full replicated plane on each rank and compares every merged index byte, tail and padding included; a mismatch names layer, rank and element. Uses host readback and is excluded from timing. Same receipt namespace as `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_INDEXER_SPLIT_PRIME`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_tp_indexer_split_check) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_INDEXER_SPLIT_PRIME` | on (`0` restores the replicated prime) | Grouped-prime chunks (t>1) score disjoint pool ranges on the two TP ranks, exchange exact score bits and global ids, and merge the global top-k in the replicated selector's order (rank 0 owns the extra pool at odd counts). Decode, rows-exact verify and unsupported shapes stay replicated. Needs two devices on `MEMRA_TP_TRANSPORT=device-push` (`glm5_tp::peer_link_admits`; an sm_100a TP-2 load arms it by default), matching resident indexers and cache lengths, at least 2 pools and 1<=k<=2048; other transports refuse once (`[glm5-tp-indexer-split] refused`) and prime replicated. A timeout traps. `MEMRA_GLM5_TP_INDEXER_SPLIT` is not read. | [RESULTS.md](../research/glm5-tp-indexer-split-20260908/RESULTS.md), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_tp_indexer_split_prime) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_VERIFY_BATCH` | on (any value but `0`) | The glm5 verify walk's per-layer batched mixer arm inside `glm5_verify_range`: each KDA layer runs one t=K+1 `kda_core` call, each MLA layer with a DSA indexer one t=K+1 `mla_attn_cached_rows_exact` call (a no-indexer MLA layer stays per row), the lm head rides the tcols class, and the routed-expert FFN batches across rows on its eligible shape (sigmoid router, pre-clamp, uniform NVFP4-class banks, local slab, n_used<=8), else the sequential loop. Per-row byte identity is bit-gated. Runs only behind `MEMRA_GLM5_SPEC`; `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH` is not rewired. `0` restores the per-row walk, read per call. Boot prints `[glm5-spec] verify walk BATCHED per layer`. | [verify-batch-20260830](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/verify-batch-20260830/), [vrest-20260831](../research/glm53-flash-bringup-20260827/vrest-20260831/), [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_verify_batch) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_VROWS_T1_DEV` | `100a` builds: on; elsewhere off | Forces the T=1 device-table MoE arm (no host sel/w readback) outside a capture region; inside an open capture the arm is keyed on the region regardless. A serving flag on sm_100a. `1`/`0` override; `MEMRA_GLM5_GRAPH_NO_CAPTURE=1` with the decode graph door forces it on whatever the value. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_vrows_t1_dev) |
| `MEMRA_GLM5_W8` | unset (bf16) | Strict `=1`: q8_0 mirror of bf16-resident projections reaching `matvec_bf16_rows_into` (m 1..=32) or `matmul_rows_exact` (verify m 2..=32) with `in_f % 32 == 0` and `out_f >= 64`: the glm5_next KDA/MLA decode trunk and any other eligible bf16 weight. A new numeric class (int8 dp4a, per-32 scales; the `MEMRA_STEP_TP_W8` class), built at first decode use. The bf16 original stays resident, so it costs VRAM; `MEMRA_W8_TRACE=1` lists the mirrored set. `MEMRA_KDA_FUSED_PROJ`'s bf16 arm declines while it is on. Opt-in and model-signed; gate `glm5_w8_gate`. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_glm5_w8), [LANE.md](../research/b200-glm5-w8-20260902/LANE.md) |
| `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH=1` | on (batched mHC decode chunks) | Batched mHC (HyperConnections) decode: up to 15 sessions share one `decode_step_batch_hyper` tick; hc glue and MoE FFN batch at m=B, KDA/MLA mixers run per session on each row's cache, width-dependent reductions keep decode-exact seams, and PP-N has its own serial stage split (no dual waves). The cap is `PRIME_MIN_T - 1` = 15: at t=16 the shared-expert trio crosses onto the prefill matmul class and breaks per-row bit-identity. `MEMRA_DECODE_BATCH_CAP` narrows only. | `MEMRA_HYPER_BATCH_SOLO` | unset (off) | Strict `=1`: at B=1 the batched hc decode walk (`hyper_batch_range_decode`) delegates to the solo walk (`hyper_range_decode`). glm5 PP-N serving decodes through the batched walk, which has neither `MEMRA_HC_DECODE_WS` nor `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH`; delegation exposes both. Byte identity is the batch walk's gated contract (`glm5-hyper-batch-gate`); `cache.pos` is untouched. Off until an A/B and a soak with boot churn. Do not arm `MEMRA_GLM5_DECODE_GRAPH` on this path: it produced wrong tapes there. | [history](FLAGS-HISTORY.md#memra_hyper_batch_solo) |
| `MEMRA_HYPER_PRIME_V2` | off; decide-by: 2026-11-18 | `1` opens the mHC prime schedule door for glm5_next on PP-2 (host-side, any build).
